# Nail Mirror — Research Bible

_Compiled 24–25 Oct 2026 by 14 research agents (9 dimensions + 4 adversarial fact-checks + synthesis). Some sources were blocked during research — items marked unverifiable need a check before marketing use._

## Executive summary

WHAT IS HONESTLY ACHIEVABLE VS "100% ACCURATE"
No phone-camera nail try-on can look exactly like the real manicure every time. ModiFace and Perfect Corp can't either, and neither claims it. Three things cap accuracy:
1. Phone cameras auto-adjust white balance and exposure and add HDR or beauty processing. The same bottle photographs differently on two phones, and differently from one frame to the next.
2. Screens differ (Display-P3 vs sRGB, True Tone, brightness). A perfect render still looks different on the customer's own phone.
3. One photo has no depth. Nail curvature, chrome, holo, glitter and cat-eye can only be approximated.

What we can deliver and defend is a "calibrated preview". At a salon table, with a printed calibration mat (grey patches, ArUco markers, mm ruler) under a fixed high-CRI light, it matches a photo of the real manicure within a measured colour error. Every screen shows a "Preview" or "Calibrated" badge. We never market it as "100% accurate". Oman's consumer protection law makes that claim risky anyway.

HOW ACCURACY WILL BE MEASURED (not claimed)
- Paired-photo protocol: same device, same light, card in frame. Render the polish on bare nails, then a technician applies the real polish and we photograph it. Compare per-nail CIEDE2000 on the core of the nail, excluding highlights.
- Colour targets for crème and gel in calibrated table mode: median ΔE00 at or below 2.0, 95th percentile at or below 3.0, stretch goal 1.5.
- Edge targets: IoU at or above 0.92, boundary error at or below 0.3 mm.
- Fairness target: no Monk skin-tone group more than 0.5 ΔE00 worse than the median.
- Blind real-vs-render test with at least 30 staff and customers: pass if they can't tell render from real much better than chance (60% or less).
- Glitter, chrome, holo and cat-eye are judged on multi-angle video and perception tests, not mean colour.
- Skin tone: Monk scale within ±1 level for at least 90% of people with the card and 75% without, checked against a colorimeter on 60-100 volunteers.

THE BIGGEST GAP TODAY
There is no verified, commercially licensed nail-segmentation dataset. MediaPipe's own model card puts landmark error at roughly 5-10% of palm size, worst at the fingertips, which is about a nail's width. So the first release (landmarks plus the smart-fit nail outline already in the repo) will look convincing in colour and finish but imperfect at the cuticle and side walls. Reaching Sam's quality bar needs a custom per-finger segmentation model trained on 2,500-4,000 consented GCC hand photos covering all skin tones, henna, extensions, French tips and glitter. The app already has a slot for it (VITE_NAIL_SEGMENTER_URL) and switches over once the model is trained.

HOW WE WILL BUILD IT (per Sam's instruction: no protocol, no phases)
Everything that works for free today ships in one release. Anything that needs money, training data, a legal opinion or a partner's approval is built in but switched off with a feature flag. The discovery questions below do not block the build: the recommended option is the default and can be changed later in Settings.

REPO STATE
A lot of the app already exists: try-on studio, WebGL renderer, MediaPipe tracker, skin analysis, recommender, and a DataProvider that writes createdBy/updatedBy audit stamps under salons/{SALON_ID}. Seed data, CSV/PDF/QR libraries and bilingual i18n are in place too.
But the build is currently broken:
- src/pages is empty, yet App.jsx imports Landing, CatalogPage, Login and NotFound from it.
- Eight admin modules don't exist: Requests, Looks, Inventory, Services, Reports, Staff, Settings, Audit.
- There is no firebase.json, firestore.rules or firestore.indexes.json.
These must be fixed first.

FREE-TIER VERDICT
- Free: React + Vite + Tailwind, Firebase Spark (Firestore, Auth with email/Google/anonymous, Hosting), on-device MediaPipe, and a custom WebGL2 renderer.
- Not free, or not available on Spark: Cloud Storage (Blaze only), Cloud Functions, phone/SMS sign-in, Firestore TTL deletes, the WhatsApp Business API, payment gateways, and calibration hardware (printed mat, optional ColorChecker, colorimeter to measure bottles, high-CRI lamp).
- Hidden risk: Firebase Hosting allows 360 MB/day on Spark (not verified this run). A first visit downloads 10-20 MB of AI files, so only about 20-35 new devices a day fit. The fix is to serve the model and runtime from Google's and jsDelivr's free CDNs.

OTHER CAVEATS
- Many research claims could not be live-verified because the web was blocked during research: PDPL details, the Monk Skin Tone licence, Pantone hexes, Firebase limits and brand distributors in Oman. Every brand hex is an approximation from swatch photos.
- Oman National Day is 20 November (moved by Royal Decree 15/2025), less than 4 weeks away, so a red/white/green collection should be seeded now.

## Key decisions

- **Delivery approach** — Build everything that works for free in a single release. Wrap every item that needs money, data, a legal opinion or partner approval in a feature flag that is off by default (stored in Firestore salon settings with an env fallback). Treat the discovery questions as defaults, not gates.
  - _Why:_ Sam said to ignore phases and make the app fully ready now, with later items enabled later. Flags keep the codebase complete while staying honest about what is switched off.
- **Fix the broken build first** — Create the missing src/pages/{Landing,CatalogPage,Login,NotFound}.jsx and src/admin/{Requests,Looks,Inventory,Services,Reports,Staff,Settings,Audit}.jsx. Add firebase.json (hosting headers, SPA rewrite, cache rules), firestore.rules, firestore.indexes.json and .firebaserc.
  - _Why:_ App.jsx imports modules that don't exist, so `vite build` fails. Without security rules, the audit trail and multi-role access aren't enforced on the server.
- **Photo storage** — Store no customer photos by default. Frames live only in memory, WebGL textures and canvases, and are wiped on exit or 'New customer'. Firestore stores 'look recipes' only (polish IDs, finish, shape, length, per-finger map, staff, date). Optional opt-in feature (flag): a hand-only crop at most 640 px, WebP at about q0.7 (25-70 KB), saved as a Firestore Bytes field in its own document, exempt from indexing, with expiresAt. Delete expired thumbnails with an app-run purge on staff login. Firestore TTL deletes are NOT free on Spark. Sharing goes through the Web Share API and download, both generated on the device.
  - _Why:_ Cloud Storage requires the Blaze plan. Hand images tied to a person are a grey zone under Oman's PDPL (possible biometric category, possible Ministry permit). Women-only salons care a lot about privacy. Firestore Bytes thumbnails fit the free 1 GiB (about 15-30k images).
- **Nail segmentation model and licence path** — v1 uses the existing landmark plus smart-fit nail geometry, labelled 'Preview'. Next model: per-finger rotated crops (128-160 px) fed to a U-Net or LRASPP with a MobileNetV3 or EfficientNet encoder, 3 classes plus base/tip keypoints. Train with PyTorch, torchvision, segmentation_models.pytorch and timm, then export with litert-torch to .tflite with MediaPipe ImageSegmenter metadata, loaded through VITE_NAIL_SEGMENTER_URL. Label with Label Studio or CVAT plus SAM 2.1 (Apache-2.0). Forbidden licences: Ultralytics YOLO (AGPL, code and weights), SegFormer/MiT (NVIDIA non-commercial, which also covers smp's mix_transformer encoder CODE), AlbumentationsX (AGPL), FastSAM, YOLOv10, Mixbox, Afifi WB models, Fitzpatrick17k. Apple's MobileOne licence is restrictive.
  - _Why:_ Landmark error is about a nail's width, so pixel masks are needed for Sam's quality bar. Cropping per finger gives instance IDs without needing an instance-segmentation head, keeps the model at 1.5 MB or less, and reuses the MediaPipe runtime that is already loaded. Every dependency stays permissively licensed so Sam owns 100% of the code.
- **Hand tracking version** — Keep @mediapipe/tasks-vision 0.10.35 (Apache-2.0, already pinned and working) with the full float16 hand_landmarker.task, runningMode VIDEO, GPU delegate with CPU fallback, and One-Euro smoothing. Upgrade to 1.x (1.1.1 was released 2026-10-14) only after it is at least two weeks old and passes the device lab, because 1.x changes the API.
  - _Why:_ Stability for this release. Also, the 1.x README says MediaPipe Tasks send performance and usage metrics to Google, so the privacy notice must say that and must not claim 'nothing leaves the device'.
- **Rendering technology** — Raw WebGL2 (the existing NailRenderer) with a linear-light workflow on RGBA16F targets. Shading: transfer the real nail's low-frequency shading, reuse its real highlights, Kubelka-Munk coverage by coat count, GGX plus a Kelemen clearcoat, finish branches (crème, gel, matte, jelly, milky, shimmer, pearl, metallic, chrome, holo, glitter, cat-eye/velvet, duochrome, French, ombré), and flakes anchored in nail UV coordinates. Use Display-P3 output for neons where supported. Port GLSL from three.js and Filament (MIT); don't ship three.js. Clamp clearcoat roughness to at least 0.045 (highp) or 0.089 (mediump).
  - _Why:_ Smallest bundle, works on Safari iOS 15+ and Chrome Android, and gives full control over the multi-pass relighting that makes the render look real. WebGPU isn't needed for 2.5D work.
- **Colour accuracy and calibration** — Ship a free printable calibration mat (PDF): ArUco markers (js-aruco2, MIT; don't use jcmellado/js-aruco because it includes an LGPL part), a 6-step neutral grey ramp, 8-12 skin and polish patches, and a mm ruler. Fit a root-polynomial camera-to-XYZ matrix and render the polish as this camera would see it. Only show the 'Calibrated' badge when the residual is ΔE00 2.5 or less. Android: set exposureCompensation while still in 'continuous' mode, then switch to manual (manual turns AE off and freezes exposure time), and calibrate AFTER locking. iOS 17.4+: lock whiteBalanceMode 'manual' and normalise exposure in software every frame (iOS can't lock exposure). Use ImageCapture.takePhoto for HD stills (Chrome Android, Safari 18.4+) and re-calibrate on the still.
  - _Why:_ Calibration matters more for accuracy than the shader does. The verified camera-control behaviour corrects the earlier assumption that 'AE lock + negative EV' works.
- **Catalogue colour data** — Store each shade as calibrated CIELAB (D65, lab65 in culori), opacity per coat (K/S, S·X from one coat over black and over white), finish parameters, and the real bottle photo. Treat the 241-shade research catalogue only as seed data marked 'approximate hex'. Each salon ticks the bottles it actually owns and measures them on the card.
  - _Why:_ The research hexes are visual estimates, not measurements, and polish isn't a flat colour. Sheer and milky shades need opacity data to render correctly.
- **Recommendation engine type** — A deterministic, explainable scoring engine: weighted fit on skin depth, undertone and confidence; nail aspect ratio, nail-to-finger ratio, cuticle shape and finger proportions (weights 0.30/0.25/0.15/0.15/0.10/0.05 as starting values); occasion and persona; trend prior; IN-STOCK filter. Out-of-stock shades get the nearest in-stock substitute by ΔE2000. Pairwise comparisons ('which looks better on you?') feed a Bradley-Terry or Elo re-rank. Every result shows a bilingual reason, and the user can override.
  - _Why:_ Free, offline, auditable and consistent. The nail-colour rules are industry consensus, not validated science, so they should rank a shortlist and the try-on itself should decide.
- **Skin-tone method** — Sample a polygon on the back of the hand defined by landmarks (MCP line toward the wrist, inset). Reject highlights, shadows, veins, hair, rings and henna. Work in white-balanced CIELAB, port Sony's Apache-2.0 cluster method, and compute ITA for depth and hue angle h_ab for undertone. Map to the 10 Monk orbs through a calibration table fitted on local volunteers, not raw nearest-ΔE. Show the 3 nearest orbs and let the customer or staff confirm. Mark undertone 'uncertain' when chroma is below about 8-10. Never use Fitzpatrick, never use ethnicity labels, and measure before the soak or massage.
  - _Why:_ Monk swatches are display colours, not real skin measurements. A -0.5 EV exposure error shifts ITA by a whole category. Olive undertone is the least reliable reading and the most common in the GCC.
- **Age handling** — Never infer age from hands or faces. Offer an optional self-chosen style persona (Classic, Minimal, Soft Glam, Trendy, Bold) and occasion. Add opt-in hand-concern toggles (soften veins, sun spots, ridges, make fingers look longer). Add a guardian-present toggle for under-18s. Optionally let the customer choose an age bracket herself.
  - _Why:_ Inferring age from hands is unreliable, can offend customers, and adds sensitive-data risk under the PDPL. Occasion predicts the right colour better than age does.
- **LLM or not** — No LLM in this release. Use curated bilingual templates attached to reason codes. A Gemini (Firebase AI Logic) text-only phrasing layer is behind a flag that is off by default; it must never receive images or personal data.
  - _Why:_ Gemini's free tier may use the content sent to it, and its quotas change. On-device models are multi-GB and weak in Arabic. Chrome's built-in AI is desktop-only. A rules engine is free, instant and private.
- **Single salon vs multi-tenant** — One salon per deployment now (VITE_SALON_ID). Keep the existing salons/{salonId}/... data model so SaaS becomes a configuration switch later. Roles: owner, manager, tech, reception.
  - _Why:_ This already matches the repo, keeps Spark quotas within one salon's needs, and leaves the SaaS business model open without rewriting anything.
- **PWA vs native** — An installable PWA (vite-plugin-pwa), with no native app. Use a rear camera via getUserMedia with playsinline, plus a photo fallback (<input type=file capture=environment>). Detect in-app browsers (Instagram, Facebook, Snapchat, TikTok) and send users out: intent:// on Android, an 'Open in Safari' overlay on iOS. Add a tablet kiosk mode (Wake Lock, iPad Guided Access, 90 s idle reset, memory-only Firestore cache).
  - _Why:_ Free, one codebase, QR entry with no install. Native apps cost store fees and review time and gain little on top of WebGL2 and MediaPipe.
- **Hosting and AI asset delivery** — Firebase Hosting (Spark) for the app shell. Load the hand model from storage.googleapis.com/mediapipe-models/hand_landmarker/hand_landmarker/float16/1/hand_landmarker.task (verified: CORS *, free) and the WASM from cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/@mediapipe/tasks-vision@0.10.35/wasm, falling back to the self-hosted copies. The service worker caches both with CacheFirst. Disclose these CDNs in the privacy notice. Cloudflare Pages is the zero-cap fallback. Don't enable COOP/COEP.
  - _Why:_ A first visit is 10-20 MB against a possible 360 MB/day Spark cap. COOP would break signInWithPopup, embeds and possibly reCAPTCHA, and MediaPipe 0.10.35 is single-threaded so it gains nothing from it.
- **Database, region and offline** — Firestore on Spark in me-central1 (Doha) with persistentLocalCache (multi-tab). On shared tablets use memoryLocalCache or clear persistence on logout. Customers read the catalogue as ONE denormalised public document (the existing public/site projection), not per-shade listeners. Paginate admin listeners. Enforce App Check (reCAPTCHA, free up to 10k assessments a month).
  - _Why:_ There is no Google Cloud region in Oman, so any region is a cross-border transfer and must be disclosed. If the 50k reads/day quota runs out on Spark, the app stops for the rest of the day, so keeping customer reads to 1-2 documents per visit is critical.
- **Auth and audit trail** — Staff sign in with email/password or Google. Customers need no login: give them anonymous auth so every booking request has a uid for the audit trail. No phone/SMS sign-in (Oman is about $0.11 per SMS and SMS-pumping fraud is a risk). Security rules enforce updatedBy == request.auth.uid and updatedAt == request.time on every write. The audit collection is append-only.
  - _Why:_ Sam wants who-and-when on every record. Enforcing it in the rules makes it tamper-resistant, and the DataProvider already stamps these fields.
- **Booking and payments** — Book from the saved look, then either open wa.me click-to-chat with a prefilled bilingual message, look code and link, or create a Firestore request in the staff inbox. Offer a .ics calendar export. Track deposits by hand (staff tick 'received'). A payment gateway (Thawani/OmanNet) and an optional 'Book on Fresha' link are behind flags.
  - _Why:_ Bookings in Oman already run through WhatsApp and Instagram DMs. The WhatsApp API and payment gateways cost money.
- **Breathable / halal / wudu labelling** — Show two separate badges: 'Breathable / water-permeable (manufacturer claim)' and 'Halal-certified ingredients (certifier, scope, date checked)'. Add a 'Wudu-friendly options' filter (henna, peel-off or removable, and products with breathable claims) with a neutral 'consult a trusted scholar' note. Never write 'wudu-valid'.
  - _Why:_ Strong demand in Oman, but the only independent study found no strong evidence of water permeation, and a post attributed to Oman's Grand Mufti treats polish as a barrier.
- **Locale and design system** — Use the 'Glaze Atelier' tokens: porcelain/cocoa neutrals, glaze-blue only for 'live', and a neutral surround (#ECECEA) behind every swatch and comparison. Fonts: IBM Plex Sans with IBM Plex Sans Arabic for the UI and Fraunces with Noto Naskh Arabic for display, self-hosted via Fontsource (OFL). Set dir on <html>, use logical CSS properties, and format with Intl using an explicit numbering system (one ١٢٣/123 setting, Western digits by default). OMR with 3 decimals, weeks start on Saturday, 12-hour clock. Fix the current contrast failures: focus ring #C8A97E on porcelain is 2.02:1, glass-dark at 0.55 alpha, chips at 40 px.
  - _Why:_ CLDR defaults ar-OM to Arabic-Indic digits but 'ar' to Western, so the digit system must be set explicitly. A coloured surround shifts how colours are perceived, which would directly hurt accuracy.
- **Analytics and tracking** — No Google Analytics and no ad pixels on the try-on screens. Use only anonymous aggregate counters in Firestore (tries, favourites, handoffs, bookings by shade and day) written in batches to protect the write quota.
  - _Why:_ Analytics sends identifiers to the US, and pixels would add PDPL consent burden, for little gain at one salon.

## Honest limits

- '100% accurate' is not possible with any phone camera. The realistic goal is a calibrated preview with measured error bounds: ΔE00 median at or below 2 in table mode. Without the calibration card, colours are approximate and labelled that way.
- Customers' own screens can't be calibrated. The same render looks different on a different phone, and True Tone or Night Shift shifts it further.
- Version 1 nail edges use landmarks plus smart-fit geometry. MediaPipe fingertip error is about a nail's width, so cuticle and side-wall edges will sometimes be visibly off until the custom segmentation model is trained. That needs a consented dataset that doesn't exist yet: 2,500-4,000 images and 100-200 annotator-hours.
- No verified, commercially licensed public nail-segmentation dataset exists. Kaggle and Roboflow sets have unknown licences, and their photos were often scraped without consent.
- MediaPipe lists henna, jewellery, tattoos, gloves and held objects as out of scope. With henna, which is common in Oman, tracking and skin readings get less reliable. The app warns but still runs the try-on.
- Undertone read from a camera is only moderately reliable even after calibration, and olive is the least reliable. Very deep (Monk 9-10) and very fair low-chroma skin give noisy hue readings, so the customer or staff confirms.
- Nail curvature can't be measured from one image; it is assumed. Chrome, holo, glitter, cat-eye and duochrome are convincing approximations, not physics.
- Neon and fluorescent polishes lie outside what the screen can show (outside sRGB). Even Display-P3 can't reproduce the glow. Label them 'brighter in person'.
- Sheer or jelly shades over existing dark polish, nail art or heavy henna stain have to guess at the hidden nail, so the result is unpredictable.
- iOS web apps can't lock exposure, set colour temperature or set focus. Only white balance, torch and zoom (plus ImageCapture from 18.4) are available, so iPhone colour stability depends on software correction against the card.
- Instagram, Facebook, Snapchat and TikTok in-app browsers often block the live camera. Users must open the system browser or use the photo upload.
- On Spark, if Firestore or Hosting quotas run out, the app stops until about 11:00-12:00 Muscat time. Nothing gets billed; it is a hard outage.
- Every real-product hex in the research catalogue is a visual estimate from swatch photos. Brand-exact colours need the salon to measure its own bottles.
- Age is deliberately not estimated. The owner's request is answered with an optional, self-chosen style persona.
- Rules matching skin tone to nail colour are industry consensus, not peer-reviewed science. Recommendations are a ranked shortlist with reasons, not a verdict.
- jsPDF doesn't handle Arabic shaping or bidi well by default. Bilingual PDFs need an embedded Arabic font and testing, or Arabic sections rendered as images.
- Oman PDPL specifics (Ministry permits for sensitive data, 72-hour breach notice, fine bands) could not be checked against the official text and need an Omani lawyer.
- GCC distribution of CND, DND, Kiara Sky, Bio Sculpture, The GelBottle, Semilac and Zoya is unconfirmed. Brand catalogues can't be promised as 'available in Oman' until distributors confirm.

## Risk register

| Impact | Risk | Mitigation |
|---|---|---|
| high | Build currently fails: App.jsx imports 4 pages and 8 admin modules that don't exist, and there is no firebase.json, firestore.rules or indexes. | Create the missing modules and Firebase config first, add a CI 'npm run build' check, then test the deploy in demo mode. |
| high | Firebase Hosting Spark bandwidth (360 MB/day, unverified) is used up by 10-20 MB first-visit AI downloads, so the site is capped after about 20-35 new devices. | Serve the model from storage.googleapis.com (CORS verified) and the WASM from jsDelivr pinned 0.10.35, keep self-hosted copies as fallback, cache with the service worker, monitor usage; Cloudflare Pages as fallback host. |
| high | Firestore quota outage on Spark (50k reads / 20k writes a day), for example from per-shade listeners on public visits or scripted abuse. | Publish the catalogue as one public document, paginate admin listeners, batch analytics counters, enforce App Check, tight security rules, and an in-app quota warning in the dashboard. |
| high | The colour mismatch between preview and the real nail damages trust, which is Sam's core worry. | Calibration mat plus 'Calibrated'/'Preview' badges, measured bottle Lab and opacity, real bottle photo next to every render, the paired-photo ΔE00 validation protocol before launch, and a 'verify with swatch' step. |
| high | Nail edges misaligned without a trained segmentation model (landmark error near fingertips). | Ship v1 as 'Preview' with a manual fine-tune nudge; start consented data collection right away; train a crop U-Net and switch it on via VITE_NAIL_SEGMENTER_URL once IoU is at least 0.90. |
| high | Oman PDPL non-compliance: hand photos as possible biometric data, skin tone as an ethnicity proxy, nail-health remarks as health data, cross-border transfer to Doha/US, MediaPipe metrics sent to Google. | On-device processing, no storage by default, separate opt-in consents, cosmetic-only wording, no age or ethnicity inference, privacy notice that discloses Google, the CDNs and regions, and Omani counsel review before enabling the thumbnail flag. |
| high | Licence contamination (AGPL Ultralytics, NVIDIA MiT encoder code in smp, AlbumentationsX, Mixbox, LGPL js-aruco, non-commercial datasets). | Keep a licence ledger in the repo, a CI check on dependency licences, and an approved-list of encoders and libraries; never bundle stone (GPL) code. |
| medium | Instagram, Snapchat and TikTok in-app browsers block the camera; most traffic comes from those apps. | User-agent and capability detection, intent:// bounce on Android, 'Open in Safari' overlay on iOS, photo-upload fallback, and QR cards that say 'scan with your phone camera'. |
| medium | iOS camera behaviour: re-prompts in home-screen apps, tracks stopping when backgrounded, macro/focus blur on close-ups, WebGL context loss on older iPhones. | Keep a single SPA camera view, re-acquire on visibilitychange, distance coaching at 20-25 cm, handle webglcontextlost, quality tiers from a startup benchmark. |
| medium | Android exposure 'lock' actually turns auto-exposure off, with a brightness jump and ignored exposure compensation. | Apply EV in continuous mode, then lock; calibrate only after locking; software gain correction every frame against the card or skin. |
| medium | Mid-range Android thermal throttling and low FPS. | Adaptive tiers (segmentation every second frame with mask warping, half-resolution environment map), auto-freeze best frame, cap at 30 fps, device lab of about 10 GCC-common phones. |
| medium | Spark data retention: Firestore TTL isn't free, so expired thumbnails and consent records pile up. | App-run purge job on staff login (query expiresAt < now, batched deletes within the 20k/day quota). |
| medium | Religious-claim backlash over 'halal' or 'breathable' polish. | Show only the manufacturer's claim, certifier, scope and date; never 'wudu-valid'; neutral 'consult a scholar' note. |
| medium | Shared salon tablet leaks the previous customer's data (Firestore IndexedDB cache, canvases). | Kiosk mode uses memoryLocalCache, 'New customer' wipe, 90 s idle reset, terminate() and clearIndexedDbPersistence() on staff logout. |
| medium | Patents held by L'Oréal/ModiFace or Perfect Corp on nail segmentation and rendering. | Freedom-to-operate search including GCC patent office coverage before commercial SaaS sale; at the own-salon stage the exposure is lower. |
| medium | Arabic copy and digit conventions feel foreign to Omani customers. | Gulf-dialect copy review by a native speaker, one global ١٢٣/123 setting, feminine imperative only in customer copy, test with Omani users. |
| medium | jsPDF Arabic rendering breaks in bilingual reports. | Embed Amiri or Noto Naskh TTF, use the shaping/R2L path, test printed output; fallback renders Arabic blocks to canvas images. |
| low | Unverified research facts (Pantone hexes, Monk Skin Tone licence, Firebase limits, brand availability in Oman) end up in marketing. | Keep trend palettes and labels editable by the admin; a verification pass before launch; credit Monk Skin Tone only after the licence is confirmed. |
| low | Free GPU quotas (Colab/Kaggle) disappear during training. | A small crop model trains in under an hour on one T4; a local GPU is an option and better for privacy. |
| low | Campaign dates wrong (National Day, Eid ±1 day by moon sighting). | National Day is 20 Nov (RD 15/2025); Hijri-aware calendar with admin override; announce Eid dates after official sighting. |

## 10x features

- **Calibrated Table Mode: tablet on a stand above a printed mat (ArUco markers, grey ramp, mm ruler), auto white balance and colour matrix, nail length in mm, and a 'Calibrated' badge only when the residual ΔE00 is 2.5 or less** — KPI: Median ΔE00 render vs real manicure at or below 2.0 (p95 at or below 3.0); at least 70% of consultations run calibrated; staff-reported screen-to-bottle mismatches trending to 0 a month
- **Shelf Sync: only the bottles physically in stock today, each scanned by barcode and measured on the card; sold-out shades turn into the nearest in-stock match by ΔE2000** — KPI: 0 recommended shades that aren't in stock; at least 90% of the catalogue with a real photo plus calibrated Lab; lost-sale recovery rate on sold-out requests
- **Best-frame auto-freeze plus 2-up/4-up grid comparison on the same frozen hand, with elimination, split slider and hold-to-peek at the bare nail** — KPI: Median time from first favourite to decision at or below 90 s; consultation at or below 4 min; at least 40% of sessions with 2+ favourites use compare
- **Upsell layers with live OMR price and time (chrome, cat-eye/velvet that moves with tilt, French/micro-French, ombré/aura, extensions with length in mm)** — KPI: Add-on attach rate per technician; average ticket up 10-25% (hypothesis to test); gel/BIAB upgrade rate
- **QR session bridge: a walk-in builds a shortlist on her own phone, and a 4-digit code moves it to the technician's tablet** — KPI: Time to first painted nail at or below 10 s on 4G (5 s when cached); handoff in at least 40% of sessions; 10 or more try-ons per session
- **Light switcher: re-render the frozen look under salon LED, Muscat daylight, or a warm wedding hall/majlis (about 2700 K) via Bradford adaptation** — KPI: 'Looked different at the event' complaints going down; bridal/occasion booking conversion compared with baseline
- **Henna-aware analysis and rendering: mask henna out of skin sampling, show sheer polish correctly over a stained nail, suggest shades that suit the henna, never refuse service** — KPI: Analysis completion on henna hands at least 80%; manual skin-tone correction rate below 20%
- **Nail Passport: per-client history of looks, bottles, shape, technician and allergy flags, with 'Repeat' or 'Seasonal twist', plus a maintenance queue that sends WhatsApp rebook nudges at 14-21 days** — KPI: Rebook rate within 6 weeks; time to rebook at or below 30 s; at least 90% of services logged with a consultation
- **'Ask my circle': a no-login, 48-hour WhatsApp vote link on the 4-up grid, so family and friends pick the winner** — KPI: Vote response rate at least 50%; bookings attributed to share links by look code; share rate at least 15% of sessions
- **Explainable bilingual AI match: 10 Monk orbs with confidence, undertone, nail shape and proportions, each with a one-line Arabic/English reason and 'Not right? Adjust'** — KPI: AI flow completion at least 70%; final look chosen from AI picks at least 35%; manual correction rate below 20%
- **Occasion collections that switch on by date (Hijri-aware Ramadan/Eid, Oman National Day 20 Nov red/white/green, wedding season, graduation), using only in-stock bottles** — KPI: Eid slots pre-booked 1-2 weeks ahead; collection try-to-booking conversion compared with non-collection shades
- **Wudu-aware filter (henna, peel-off or removable, breathable products shown as the maker's claim) plus a privacy guard that captures hands only, processes on the device and resets the kiosk** — KPI: Filter usage share during Ramadan; camera permission grant at least 85%; 0 complaints about religious claims or photo privacy

## Later scope

- Built in now but switched OFF by a flag, or waiting on external input:
- Custom nail-segmentation model (VITE_NAIL_SEGMENTER_URL). Turns on after consented GCC data is collected and labelled and the model hits nail IoU 0.90 or more, Boundary F1 0.85 or more, and a subgroup gap of 3 IoU points or less.
- Opt-in lookbook and before/after thumbnails stored in Firestore Bytes. Turns on after Omani counsel reviews the PDPL position.
- Cloud Storage, Cloud Functions, higher Hosting bandwidth: only if Sam moves to the Blaze plan, with a budget alert and a billing kill-switch.
- Payment gateway for deposits (Thawani, OmanNet or a bank gateway). Has transaction fees.
- Gemini text-only phrasing layer through Firebase AI Logic. Never images.
- Branded Snapchat Lens, which needs Snap approval.
- Group and bridal board for the bride and her party.
- 'Help me decide' tournament plus a remote vote with a live tally, if not already enabled at launch.
- Outfit, abaya and accessory colour match via a camera sampler.
- Lighting presets beyond the three built-in ones, and multi-angle LUTs measured per product for duochrome and metallic shades.
- Offline home-service kit mode with a sync queue (the PWA basics ship now; the kit builder comes later).
- Multi-salon SaaS: tenant onboarding, OMR billing tiers, white-label for chains, distributor sponsorship slots with aggregated anonymous insights.
- Upgrade to MediaPipe tasks-vision 1.x after it passes the device lab, and evaluate WebGPU or LiteRT.js.
- Kiosk hardware bundle (tablet, stand, ring light, printed mats) and pre-sized press-on retail kits.
- Brand-exact catalogue imports once Oman distributors (Nazih, Madi, Apparel Group, Sawaya) confirm availability and data.

## Discovery questions (defaults applied, change any time)

- **1. How do we earn the 'as accurate as possible' result in the salon? Will each consultation station use a printed calibration mat and a fixed daylight lamp?** — default: _Yes: Table Mode at every station with a printed mat and a CRI 90+ 5000-6500 K lamp_ · options: Yes: Table Mode at every station with a printed mat and a CRI 90+ 5000-6500 K lamp, Mat only, used for 'accurate mode' when the customer wants it, No calibration: preview badge only
- **2. Where does the shade catalogue come from?** — default: _Tick the bottles you own from the 241-shade seed catalogue, then photograph each swatch stick on the card_ · options: Tick the bottles you own from the 241-shade seed catalogue, then photograph each swatch stick on the card, Enter only your own bottles from scratch (barcode scan plus swatch photo), Wait for brand-exact data from distributors
- **3. Should the app keep any customer hand photos?** — default: _Store nothing; customers save or share on their own phone (look recipes only in the database)_ · options: Store nothing; customers save or share on their own phone (look recipes only in the database), Opt-in small thumbnails in Firestore, auto-deleted after 90 days, after a lawyer reviews it, Full photos in Cloud Storage (needs the paid Blaze plan)
- **4. Is this for one salon, several of your own branches, or a product to sell to other salons?** — default: _One salon now, built so more can be added later_ · options: One salon now, built so more can be added later, Several of my own branches from day one, SaaS for other Muscat salons from day one
- **5. How should customers book?** — default: _WhatsApp click-to-chat with the look attached, plus a request inbox in the admin_ · options: WhatsApp click-to-chat with the look attached, plus a request inbox in the admin, Link out to an existing Fresha or other booking page, Full in-app calendar with online deposits (gateway fees)
- **6. Should customers have to log in?** — default: _No login: QR session, phone number asked only when booking (anonymous ID for the audit trail)_ · options: No login: QR session, phone number asked only when booking (anonymous ID for the audit trail), Optional account with email or Google to keep a lookbook across devices, Required login with phone/SMS
- **7. You asked about age. How should the AI handle it?** — default: _No age at all; optional style persona and occasion instead_ · options: No age at all; optional style persona and occasion instead, Optional age bracket the customer selects herself, Automatic age estimate from the hand (not recommended)
- **8. May the salon collect consented hand photos to train our own nail-edge model?** — default: _Yes, with a bilingual consent form (guardian consent for minors), about 300+ customers and staff_ · options: Yes, with a bilingual consent form (guardian consent for minors), about 300+ customers and staff, Staff and volunteers only, smaller dataset, No: stay with landmark-based edges
- **9. Where should the website and AI files be hosted?** — default: _Firebase Hosting for the app, AI files from Google's and jsDelivr's free CDNs (disclosed in the privacy notice)_ · options: Firebase Hosting for the app, AI files from Google's and jsDelivr's free CDNs (disclosed in the privacy notice), Everything self-hosted on Firebase (risk of hitting the daily cap), Move static hosting to Cloudflare Pages (no bandwidth cap), keep Firebase for data and login
- **10. How should breathable or 'halal' polishes be shown?** — default: _Separate badges: 'Breathable (manufacturer claim)' and 'Halal ingredients (certifier, scope, date)', plus a wudu-friendly filter and a neutral 'consult a scholar' note_ · options: Separate badges: 'Breathable (manufacturer claim)' and 'Halal ingredients (certifier, scope, date)', plus a wudu-friendly filter and a neutral 'consult a scholar' note, Show only products whose certificate copy is on file, Don't show these labels at all
- **11. Which digits should Arabic screens use for prices, times and codes?** — default: _Western digits (123) by default with a ١٢٣ toggle_ · options: Western digits (123) by default with a ١٢٣ toggle, Arabic-Indic (١٢٣) by default with a 123 toggle, Follow each phone's own setting
- **12. Will you get an Omani lawyer to review the privacy design before any optional photo storage is switched on?** — default: _Yes, before switching on thumbnail storage or launching SaaS_ · options: Yes, before switching on thumbnail storage or launching SaaS, Later, once the business grows, No legal review

## Fact-check corrections

### skin
- [confirmed] The 10 Monk Skin Tone hex codes are #f6ede4, #f3e7db, #f7ead0, #eadaba, #d7bd96, #a07e56, #825c43, #604134, #3a312a and #292420. (skin-tone-classifier 1.2.6 wheel, file stone/image.py, DEFAULT_TONE_PALETTE['monk'], which cites 'Monk, Ellis. Monk Skin Tone Scale, 2019. https://skintone.google': https://files.pythonhosted.org/packages/d8/e4/a21bd06c4086a0b9b21d584f79fe66bf6e2da882883b386769274521902f/skin_tone_classifier-1.2.6-py3-none-any.whl. I re-extracted this file myself. skintone.google itself failed DNS, so the check rests on code that quotes Google, not on Google's own page.)
- [confirmed] Computed CIELAB values for the MST swatches (sRGB, D65), for example MST1 L*94.2 / ITA 83°, MST6 L*55.1 / ITA 11°, MST10 L*14.6 / ITA -84°, with uneven L* steps (5→6 is about 22.8). (My own recomputation using IEC 61966-2-1 sRGB, the stated sRGB→XYZ matrix and the CIE 1976 L*a*b* formulas (D65 white 0.95047 / 1.0 / 1.08883). All 10 rows match to 0.1 (for example MST5 L77.9 a3.5 b23.1 h81.5° ITA50; MST8 L30.7 h48.8° ITA-55).)
- [unverifiable] The Monk Skin Tone Scale is licensed CC BY 4.0, with credit to 'Monk Skin Tone Scale, Dr. Ellis Monk / Google'. (skintone.google failed DNS from this sandbox, and WebSearch was unavailable because the shared 200-call budget was used up. No Google-owned GitHub repo states the MST license; google-research-datasets/scin uses eMST labels under its own 'SCIN Data Use License'. A human must check the site footer and terms before shipping the orbs or the name.)
- [corrected] MediaPipe (@mediapipe/tasks-vision Hand Landmarker) is Apache-2.0 and runs fully on-device, so hand images never leave the phone, which supports privacy by design and PDPL compliance. → **The license is confirmed: the npm package is Apache-2.0 (latest 1.1.1, published 2026-10-14), the repo LICENSE is Apache-2.0, and the Hand Tracking model card says 'LICENSED UNDER Apache License, Version 2.0'. The privacy claim needs a qualifier. The package README Privacy Notice (last modified June 5, 2026) says input images are processed on-device and not sent to Google, BUT also says 'MediaPipe Tasks APIs send metrics about the performance and utilization of the APIs in your app to Google' and 'You are responsible for obtaining informed consent from your app users about Google's processing of MediaPipe metrics data as required by applicable law.' The app's privacy notice and consent flow must disclose this. I could not verify whether the metrics can be turned off. The model card also lists the primary intended use as 'research and entertainment'; that is not a license restriction.** (https://registry.npmjs.org/@mediapipe/tasks-vision (tarball tasks-vision-1.1.1.tgz, package/README.md, section 'Privacy Notice'); https://raw.githubusercontent.com/google-ai-edge/mediapipe/master/LICENSE; https://storage.googleapis.com/mediapipe-assets/Model%20Card%20Hand%20Tracking%20(Lite_Full)%20with%20Fairness%20Oct%202021.pdf)
- [confirmed] The legacy MediaPipe Hands solution was replaced by Hand Landmarker on 1 Mar 2023, and its handedness output assumes a mirrored (selfie) image, so it must be flipped for the rear camera. (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/google-ai-edge/mediapipe/master/docs/solutions/hands.md (line 23: 'As of March 1, 2023, this solution was upgraded...'; lines 224-226: 'handedness is determined assuming the input image is mirrored... please swap the handedness output'). The Tasks web result type exposes `handedness` and the deprecated `handednesses`: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/google-ai-edge/mediapipe/master/mediapipe/tasks/web/vision/hand_landmarker/hand_landmarker_result.d.ts)
- [confirmed] Afifi's sRGB white-balance models (WB_sRGB, CVPR 2019, and mixedillWB, WACV 2022) are research-only and cannot be used commercially. (WB_sRGB README: 'This software and the dataset are provided for research purposes only. A license must be obtained for any commercial application.' Its LICENSE.md is also CC BY-NC-SA 4.0 (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/mahmoudnafifi/WB_sRGB/master/LICENSE.md). mixedillWB README: 'research purposes only and CANNOT be used for commercial purposes' (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/mahmoudnafifi/mixedillWB/main/README.md).)
- [confirmed] The Fitzpatrick17k dataset is CC BY-NC-SA 3.0, so it cannot be used commercially. (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/mattgroh/fitzpatrick17k/main/README.md, Licensing section: CC BY-NC-SA 3.0 Unported. The images come from Atlas Dermatologico and DermaAmin, which carry their own third-party rights.)
- [confirmed] The skin-tone-classifier (stone) code is GPL-3.0, so it must not be bundled in a closed-source app. (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ChenglongMa/SkinToneClassifier/main/LICENSE (GNU GPL Version 3, 29 June 2007).)
- [confirmed] Sony's apparent_skincolor code is Apache-2.0, uses thresholds L* 60 (dark/light) and hue 55° (red/yellow), and its algorithm is Gaussian σ=1, k-means with 5 clusters on (L*, hue), the histogram mode per cluster, then the 3 brightest clusters. → **Minor nuance: predict.py seeds KMeans with random_state=2021, so it is deterministic as shipped. Hue is computed as degrees(arctan(b/(a+eps))) rather than atan2, and the code uses the deprecated skimage `multichannel=True`, so a port should use atan2 and the current skimage API. The thresholds come from face datasets (CelebAMask-HQ, FFHQ, CFD).** (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/SonyResearch/apparent_skincolor/main/LICENSE (Apache 2.0); https://raw.githubusercontent.com/SonyResearch/apparent_skincolor/main/README.md ('values below 60 are considered to be dark tones'; 'values below 55 are considered to red hues'); https://raw.githubusercontent.com/SonyResearch/apparent_skincolor/main/extract/predict.py)
- [confirmed] FC4 is MIT, and FFCC is Apache-2.0 but expects linear RAW-like input. → **Caveat: the FC4 code is MIT, but it is Python 2 / TensorFlow 1 and trained on Gehler-Shi and NUS-8 RAW data. The repo does not state any license for the trained weights or the datasets, so commercial use of pretrained FC4 weights is unverified; retraining on your own data avoids the question.** (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/yuanming-hu/fc4/master/LICENSE (MIT); https://raw.githubusercontent.com/yuanming-hu/fc4/master/README.md ('these photos are linear (RAW sensor activations)'); https://raw.githubusercontent.com/google/ffcc/master/LICENSE (Apache 2.0); https://raw.githubusercontent.com/google/ffcc/master/README.md ('linear data after black level removed'))
- [confirmed] js-aruco's LICENSE mixes MIT, BSD-style and LGPL-v3 parts, and the default opencv.js build probably lacks the contrib mcc chart detector (unverified). → **Both parts are confirmed. Added fact that matters for the architecture: the default opencv.js 4.x build already includes objdetect's ArucoDetector, CharucoDetector, Dictionary and GridBoard (whitelist in platforms/js/opencv_js.config.py), and OpenCV 4.x is Apache-2.0. Detecting the ArUco salon card therefore does not need the LGPL-tainted js-aruco. mcc is not in the whitelist, so ColorChecker detection needs a custom WASM build or your own marker-based patch locating.** (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/jcmellado/js-aruco/master/LICENSE.txt (MIT + BSD + GNU LGPL v3 text); https://raw.githubusercontent.com/opencv/opencv/4.x/platforms/js/opencv_js.config.py (lines 133-149, white_list line 236 with no mcc); https://raw.githubusercontent.com/opencv/opencv/4.x/LICENSE (Apache 2.0); npm @techstark/opencv-js 5.0.0-release.1 is Apache-2.0)
- [corrected] W3C Image Capture implementation status: Chrome supports whiteBalanceMode and exposureMode on Android, Linux/ChromeOS, Windows (60) and Mac (111); colorTemperature only on Android (60); torch is not listed; Firefox needs a flag; Safari has no data. → **In that file (notes updated Feb 2023), colorTemperature is listed for Android (60), Linux/ChromeOS (✓), Windows (60) AND Mac (111), not Android only; exposureCompensation shows no version on Android; and Chrome's 'Unsupported platforms' line is 'iOS'. The 'Safari has no data' part is stale. MDN browser-compat-data 8.1.7 (2026-10-22) records ImageCapture in Safari/iOS Safari from 18.4 (takePhoto, getPhotoCapabilities, getPhotoSettings) and grabFrame from Safari 26. Firefox still needs the dom.imagecapture.enabled flag.** (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/w3c/mediacapture-image/main/implementation-status.md ; https://registry.npmjs.org/@mdn/browser-compat-data/-/browser-compat-data-8.1.7.tgz (data.json → api.ImageCapture) ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/mdn/browser-compat-data/main/api/ImageCapture.json)
- [corrected] Torch control is not available or not reliable in iOS Safari, iOS Safari mostly cannot lock AWB or AE from the web, and torch works on Chrome Android via applyConstraints({advanced:[{torch:true}]}). → **WebKit implements the `torch` constraint (commit '[MediaStream] Add support for torch', 4 Oct 2023) and the `whiteBalanceMode` constraint (commit '[MediaStream] Add support for whiteBalanceMode', 18 Sep 2023). They are exposed in MediaTrackSupportedConstraints, MediaTrackCapabilities and MediaTrackConstraints with no feature flag. AVVideoCaptureSource.mm maps whiteBalanceMode 'manual' to AVCaptureWhiteBalanceModeLocked, 'single-shot' to AutoWhiteBalance and 'continuous' to ContinuousAutoWhiteBalance, and drives AVCaptureTorchMode. So on iOS Safari a web app CAN lock AWB and switch the torch on supported cameras. What iOS still cannot do: WebKit's IDL marks exposureMode, exposureCompensation, exposureTime, colorTemperature, iso and focusMode as FIXME (not implemented), so exposure cannot be locked and a Kelvin value cannot be set from the web on iOS. I did not verify which Safari release first shipped this; it is probably 17.x. Chrome Android torch and AWB/AE lock are confirmed in Chromium's VideoCaptureCamera2.java (SUPPORTS_TORCH, CONTROL_AWB_LOCK, CONTROL_AE_LOCK).** (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/WebKit/WebKit/main/Source/WebCore/Modules/mediastream/MediaTrackConstraints.idl ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/WebKit/WebKit/main/Source/WebCore/Modules/mediastream/MediaTrackSupportedConstraints.idl ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/WebKit/WebKit/main/Source/WebCore/platform/mediastream/cocoa/AVVideoCaptureSource.mm ; https://github.com/WebKit/WebKit/commits/main/Source/WebCore/Modules/mediastream/MediaTrackConstraints.idl ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/chromium/chromium/main/media/capture/video/android/java/src/org/chromium/media/VideoCaptureCamera2.java)
- [confirmed] In Fitzpatrick17k, ITA from uncalibrated photos agreed with expert Fitzpatrick labels within ±1 type in 45.84% of cases (full image) and 52.97% (YCbCr mask), and especially poorly for types III-IV (34-44%). → **Exact table: Type I 49.48/50.24%, II 43.04/49.34%, III 34.71/43.89%, IV 34.42/40.89%, V 78.54/92.53%, VI 73.73/88.39%, overall 45.84/52.97%. Nuances: the analysis cell calls ITA_label with method=None, i.e. the 'empirical' thresholds, not Kinyanjui's (both are defined). The code also computes 'no_mask_ITA_label' from the ycbcr_mask_ITA column, so the 'Full Image' column may not be what its name says. These are clinical disease photos, so they are only a rough proxy for hand photos.** (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/mattgroh/fitzpatrick17k/main/ita_fitzpatrick_analysis.ipynb (cells 6 and 8))
- [unverifiable] ITA° = arctan((L*-50)/b*)·180/π (Chardon 1991), with six categories >55, 41-55, 28-41, 10-28, -30 to 10, < -30 (Del Bino 2006). (The DOIs for Chardon 1991 and Del Bino 2006/2013 could not be reached (doi.org, Wiley and PubMed all failed DNS). The only reachable code, the Fitzpatrick17k notebook, uses the same ITA formula and the Kinyanjui thresholds (>55, >41, >28, >19, >10, ≤10) for mapping to Fitzpatrick, which is a different scheme from the six Del Bino categories. The Del Bino thresholds are not confirmed by a primary source in this run.)
- [confirmed] Ramadan starts about 8 Feb 2027, Eid al-Fitr is about 9-10 Mar 2027 and Eid al-Adha about 16-17 May 2027 (2026: Eid al-Fitr about 20 Mar, Eid al-Adha about 27 May), all subject to moon sighting. → **This is a computational check, not an official Omani source. On the Umm al-Qura calendar (hijridate library), 1 Ramadan 1448 = 2027-02-08, 1 Shawwal 1448 = 2027-03-09 and 10 Dhu al-Hijjah 1448 = 2027-05-16; for 1447, Eid al-Fitr = 2026-03-20 and Eid al-Adha = 2026-05-27. python-holidays' Omani estimates for 2027 are Eid al-Fitr holidays 8-11 Mar and Eid al-Adha holidays 15-18 May. Oman announces dates by local crescent sighting, so expect ±1 day.** (PyPI hijridate (Umm al-Qura) computation run this session; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/vacanza/holidays/dev/holidays/countries/oman.py)
- [corrected] Oman National Day is 20 November, moved from 18 November by royal decree under Sultan Haitham and observed from 2022 or 2023. → **The date is right but the year is wrong. python-holidays, which cites 'Decree 15/2025 (National day is moved)' (decree.om/2025/rd20250015), keeps 18-19 Nov holidays up to 2024 and moves them to 20-21 Nov from 2025. The move dates from Royal Decree 15/2025 (Jan 2025), with 20 Nov first observed in 2025, not 2022/2023. This comes from a maintained library that cites the decree; decree.om itself could not be reached.** (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/vacanza/holidays/dev/holidays/countries/oman.py (lines 43-45 and 81-89))
- [unverifiable] Oman PDPL (Royal Decree 6/2022, in force February 2023; Executive Regulations by Ministerial Decision 34/2024) treats biometric, genetic, health and ethnic-origin data as sensitive; processing needs a Ministry permit, children's data needs guardian consent, and cross-border transfer is restricted. (qanoon.om, decree.om and mtcit.gov.om all failed DNS, and WebSearch was unavailable (budget exhausted). No primary text was reached. Because this claim drives the privacy architecture, local counsel or a later run must check it against the official text.)
- [unverifiable] Pantone Color of the Year 2025 is 'Mocha Mousse' 17-1230 (about #A47864) and 2026 is 'Cloud Dancer' 11-4201 (about #F0EEE9). (pantone.com could not be reached and no primary mirror exists on GitHub, npm or PyPI. The hex values are the report's approximations, not Pantone-published values, and should stay admin-editable in Firestore as the report proposes.)
- [unverifiable] The EU banned the TPO photoinitiator in cosmetics, including gel polish, from 1 Sep 2025 under Regulation (EU) 2025/877 (Omnibus VII); GSO/GCC status unknown. Moving from 0.10.35 to 1.x is a major-version upgrade.** (HEAD https://storage.googleapis.com/mediapipe-models/hand_landmarker/hand_landmarker/float16/latest/hand_landmarker.task returned content-length 7819105, last-modified 2023-04-26. Unzipping the bundle gives hand_detector.tflite (2,339,878 B) and hand_landmarks_detector.tflite (5,478,949 B). These are byte-for-byte the legacy palm_detection_full.tflite (2,339,846 B) and hand_landmark_full.tflite (5,478,917 B) plus about 32 B, so the Oct-2021 FULL model card applies. - [corrected] Model card fairness: full-model MNAE averages 5.08% across Fitzpatrick 1-6 and 10.09% across 14 regions, with type 1 worst at 5.76% and type 6 at 6.21%. Landmark error near the fingertip is about 5-10% of palm size. Henna, jewelry, tattoos, gloves and held objects are out of scope. → **The averages (5.08% full / 5.67% lite by skin type; 10.09% full / 12.02% lite by region), the out-of-scope list and the 'not tested in-the-wild, low-end devices, low light, motion blur' wording are all correct. Two errors: (1) The worst skin type is type 6 (6.21%), with type 1 second (5.76%); the per-type full-model values are 5.76 / 4.67 / 4.60 / 4.69 / 4.53 / 6.21. (2) The card gives no fingertip-specific number. It only says 'per-joint MNAE is the smallest at the base of each finger, and gets larger toward the fingertip'. So 5.08% and 10.09% are averages over all 21 joints, and fingertip error is HIGHER than those (amount not stated). This strengthens the case for mandatory segmentation; it does not change it.** (https://storage.googleapis.com/mediapipe-assets/Model%20Card%20Hand%20Tracking%20(Lite_Full)%20with%20Fairness%20Oct%202021.pdf (downloaded and parsed with pdftotext))
- [corrected] MediaPipe tasks-vision 1.1.1 has no WebGPU path; its GPU delegate is WebGL2 only ('no WebGPU code in the 1.1.1 bundle'). → **For the public API the claim holds. vision.d.ts offers only delegate?: 'CPU' | 'GPU', the documented GPU contexts are WebGL2RenderingContext, and vision_bundle.mjs has no WebGPU references. However, the shipped WASM loader (vision_wasm_internal.js) DOES contain Emscripten WebGPU (emwgpu/Dawn) glue: about 239 'WebGPU' references, a Module['preinitializedWebGPUDevice'] hook and canvas.getContext('webgpu'). These are not exposed through the JS API. Plan on WebGL2 for MediaPipe, but re-check each 1.x release, because a WebGPU delegate looks to be in progress. The WASM size is verified: vision_wasm_internal.wasm is 13,519,922 B (3,954,071 B gzipped), and the no-SIMD build is 12,627,509 B.** (npm tarball @mediapipe/tasks-vision 1.1.1: vision.d.ts, vision_bundle.mjs, wasm/vision_wasm_internal.js)

### rendering
- [confirmed] iOS/iPadOS Safari (WebKit) supports only these camera constraints: whiteBalanceMode, zoom, torch, backgroundBlur and powerEfficient. exposureMode, focusMode, exposureCompensation, exposureTime, colorTemperature, iso, brightness and focusDistance are FIXME placeholders. (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/WebKit/WebKit/main/Source/WebCore/Modules/mediastream/MediaTrackConstraints.idl (fetched 2026-10-24). The dictionary's active members include whiteBalanceMode, zoom, torch, backgroundBlur and powerEfficient. exposureMode, focusMode, pointsOfInterest, exposureCompensation, exposureTime, colorTemperature, iso, brightness, contrast, saturation, sharpness and focusDistance appear only as FIXME placeholders.)
- [confirmed] WebKit maps whiteBalanceMode 'manual' to AVCaptureWhiteBalanceModeLocked, 'single-shot' to AutoWhiteBalance and 'continuous' to ContinuousAutoWhiteBalance. Reconfiguration can switch off the torch, so it is re-enabled. takePhoto encodes JPEG at AVVideoQualityKey 1 and uses the format's max photo dimensions. (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/WebKit/WebKit/main/Source/WebCore/platform/mediastream/cocoa/AVVideoCaptureSource.mm: whiteBalanceModeFromMeteringMode and supportedWhiteBalanceModes (lines ~172-200 and ~534-546), photoConfiguration (AVVideoCodecTypeJPEG, AVVideoQualityKey @(1), maxPhotoSizeForCurrentPreset), and the comment 'Updating the device configuration may switch off the torch' (~line 1009).)
- [corrected] On Android, Chrome implements exposureMode 'manual' as an AE lock, so you can lock AE and also apply a slightly negative exposureCompensation (-0.3 to -0.7 EV) to protect highlights. → **Chromium does not set CONTROL_AE_LOCK when applying constraints. exposureMode 'manual' maps to AndroidMeteringMode.FIXED, and configureCommonCaptureSettings() then sets CONTROL_AE_MODE_OFF. It sets SENSOR_EXPOSURE_TIME to the last exposure time seen in a capture result, or to the middle of the sensor range if none is known. SENSOR_SENSITIVITY (ISO) is set only if the page passed an explicit iso; otherwise the TEMPLATE_PREVIEW request value applies, not the ISO that AE had chosen. The ISO in getSettings() is read from the request, not the capture result. CONTROL_AE_LOCK_AVAILABLE only decides whether 'manual' is advertised in getCapabilities(). The W3C spec also says exposureCompensation is used only in 'continuous'/'single-shot' modes, so a negative EV has no effect after switching to 'manual'. Implications: (1) set exposureCompensation while still in 'continuous' and let AE converge, then switch to 'manual'. (2) Expect a brightness jump at lock time, and run or refit the card calibration after locking, never before. (3) Where needed, drive exposureTime (100 µs units) and iso explicitly in a closed loop against the card. (4) Keep per-frame software exposure correction on Android too, not only on iOS.** (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/chromium/chromium/main/media/capture/video/android/java/src/org/chromium/media/VideoCaptureCamera2.java (configureCommonCaptureSettings: 'if (mExposureMode == NONE || FIXED) CONTROL_AE_MODE_OFF ... SENSOR_EXPOSURE_TIME = mLastExposureTimeNs'; 'if (mIso > 0) SENSOR_SENSITIVITY'; AE_LOCK is only read, never written); https://raw.githubusercontent.com/chromium/chromium/main/media/capture/video/android/video_capture_device_android.cc (MANUAL -> FIXED); W3C Image Capture spec source https://raw.githubusercontent.com/w3c/mediacapture-image/gh-pages/index.html ('Exposure Compensation ... only used in single-shot and continuous exposureMode'))
- [confirmed] Android Chrome whiteBalanceMode: 'manual' = CONTROL_AWB_LOCK, 'continuous' = AWB auto, 'none' = AWB off, 'single-shot' not supported. colorTemperature maps to the nearest of 7 AWB presets (2850/2950/4250/4600/5000/6000/7000 K), with step 50 reported. (VideoCaptureCamera2.java: COLOR_TEMPERATURES_MAP (lines ~1104-1114), STEP_COLOR_TEMPERATURE 50, comment 'White Balance mode AndroidMeteringMode.SINGLE_SHOT is not supported', FIXED -> CONTROL_AWB_LOCK true. Extra detail found: once colorTemperature > 0 has been set, the preset AWB mode is re-applied on every request, even after switching back to 'continuous', because mColorTemperature is never reset. Treat colorTemperature as one-way within a track and use software white balance instead.)
- [confirmed] ImageCapture.takePhoto: Chrome Android 60+, Safari iOS 18.4+. grabFrame: Chrome 59+, Safari 26+. (@mdn/browser-compat-data 8.1.7 data.json (npm, published 2026-10-22): api.ImageCapture.takePhoto chrome_android 60, safari_ios 18.4; api.ImageCapture.grabFrame chrome_android 59, safari_ios 26.)
- [corrected] MediaStreamTrackProcessor: Chrome 94+ (window-exposed) and Safari 18+, usable as an alternative frame source. → **In Safari 18+, MediaStreamTrackProcessor exists only in DedicatedWorker scope: WebKit IDL has Exposed=DedicatedWorker and EnabledBySetting=MediaStreamTrackProcessingEnabled. new MediaStreamTrackProcessor() on the main thread is undefined in Safari. Chrome's Window exposure is non-standard, and BCD flags it as a partial implementation. A shared code path must transfer the MediaStreamTrack to a worker. Otherwise use requestVideoFrameCallback (Safari 15.4+, Chrome 83+) as the main-thread primary path.** (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/WebKit/WebKit/main/Source/WebCore/Modules/mediastream/MediaStreamTrackProcessor.idl ; BCD 8.1.7 api.MediaStreamTrackProcessor (chrome_android 94 partial: 'Exposed on Window instead of DedicatedWorker'; safari_ios 18))
- [confirmed] requestVideoFrameCallback: Safari 15.4+, Chrome 83+. VideoFrame.colorSpace: Chrome 94+, Safari 16.4+. (BCD 8.1.7: api.HTMLVideoElement.requestVideoFrameCallback (chrome_android 83, safari_ios 15.4); api.VideoFrame.colorSpace (chrome_android 94, safari_ios 16.4))
- [confirmed] WebGL drawingBufferColorSpace='display-p3': Chrome 104+, Safari 16.4+. Safari lacks unpackColorSpace, so uploaded video/textures are treated as sRGB. (BCD 8.1.7: api.WebGL2RenderingContext.drawingBufferColorSpace (chrome_android 104, safari_ios 16.4); unpackColorSpace (chrome 104, safari/safari_ios false). Khronos WebGL spec source (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/KhronosGroup/WebGL/main/specs/latest/1.0/index.html) says both attributes default to 'srgb' and that uploads convert to unpackColorSpace. That Safari actually converts P3 video to sRGB on upload is inferred from the spec default, not observed. Log it on real devices.)
- [confirmed] WebGPU ships in Safari 26 and Chrome Android 121+. (BCD 8.1.7 api.GPU: chrome_android 121, safari_ios 26 (desktop Chrome on Linux only fully from 144))
- [confirmed] WebGL2 baseline: Safari iOS 15+, Chrome Android 58+. EXT_color_buffer_float: Safari iOS 15, Chrome Android 56. EXT_color_buffer_half_float: Safari iOS 14, Chrome Android 63. OES_texture_float_linear on iOS is iPadOS-only. OffscreenCanvas webgl2: Safari 17+, Chrome 69+. (BCD 8.1.7: api.WebGL2RenderingContext (chrome_android 58, safari_ios 15); api.EXT_color_buffer_float (56/15); api.EXT_color_buffer_half_float (63/14); api.OES_texture_float_linear safari_ios note 'Only supported on iPadOS.'; api.OffscreenCanvas.getContext.webgl2_context (chrome_android 69, safari_ios 17))

- [confirmed] culori's differenceCiede2000 converts inputs to lab65 internally. Feeding D50 'lab' objects gives 1.8566 instead of 2.0425 on Sharma pair 1; with lab65 inputs the Sharma reference values are reproduced. (culori 4.0.2 src/difference.js line 100: let lab = converter('lab65'). Re-run 2026-10-24 with node 22: lab65 inputs gave 2.0425, 2.8615, 4.3065, 2.3669 and 1.2644, all exact. 'lab' (D50) inputs gave 1.8566, 2.5521, 3.9586, 2.5204 and 1.2267.)
- [confirmed] MediaPipe's own landmark One-Euro filter uses min_cutoff 0.05, beta 80, derivate_cutoff 1.0. (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/google-ai-edge/mediapipe/master/mediapipe/modules/pose_landmark/pose_landmark_filtering.pbtxt. Those values apply to the pose IMAGE landmarks. World landmarks (meters) use min_cutoff 0.1, beta 40, derivate_cutoff 1.0 with disable_value_scaling: true, and the ROI filter uses 0.01/10/1.0. Use the 0.1/40 set as the starting point for the 3D finger-orientation filters.)
- [confirmed] Filament: clamp perceptual roughness to >= 0.089 for half-float (mediump) math; GGX alpha = perceptualRoughness^2. (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/google/filament/main/docs/Filament.md.html ('we must clamp the roughness to 0.089'; Frostbite clamps to 0.045 in fp32). Filament shaders: MIN_PERCEPTUAL_ROUGHNESS 0.089 (fp16) / 0.045 (fp32), and clearCoatPerceptualRoughness = clamp(..., 0.089, 1.0). This conflicts internally with the research's gel recipe of clearcoat perceptual roughness 0.03-0.06: those values are below both the fp16 clamp and the fp32 minimum. Use 0.045-0.09 with highp, or 0.089 with mediump.)
- [confirmed] Linear sRGB -> linear Display-P3 matrix [[0.8225,0.1775,0],[0.0332,0.9668,0],[0.0171,0.0724,0.9105]] and its inverse; Display-P3 uses the sRGB transfer curve. Image Capture exposureTime is in 100 µs units. (Recomputed from the sRGB/P3 primaries and D65 (numpy), matching to 4 decimals, inverse included. CSS Color 4 source (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/main/css-color-4/Overview.bs): display-p3 'Transfer function: same as srgb'. W3C Image Capture source: 'The value is in 100 microsecond units'.)
- [confirmed] WebXR Lighting Estimation (XRLightEstimate) exists only in Chrome/Chrome Android 90+, is experimental, and is not in Safari. (BCD 8.1.7 api.XRLightEstimate: chrome_android 90, safari_ios false, status experimental: true)
- [unverifiable] Kubelka finite-layer reflectance R = (1 − Rg(a − b·coth(bSX))) / (a − Rg + b·coth(bSX)), with a = 1 + K/S and b = √(a² − 1) (Kubelka 1948). → **The 1948 JOSA paper could not be reached (DOI and publisher hosts blocked). The formula does pass both mathematical limit checks: as SX→∞ (coth→1), R → a − b = R∞, independent of Rg; as SX→0 (coth→∞), R → Rg. It is the standard hyperbolic two-flux solution, but the citation itself is not verified.** (Algebraic check only; https://doi.org/10.1364/JOSA.38.000448 not reachable)
- [unverifiable] ΔE00 thresholds from Paravina et al. 2015 (50:50 perceptibility 0.8, acceptability 1.8) and Mahy 1994 (ΔE*ab JND ≈ 2.3). → **No primary source was reachable this session: the WebSearch budget for the turn was exhausted, and doi.org, Crossref, Wiley and arXiv were blocked by the proxy. The values match common domain knowledge, but they were not re-verified. Keep the 'not re-verified' label on the validation targets until someone checks them.** (https://doi.org/10.1111/jerd.12149 (not reachable))
- [unverifiable] ModiFace nail try-on paper: Duke et al. 2019, CVPR Workshops, arXiv 1906.02222. → **arXiv, export.arxiv.org, CVF open access and Semantic Scholar were all blocked. The arXiv ID and venue remain unverified.** (https://arxiv.org/abs/1906.02222 (not reachable))

Standard edition is still the right choice.** (https://cloud.google.com/firestore/pricing (fetched 2026-10-24; Standard edition page): 'Quotas are applied daily and reset around midnight Pacific time', 'Firestore allows exactly one free database per project', 'No free quota for named databases...
