## AI / computer-vision stack

FREE IN-BROWSER AI / COMPUTER-VISION STACK FOR NAIL TRY-ON. Research verdict as of 2026-10-24.

RESEARCH CAVEAT: this session's shared WebSearch budget was already used up when the task started. The sandbox proxy also blocked arxiv.org, ai.google.dev, developers.google.com, huggingface.co, kaggle.com, universe.roboflow.com, docs.ultralytics.com, developer.chrome.com, onnxruntime.ai and pytorch.org. Facts were therefore checked only against primary sources that were reachable:
- GitHub repos, LICENSE files and docs folders (including the docs sources for ONNX Runtime, Ultralytics and MediaPipe)
- the npm registry and PyPI, for versions, licenses and package sizes
- Google Cloud Storage, for MediaPipe model files, byte sizes and the hand-tracking model-card PDF
Anything taken from memory is marked UNVERIFIED in the fields below. This applies to the ModiFace paper numbers, Gemini free-tier quotas and terms, Kaggle and Colab GPU quotas, and the Roboflow and Kaggle nail datasets.

RECOMMENDED ARCHITECTURE (all permissive licenses, zero server cost):
1) MediaPipe Hand Landmarker (Apache-2.0). Model hand_landmarker.task, float16, 7.82 MB (5.82 MB gzipped); it bundles the palm detector (2.34 MB, 192x192 input) and the landmark model (5.48 MB, 224x224 input). It already runs in the app (tasks-vision 0.10.35 is pinned; the current stable is 1.1.1, released 2026-10-14). It gives 21 landmarks per hand, handedness and metric world landmarks.
2) Per-nail segmentation as a two-stage, landmark-guided crop model, the same detector-then-crop idea MediaPipe itself uses. For each finger, cut a rotation-normalised crop (DIP to TIP axis, about 2.2-3x the distal-segment length, so long extensions and stilettos fit) and run a tiny 2-3 class U-Net / LRASPP with a MobileNetV3 encoder on 5-10 crops batched together. Instance identity comes free from the finger index, so no AGPL YOLO-seg or heavy instance-segmentation model is needed. Model budget is 1.5 MB or less.
   - Train in PyTorch with segmentation_models.pytorch (MIT) or torchvision (BSD).
   - Export to TFLite with litert-torch (Apache-2.0) and add metadata with MediaPipe's image_segmenter metadata writer, so the model runs inside the MediaPipe ImageSegmenter slot the app already has. That keeps a single WASM runtime and the WebGL2 GPU delegate.
   - Alternatives are LiteRT.js (WebGPU/WebNN, about 3.1 MB gzipped extra WASM) or ONNX Runtime Web 1.30 (WebGPU/WASM, 3.7-6.7 MB gzipped extra WASM).
   - Fallback: a full-frame semantic model for photo mode when no hand or palm is detected.
3) Nail orientation from the DIP-to-TIP vector (thumb IP-to-TIP), refined by mask PCA (principal axis of the mask) with its sign set by the landmark vector. 3D nail normal from world landmarks. Curvature from a parametric cylinder prior plus a shading-profile heuristic; it cannot be measured from a single photo.
4) Skin sampling on a landmark-defined polygon on the back of the hand (MCP line to wrist, inset), in white-balanced CIELAB, with highlight, shadow, henna and jewelry trimming. ITA degree gives depth; hue angle gives undertone. An optional white or grey reference card is needed for trustworthy undertone.
5) Natural-language advice: a deterministic bilingual (AR/EN) rules engine as the core. Gemini through Firebase AI Logic is optional, sending only anonymised attributes and never hand photos. Reason: free-tier data may be used by Google and reviewed by humans (UNVERIFIED, from memory), and quotas change.
   - Chrome's built-in Prompt API (Gemini Nano) is desktop-only and was documented around en/es/ja input as of Feb 2026; it does not fit mobile Arabic use.
   - WebLLM needs WebGPU and 0.4-3.4 GB of VRAM per model (for example Qwen3.5-0.8B q4f16 needs about 1.63 GB), too heavy for 4G phones.
6) Age: do not infer it from hands. No reliable free model exists, and it is ethically and legally risky. Use an optional self-declared style preference instead.

KEY LICENSE FINDINGS:
- Ultralytics YOLO code and weights (including YOLO26, the current version) are AGPL-3.0, and so is the ultralytics/assets weights repo. Shipping them in a closed commercial web app needs the paid, quote-only Ultralytics Enterprise License.
- SegFormer weights carry the NVIDIA Source Code License: research or evaluation only. Also avoid the mit_b* encoder weights in segmentation_models.pytorch for the same reason.
- Albumentations is now continued as AlbumentationsX under AGPL-3.0. Pin albumentations 2.0.8 (MIT) or use torchvision v2 / Kornia.
- MediaPipe Model Maker has no image segmenter (only gesture recognizer, image classifier, object detector) and was last released April 2024.
- TensorFlow.js was last released 4.22.0 in Oct 2024, and Ultralytics replaced its tfjs export with LiteRT. Do not start new work on TF.js.
- MMDetection's last release was 3.3.0 in Jan 2024 (stale).
- Permissive alternatives: RTMDet-Ins (Apache, 5.6M parameters for the tiny size) and RF-DETR-Seg (Apache, but 33.6M+ parameters, too big for 4G).
- Labeling assistants: SAM 2.1 (Apache-2.0), SAM 3 (SAM License, royalty-free commercial use with trade-control restrictions; 848M parameters, server/Colab GPU only), MobileSAM and EfficientSAM (Apache), Grounded-SAM-2 (Apache), CVAT (MIT), Label Studio plus its ML backend with SAM2 image and video (Apache).

WEB RUNTIME FACTS (verified on the gpuweb implementation-status wiki): WebGPU ships in Chrome Android 121+ on Android 12+ with ARM, Qualcomm and Intel GPUs, and in Safari 26 on iOS/iPadOS/macOS. It is still TBD on Samsung Xclipse GPUs and behind a flag in Firefox Android. MediaPipe tasks-vision uses WebGL2 for GPU, not WebGPU. ORT-Web multithreading needs cross-origin isolation (COOP/COEP headers, settable in firebase.json).

DATA: there is no high-quality, clearly commercially licensed nail-segmentation dataset that could be verified. The MediaPipe hand model card itself lists henna, jewelry, tattoos, gloves and held objects as out of scope, and reports landmark error growing toward the fingertip (5-10% of palm size, so landmarks alone cannot place nails accurately). The product therefore needs its own consented GCC dataset:
- 2,500-4,000 images from 300+ people
- all 10 Monk Skin Tone (MST) levels, henna, extensions, French tips, glitter/chrome, kids with guardian consent, elderly, many phones and lighting conditions
- labeled with SAM-assisted polygons plus base and tip keypoints

Targets:
- nail IoU of 0.90 or more; 10th-percentile per-nail IoU of 0.80 or more
- Boundary F1 at 2 px of 0.85 or more; per-nail recall of 0.98 or more
- subgroup gap of 3 IoU points or less
- 24 fps or more on mid-range Android, 30 fps or more on iPhone 12+
- total first-load AI payload of about 10-12 MB gzipped, cached by a service worker

'100% accurate' is not achievable. The honest-limits list covers why: camera auto white balance, screens, existing polish, curvature, device fragmentation and patents.

| Task | Recommended | Why | Risk |
|---|---|---|---|
| (a) Hand detection + 21-landmark tracking (multi-hand, handedness, world landmarks) | MediaPipe Hand Landmarker via @mediapipe/tasks-vision (Apache-2.0). Self-host the WASM and hand_landmarker.task (float16, 7,819,105 bytes). Use runningMode VIDEO, numHands 2 (default is 1), delegate GPU with CPU fallback, and One Euro filtering of landmarks. | Free, on-device, already integrated in the app, and Apache-2.0 for both code and model (the model card states Apache 2.0). Two-stage pipeline: palm detector at 192x192 input (2016x18 anchor output), then landmark regressor on a 224x224 crop giving 21 screen landmarks, handedness, presence score and 21 metric world landmarks from the GHUM hand model. In VIDEO mode the palm detector runs only when tracking is lost, so cost is mostly the landmark model per hand. Fairness (model card, full model): MNAE (mean absolute landmark error normalised by palm size) is 5.08% on average across Fitzpatrick 1-6. Type 1 is worst at 5.76% and type 6 at 6.21%. Across 14 world regions the average is 10.09%. Error is smallest at the finger base and largest at the fingertip; that is exactly where nails are, so segmentation refinement is mandatory for an accurate look. | Model card (verified) lists as out of scope: gloves, hands holding objects, and decoration on the hand including jewelry, tattoos and henna. It was 'not tested in in-the-wild smartphone conditions, including low-end devices, low light, motion blur'. Errors grow for blurry or occluded joints. The detector finds PALMS, so extreme close-ups showing only fingertips may not be detected; the UX must frame the whole hand. Handedness assumes mirrored (selfie) input unless the app compensates. Long extensions or stilettos may pull the TIP landmark. Overlapping hands can swap identities. |
| (b) Per-nail instance segmentation (live camera) | Two-stage, landmark-guided rotated crops per finger, then a tiny semantic segmenter, giving instances by construction.
- Model: U-Net or LRASPP decoder on a MobileNetV3-Small/Large encoder (torchvision BSD weights), 128x128 or 160x160 input.
- Classes: background / nail plate including polish / free edge or extension (optional cuticle band).
- Train with PyTorch + segmentation_models.pytorch (MIT).
- Export to .tflite with litert-torch (Apache-2.0), then add metadata with mediapipe.tasks.python.metadata.metadata_writers.image_segmenter (Apache-2.0) and run in MediaPipe ImageSegmenter (already wired in src/ai/segmentation/nailSegmenter.js).
- Batch all 5-10 crops into one call where possible.
- Warp masks back with the inverse affine transform and refine edges with a guided or joint-bilateral filter. | Landmark error near fingertips (model card) means landmark-only nail ellipses will be visibly misaligned; pixel masks are needed. Cropping per finger gives each nail many pixels at small input sizes, normalises rotation, kills false positives on rings, skin and background, and yields instance ids (finger plus hand) without an instance-segmentation head, so no AGPL. Model size can stay well under 1.5 MB (LRASPP-MobileNetV3-Large is 3.22M parameters, 12.49 MB fp32, at full width; a Small encoder at 128 px is far smaller). It reuses the single MediaPipe runtime already loaded, so no second 3-7 MB WASM download. Same principle as ModiFace's small-object design and MediaPipe's own detector-then-crop pipeline. | Depends on landmarks (fails where the hand is not detected: heavy henna, gloves, palm out of frame); keep the full-frame fallback. Clear acrylic or transparent extensions and nail art with skin-like nude colors are hard. Crops must grow adaptively when the mask touches the crop border (long stilettos). Two hands means up to 10 crops per frame; batch them, or alternate frames and warp masks in between. |
| (b2) Photo mode / high-quality still render | Run the same crop model at higher resolution (192-256 px crops) on the full-resolution photo, then edge-aware refinement (fast guided filter or joint bilateral upsampling of the confidence map using the RGB image), 1-2 px alpha feather at sidewalls and cuticle. Optional user tap-to-fix with the lazily loaded MediaPipe InteractiveSegmenter v2. | Edges at the cuticle and sidewalls decide whether the result looks real. A still photo allows a 1-1.5 s budget, so higher resolution and refinement are affordable. | Phone camera processing (HDR, sharpening, beauty filters) creates halos that bias edges. |
| (b3) In-browser inference runtime for the custom nail model | Default: MediaPipe tasks-vision ImageSegmenter with the custom .tflite plus segmenter metadata (WebGL2 GPU delegate, CPU fallback). It shares the WASM already downloaded for the hand tracker. Upgrade path: LiteRT.js (@litertjs/core 2.6.0, Apache-2.0) for WebGPU / WebNN with per-op WASM fallback where JSPI is available. | Avoids a second runtime (+3-7 MB gzipped) on 4G. LiteRT is Google's TFLite successor and Ultralytics now routes browser deployment to LiteRT.js. ORT-Web is the best choice if the model is ONNX-native. | WebGPU is unavailable on iOS below 26 and is 'TBD' on Samsung Xclipse GPUs in Chrome Android, so the WASM/WebGL fallback must be kept and tested. Running two runtimes doubles memory and download. |
| (b4) Training framework, augmentation and export toolchain | Stack:
- Training: PyTorch 2.14 + torchvision 0.29 (BSD) + segmentation_models.pytorch 0.5.0 (MIT) + timm (Apache-2.0) with lightweight encoders (MobileNetV3, EfficientNet-Lite, MobileOne after checking its license).
- Augmentation: albumentations 2.0.8 (MIT, pinned) or torchvision.transforms.v2 / Kornia (Apache-2.0).
- Export: litert-torch 0.10.0 (Apache-2.0) to .tflite, ai-edge-quantizer for int8/fp16, MediaPipe metadata writer. Alternatively torch.onnx export with onnx 1.23 for ORT-Web. | Every piece is permissive. Avoid AlbumentationsX (AGPL-3.0-only, the successor of albumentations since 2.0.8), Ultralytics (AGPL) and NVIDIA SegFormer / MiT weights (non-commercial). MediaPipe Model Maker cannot train segmenters. | ImageNet-pretrained weights are an industry-wide grey zone (dataset terms). torchvision's README says pre-trained models 'may have their own licenses'; SWAG weights are CC-BY-NC. |
| (b5) Labeling and auto-labeling | Self-hosted Label Studio (Apache-2.0) with label-studio-ml-backend (Apache-2.0) examples segment_anything_2_image / segment_anything_2_video, or CVAT (MIT) with its SAM serverless interactor. Auto-label with MediaPipe landmarks giving point prompts to SAM 2.1 (Apache-2.0), or the SAM 3 text prompt 'fingernail' (SAM License, commercial allowed with trade-control restrictions) on a Colab or Kaggle GPU, then human QA of every mask. | Cuts polygon time from minutes to seconds per nail. SAM 2 video propagation labels whole hand videos from one key frame. | SAM masks around cuticles, glitter and transparent extensions are often wrong; human QA is non-negotiable. Do not upload customer photos to third-party hosted labeling SaaS without consent and a data processing agreement. |
| (c) Nail orientation (DIP to TIP) and plate curvature estimation | Steps:
1. Axis: u = normalize(TIP - DIP) (landmarks 8-7, 12-11, 16-15, 20-19; thumb 4-3), smoothed with a One Euro filter.
2. Refine with the mask's principal axis (second moments / PCA, as in nailSegmenter.refineWithMask), with its sign forced to agree with u.
3. Optionally regress base and tip keypoints in the crop model (ModiFace predicted a base-to-tip direction field).
4. 3D: nail normal n from world landmarks, as the cross product of the distal-phalanx direction and the lateral axis (index MCP to pinky MCP); orient by handedness and use the dorsal score; hide or fade a nail when n faces away from the camera.
5. Foreshortening: observed nail length divided by a width-based expected length gives the pitch.
6. Curvature: assume an elliptic-cylinder shell, with transverse radius about 0.5-0.9 x nail width (a prior to tune visually; UNVERIFIED anatomical constant). Estimate per-nail C-curve strength from the luminance falloff across the width in the original image (fit a quadratic to the cross-axis luminance profile).
7. Render shading as target albedo x normalised original shading, plus a specular band along the long axis placed where the real nail's highlight is (top-percentile luminance inside the mask). | A single RGB camera has no true depth on the nail plate. Using the real image's own shading and highlights gives the most photoreal result with zero extra models. The landmark plus mask-PCA combination is robust and cheap. A learned direction head helps with occlusion. | Curvature is an assumption, not a measurement. Thumbs seen side-on and fingers pointing at the camera foreshorten heavily. World landmarks come from synthetic GHUM fitting (model card), so the z value is approximate. |
| (d) Skin sampling region selection (depth + undertone) | ROI: a quad on the back of the hand between the MCP line (landmarks 5, 9, 13, 17), inset about 15-20% toward the wrist (0), shrunk about 12% sideways. Add proximal phalanges (between MCP and PIP) as a secondary ROI. Avoid knuckles (creases, darker), nails, and the ring zone on proximal phalanges. Require the dorsal side (palm normal and handedness); the palm is lighter and not representative.

Pixel rejection: drop the top 5% luminance (specular highlights), the bottom 10% (shadows), and high-chroma orange/brown outliers (henna) via 2-3 cluster k-means in CIELAB, keeping the largest skin-locus cluster. Also drop blue/green vein pixels and saturated or clipped pixels.

Statistics: median Lab after white balance. ITA = atan((L - 50) / b) x 180 / pi gives depth (Chardon bins: very light above 55, light 41-55, intermediate 28-41, tan 10-28, brown -30 to 10, dark below -30). Hue angle h_ab gives undertone (warm / neutral / cool / olive bands to be calibrated). Map to the nearest Monk Skin Tone (MST) orb. Quality gates: enough pixels, low variance, and a confidence score.

Calibration: a white or neutral-grey reference card in frame (von Kries scaling). Without it, flag undertone as low confidence. | Matches the existing src/ai/skinAnalysis.js design, which is sound. The dorsal hand is what the polish is seen against. A robust median with trimming beats a mean. Lab and ITA are standard in dermatology and cosmetics colorimetry. | Phone auto white balance, Smart HDR and beauty modes shift skin color. getUserMedia gives little white-balance control, especially on iOS Safari. Henna on the back of the hand can cover the whole ROI; ask for the other hand or the wrist. Undertone from cameras is inherently uncertain without a reference card. |
| (d2) Nail shape / length and finger-proportion analysis (inputs to the recommender) | From the nail mask in crop coordinates:
- length / width ratio
- free-edge length (class 2 area or extent beyond the fingertip)
- distal contour descriptors: width at 80% and 95% of length relative to max width, tip curvature radius from a circle or ellipse fit, taper angle, pointedness
- classify into square / squoval / round / oval / almond / coffin-ballerina / stiletto with rules or a tiny gradient-boosted classifier on these descriptors

Finger proportions from landmarks (segment lengths normalised by palm size, wrist to middle MCP) plus finger width from a perpendicular skin-edge search (as the existing Smart Fit does). | Deterministic and explainable, works with any segmentation model, no extra download. | Existing extensions mask the natural nail-bed shape; the free edge is hidden under dark polish; label ambiguity between squoval and oval. |
| (d3) Temporal stability | One Euro filter on landmarks (already in src/ai/oneEuro.js). Per-finger affine warp of the previous mask plus EMA blending with the new mask. Run the segmenter every frame on WebGPU-capable devices, every 2nd frame on weak devices, warping in between. Hysteresis on nail visibility. | Flicker at the edges destroys realism more than small static errors do. | Fast hand motion makes warped masks lag. |
| (e) Natural-language advice / recommendation text | A deterministic, explainable rules engine (already started in src/ai/recommend.js) with curated bilingual Arabic/English templates and reason codes (for example: 'MST 7 + warm undertone + short wide nails, so almond or oval and deep berry or terracotta, because...'). OPTIONAL: Gemini Flash-Lite / Flash through Firebase AI Logic for free-form Q&A, sending only anonymised attributes (never hand images), with caching and template fallback when the quota is exhausted. | Zero cost, offline, instant, auditable, consistent across salons, and no privacy exposure. LLMs at the free or on-device level are either privacy-problematic (Gemini free tier) or too heavy and weak in Arabic (on-device 0.5-2B models). | UNVERIFIED (from memory of the Gemini API Additional Terms): content sent to Unpaid Services may be used to improve Google products and reviewed by humans, so no personal data or images go to the free tier. Quotas can be cut without notice. |
| (e2) Age (owner request) | Do NOT estimate age from hands. Offer an optional self-declared preference (for example a style persona such as classic / trendy / minimal), not age. | No validated, free, on-device RGB hand-age model exists. Error would be large, and appearance-based age guessing is sensitive and could be offensive to clients. Datasets with age labels (11K Hands: ages 18-75, research-oriented) do not cover kids and are not cleared for commercial training. It may also trigger personal-data obligations. | Reputational and legal risk (Oman Personal Data Protection Law, Royal Decree 6/2022 (UNVERIFIED detail): sensitive-data categories; consent for minors). |
| (g) Free training compute | Kaggle Notebooks (GPU T4 x2 or P100) for scheduled training runs, plus Google Colab free T4 for interactive SAM-assisted labeling and experiments. Store data in a private Kaggle dataset or Google Drive under a consent-tracked folder. | A crop U-Net trains in under an hour on one T4; SAM 2.1 / SAM 3 auto-labeling fits a 16 GB T4. | Quotas change; uploading customer images to cloud notebooks needs consent and appropriate data handling. |
