### Segmentation model training plan

1. 0. Decide the architecture before collecting data. Use landmark-guided per-finger crops of 128-160 px, rotation-normalised so the DIP-to-TIP axis points up. Crop length is about 2.6x the distal segment and grows adaptively if the mask touches the border. Output 3 classes (background, nail plate incl. polish, free edge / extension) plus 2 keypoint heatmaps (nail base centre, tip centre). Keep a full-frame 256-320 px semantic model as the photo-mode fallback when no palm is detected.
2. 1. Legal and consent first. Use a bilingual AR/EN consent form covering capture, ML training, commercial use, retention period and withdrawal; guardian consent for under-18s; no faces in frame. Store consent IDs with images. Check the Oman Personal Data Protection Law (Royal Decree 6/2022 and its executive regulations; UNVERIFIED details) on sensitive data such as biometrics and ethnicity-adjacent skin tone. Keep raw data off free cloud tools unless consent covers it.
3. 2. Capture protocol in Muscat salons (and staff homes for varied lighting). Each participant gives both hands. Poses: dorsal flat on a table, dorsal held up, fingers spread and closed, curled (salon 'claw' pose), thumb side view, slight palm tilt, plus 5-10 s video clips. Distance: whole hand filling 40-70% of the frame (palm detector needs the palm) plus some close-ups. Use the salon's actual customer phones and tablets, front and rear cameras.
4. 3. Stratification quotas (targets, adjust after pilot), per 1,000 images:
- MST: all 10 levels, at least 6% each, oversampling MST 6-10 and 1-2 where models fail most (the model card shows Fitzpatrick 1 and 6 worst).
- Henna: at least 15% (fresh orange, mature brown, black henna; on the back of the hand, on fingers, on nails).
- Extensions: at least 15% (gel, acrylic, press-on; short to XL; coffin, stiletto, almond).
- French tips: at least 8%.
- Existing polish: at least 35% (creme dark / light / nude, sheer, glitter, chrome, cat-eye, nail art, chipped).
- Bare natural nails: at least 25%, incl. bitten, ridged, short.
- Kids 6-12: about 5%, consent permitting. Ages 60+: at least 8%. Male hands: at least 10%.
- Accessories: rings and bracelets in at least 20%.
- Negatives: gloves and objects.
5. 4. Lighting and device matrix. Salon LED (neutral 4000-5000 K), cool 6500 K, warm 2700-3000 K home light, window daylight, mixed, low light (at least 10%), strong backlight, ring light. At least 10 phone models: several iPhone generations, Samsung Galaxy A and S (both Snapdragon and Exynos), Xiaomi / Redmi, Huawei / Honor, Oppo / Realme / Vivo. Record EXIF and device metadata. Optionally capture a neutral-grey card in 10% of frames to build a color-constancy test set for skin tone.
6. 5. Dataset size and splits. Aim for 2,500-4,000 still images plus about 200 videos (sample 1 frame every 0.5-1 s), from 300+ unique participants. Split by participant (never by image): 70/15/15. Hold out 2 salons and 2 phone models entirely as an out-of-distribution test. Build a 400-600 image golden test set double-annotated by two people; inter-annotator IoU sets the realistic accuracy ceiling.
7. 6. Labeling workflow. Self-host Label Studio + ML backend (segment_anything_2_image / _video) or CVAT with the SAM interactor. Pre-label automatically with MediaPipe landmarks as point prompts to SAM 2.1, or the SAM 3 text prompt 'fingernail', on a Colab or Kaggle GPU. Human annotators correct the polygons at 200-400% zoom, especially the cuticle line, lateral folds and the free-edge boundary. Add base and tip keypoints and attributes. QA: 10% re-review, reject masks with boundary error over 2 px. Budget about 100-200 annotator-hours for 15k-20k nails (estimate).
8. 7. Label definitions (write a bilingual guideline with examples). Nail plate means the visible plate where polish would go, from the cuticle / eponychium line to the free edge, including existing polish and art. The free-edge / extension class is the part beyond the hyponychium (white tip or artificial extension). Exclude cuticle skin, side skin, rings and henna on skin. Occluded parts are not drawn. Clear or transparent extensions are drawn by their outline.
9. 8. Augmentation, using albumentations 2.0.8 (MIT, pinned; NOT AlbumentationsX, which is AGPL) or torchvision.transforms.v2 / Kornia:
- Geometry: rotation plus or minus 25 degrees after crop normalisation, scale 0.8-1.25, perspective, elastic (small).
- Photometric: brightness, contrast, gamma, white-balance shifts simulating 2700-6500 K, ISO noise, JPEG quality 40-95, motion blur, defocus blur, sharpening halos.
- Domain-specific: synthetic re-polish (repaint GT nail regions with random Lab colors, finishes and glitter textures so the model learns shape, not color; include skin-like nudes and black), fake specular highlight stripes, finger occlusion cutouts, ring and jewelry copy-paste, henna-pattern overlays used sparingly (prefer real henna).
10. 9. Model training. U-Net or LRASPP with a MobileNetV3-Small/Large or EfficientNet-Lite encoder (ImageNet-pretrained, permissive). Loss: Dice + BCE / CE, or Lovasz-softmax, plus a boundary-weighted term (distance-transform weights near edges). Use Loss-Max-Pooling or OHEM for hard pixels, plus keypoint heatmap loss. AdamW, cosine schedule, 100-200 epochs on crops; mixed precision on a Colab / Kaggle T4. Then quantization-aware fine-tuning or post-training fp16 / int8 calibration on 500 representative crops.
11. 10. Export and runtime integration. litert-torch to .tflite, then MediaPipe image_segmenter MetadataWriter (labels + activation), load in the existing MediaPipe ImageSegmenter slot (VITE_NAIL_SEGMENTER_URL). Parallel path: ONNX to ORT-Web WebGPU, or LiteRT.js WebGPU, for A/B testing. Confirm numerical parity of TFLite vs PyTorch on 200 golden crops (mean abs diff of probabilities below 0.01).
12. 11. Metrics (computed per subgroup):
- nail-class IoU (not mean IoU including background)
- per-nail IoU distribution (median and 10th percentile)
- Boundary IoU (Cheng et al. 2021) and Boundary F1 / BF score (tolerance 2 px at crop resolution)
- per-nail recall and precision at IoU 0.5 and 0.75 (visible nails at least 24 px wide)
- free-edge class IoU
- direction angular error (median and 90th percentile, degrees)
- nail length / width error (%)
- temporal jitter on static-hand videos (edge RMS px, flicker rate)
- latency p50 / p95 and sustained fps after 5 minutes (thermal)
- model size and download time over a throttled 4G profile
- subgroup gaps across MST, henna, extensions, polish type, age group, lighting and device.
13. 12. Acceptance targets for v1 (proposed):
- nail IoU of 0.90 or more overall; 10th-percentile per-nail IoU of 0.80 or more
- Boundary F1 at 2 px of 0.85 or more; free-edge IoU of 0.75 or more
- per-nail recall at IoU 0.5 of 0.98 or more; fewer than 1 false nail per 100 frames
- median direction error under 5 degrees
- worst MST-group gap of 3 IoU points or less; henna and extension subgroups within 5 points
- at least 24 fps live on a 2023-class mid-range Android (Chrome, GPU), at least 30 fps on iPhone 12+ (Safari), at least 12 fps on low-end with frame skipping
- photo-mode render in 1.5 s or less
14. 13. Size budget for 4G. Nail model 1.5 MB or less (fp16 / int8). Total first-load AI payload about 10-12 MB compressed: MediaPipe vision WASM 3.94 MB gzipped + hand_landmarker 7.82 MB raw (5.82 MB gzipped) + nail model. Cache with the existing vite-plugin-pwa service worker. Lazily load optional extras (InteractiveSegmenter 30.5 MB, LLM) only on demand. Verify that Firebase Hosting actually compresses .task / .tflite responses (UNVERIFIED).
15. 14. Device benchmark lab. Use a fixed recorded test video plus 10 target devices. Log fps, p95 latency, delegate actually used (GPU vs CPU fallback), WebGPU availability (Safari 26+, Chrome Android 121+ with Android 12+), memory and thermal state. Run in CI with Playwright for desktop regressions and manually on phones each release.
16. 15. Iterate with active learning. Ship a salon-only 'report bad mask' button that saves (with consent) the frame and landmarks. Weekly: rank new frames by model uncertainty (low max-probability, mask / landmark disagreement), label the top 200, retrain, and re-run subgroup metrics before release.
17. 16. Skin-tone module validation. Collect 100+ participants photographed with a grey card under 3 lighting conditions. Compare uncalibrated vs calibrated ITA and hue-angle stability (standard deviation across lighting). Have 2-3 trained staff rate MST under daylight-balanced light as reference. Report agreement (weighted kappa) and show confidence in the UI.
18. 17. Documentation and governance. Keep a model card for our nail model (data composition, subgroup metrics, known failures such as henna, transparent extensions and heavy glitter), a dataset datasheet, and a license ledger for every dependency and weight file.

### Open-source projects & papers

- google-ai-edge/mediapipe (Hand Landmarker, ImageSegmenter, InteractiveSegmenter, metadata writers) (Apache-2.0) — On-device ML pipelines. Web package @mediapipe/tasks-vision 1.1.1 (2026-10-14; 1.2.0 RCs daily). Python metadata_writers/image_segmenter.py attaches the metadata required by the web ImageSegmenter. · https://github.com/google-ai-edge/mediapipe
- google-ai-edge/mediapipe-samples-web (Apache-2.0) — Browser demos for Hand Landmarker, Image Segmentation, Interactive Segmentation and others (TypeScript, Vite). · https://github.com/google-ai-edge/mediapipe-samples-web
- google-ai-edge/LiteRT (LiteRT.js under litert/js) (Apache-2.0) — TFLite successor; @litertjs/core 2.6.0 runs .tflite in the browser on WebGPU / WebNN / XNNPack-WASM. · https://github.com/google-ai-edge/LiteRT
- google-ai-edge/litert-torch (formerly ai-edge-torch) (Apache-2.0) — PyTorch to LiteRT (.tflite) converter (PyPI litert-torch 0.10.0, 2026-10-20). · https://github.com/google-ai-edge/litert-torch
- microsoft/onnxruntime (onnxruntime-web) (MIT) — ORT-Web 1.30.0 with WebGPU / WASM / WebNN / WebGL EPs; v1.31.0 native released 2026-10-09. · https://github.com/microsoft/onnxruntime
- qubvel-org/segmentation_models.pytorch (MIT) — 12 architectures (U-Net, U-Net++, FPN, DeepLabV3+, Segformer, DPT...) and 800+ encoders including timm. · https://github.com/qubvel-org/segmentation_models.pytorch
- pytorch/vision (LRASPP / DeepLabV3 MobileNetV3) (BSD-3-Clause (weights: 'may have their own licenses')) — lraspp_mobilenet_v3_large: 3.22M params, 57.9 mIoU COCO-VOC, 12.49 MB. deeplabv3_mobilenet_v3_large: 11.03M params, 60.3 mIoU, 42.3 MB. · https://github.com/pytorch/vision
- open-mmlab/mmdetection (RTMDet-Ins) (Apache-2.0) — RTMDet-Ins-tiny: 5.6M params, mask AP 35.4 COCO. · https://github.com/open-mmlab/mmdetection
- roboflow/rf-detr (Apache-2.0 for package and all Seg models (PML 1.0 for some detection sizes and rfdetr_plus)) — DETR-style real-time detection and segmentation (Seg-N to Seg-2XL). · https://github.com/roboflow/rf-detr
- ultralytics/ultralytics (YOLO26 / YOLO11 / YOLOv8 -seg) (AGPL-3.0 (weights in ultralytics/assets also AGPL-3.0) or paid Enterprise) — Best-in-class easy instance segmentation; tfjs export replaced by LiteRT as of 8.4.83. · https://github.com/ultralytics/ultralytics
- facebookresearch/sam2 (Apache-2.0) — Promptable image and video segmentation (tiny 38.9M to large 224.4M). · https://github.com/facebookresearch/sam2
- facebookresearch/sam3 (SAM License (royalty-free; trade-control restrictions)) — Text- or concept-prompted instance segmentation and tracking (848M params; SAM 3.1 released 2026-03-27). · https://github.com/facebookresearch/sam3
- ChaoningZhang/MobileSAM (Apache-2.0) — SAM with a 5M Tiny-ViT encoder (9.66M total), ONNX export. · https://github.com/ChaoningZhang/MobileSAM
- yformer/EfficientSAM (Apache-2.0) — Lightweight SAM (S / Ti) with ONNX encoder and decoder. · https://github.com/yformer/EfficientSAM
- IDEA-Research/Grounded-SAM-2 + GroundingDINO (Apache-2.0) — Text-prompted detect, then segment, then track. · https://github.com/IDEA-Research/Grounded-SAM-2
- cvat-ai/cvat (MIT) — Self-hosted annotation tool with a SAM serverless interactor (nuclio). · https://github.com/cvat-ai/cvat
- HumanSignal/label-studio + label-studio-ml-backend (Apache-2.0) — Annotation tool with ML backends: segment_anything_2_image, segment_anything_2_video, grounding_sam, grounding_dino. · https://github.com/HumanSignal/label-studio-ml-backend
- khiem-nguyen-ict/nail-try-on (README states MIT, but no LICENSE file found at the repo root (unclear)) — FastAPI server: MediaPipe Hands + Roboflow-hosted RF-DETR-seg-large nail polygons, one-to-one matching of nails to fingertips, HSV recolor with distance-transform gloss. About 2-3 FPS on a free cloud CPU; no accuracy metrics. · https://github.com/khiem-nguyen-ict/nail-try-on
- srivatsan-ramesh/Virtual-Makeup (MIT) — 2017 Python script applying lipstick, blush and nail polish (color and texture transfer) to images; 143 stars. · https://github.com/srivatsan-ramesh/Virtual-Makeup
- vinayaktiwariug20/magnetic-lacquer (MIT) — three.js / WebGL physics-based magnetic (cat-eye / velvet) polish preview: Kajiya-Kay anisotropic specular along flake chains, GGX + Fresnel clear coat. · https://github.com/vinayaktiwariug20/magnetic-lacquer
- clsoftlab-lang/single-use-nail-art (Apache-2.0 (docs CC BY 4.0)) — Static SVG hand preview with French / glitter / gems / matte overlays plus a rule-based shadeRecommend() (warm/cool + occasion + rating scoring). No camera or CV. · https://github.com/clsoftlab-lang/single-use-nail-art
- Phanabani/essie-hand-previewer (MIT) — Firefox extension selecting the Essie hand-preview image matching skin tone. · https://github.com/Phanabani/essie-hand-previewer
- Harlan-han/nailmind-ai-tryon-ops (No license file (all rights reserved by default)) — Nail try-on and operations platform using a RunningHub generative workflow + DeepSeek; no segmentation details. · https://github.com/Harlan-han/nailmind-ai-tryon-ops
- mlc-ai/web-llm (Apache-2.0) — In-browser LLMs on WebGPU (Qwen3 / 3.5, Gemma 2/3/4, Llama 3.2, Phi, SmolLM2). · https://github.com/mlc-ai/web-llm
- huggingface/transformers.js (Apache-2.0) — ONNX models in the browser (v4.3.1), including SAM and segmentation pipelines. · https://github.com/huggingface/transformers.js
- webmachinelearning/prompt-api (W3C CG (spec)) — Explainer for the browser LanguageModel API (multimodal input, structured output). · https://github.com/webmachinelearning/prompt-api
- ModiFace / L'Oreal and Perfect Corp YouCam Nails (commercial, closed) (Proprietary (possible patents; UNVERIFIED)) — Production nail try-on references. · https://arxiv.org/abs/1906.02222
- _Nail Polish Try-On: Realtime Semantic Segmentation of Small Objects for Native and Browser Smartphone AR Applications (Duke, Ahmed, Phung, Kezele, Aarabi - ModiFace; CVPR 2019 Workshop on CV for AR/VR)_ — All from memory, UNVERIFIED: arXiv was unreachable this session. Client-side real-time fingernail semantic segmentation for native (iOS) and browser (TensorFlow.js) apps. A cascaded multi-resolution encoder-decoder (MobileNetV2-style encoder on high- and low-resolution inputs, fused like ICNet) whose input resolution and depth can be tuned for a smooth accuracy-runtime trade-off. Loss Max-Pooling style loss for class imbalance on small objects. Predicts a fingernail base-to-tip direction field used by the postprocessing and rendering algorithm (gradient and gloss along the nail axis). Custom annotated fingernail dataset, not released. · UNVERIFIED, from memory of the abstract: highest setting 94.5 mIoU at 29.8 ms in native applications on an iPad Pro; lower settings run faster, including in-browser real time. Reusable ideas: small-object design (high resolution where nails are), direction prediction, imbalance-aware loss, render along the base-to-tip axis.
- _MediaPipe Hands: On-device Real-time Hand Tracking (Zhang, Bazarevsky, Vakunov et al., CVPR 2020 Workshop)_ — Two-stage pipeline: single-shot PALM detector (square anchors reduce anchor count 3-5x, encoder-decoder features, focal loss), then a 21-keypoint 2.5D/3D landmark regressor on the crop. Trained on about 30K real annotated images plus synthetic renders; tracking reuses the previous landmarks to skip detection. · Palm detector 95.7% average precision vs 86.22% for the baseline (verified in the MediaPipe docs on GitHub). Model card: full model MNAE 5.08% (skin types), 10.09% (regions).
- _Searching for MobileNetV3 (Howard et al., ICCV 2019) - introduces the LR-ASPP segmentation head_ — Hardware-aware NAS mobile backbone plus the Lite Reduced ASPP decoder for efficient semantic segmentation. · torchvision LRASPP-MobileNetV3-Large: 3.22M params, 57.9 mIoU on COCO-VOC labels (verified in the torchvision source).
- _U-Net: Convolutional Networks for Biomedical Image Segmentation (Ronneberger et al., 2015)_ — Encoder-decoder with skip connections; precise boundaries from little data. A natural fit for per-nail crop segmentation. · Classic baseline; works with small datasets plus heavy augmentation.
- _ICNet for Real-Time Semantic Segmentation on High-Resolution Images (Zhao et al., ECCV 2018)_ — Cascade of low / medium / high-resolution branches fused by cascade feature fusion, combining context from low resolution with detail from high resolution. · Basis for the cascade idea used for small objects such as nails (UNVERIFIED that ModiFace cites it directly).
- _Loss Max-Pooling for Semantic Image Segmentation (Rota Bulo, Neuhold, Kontschieder, CVPR 2017)_ — Adaptive re-weighting of the per-pixel loss to focus on poorly predicted (often small or rare-class) pixels. · Improves under-represented classes, relevant to small nails vs large background.
- _The Lovasz-Softmax loss: A tractable surrogate for the optimization of the intersection-over-union measure (Berman et al., CVPR 2018)_ — Directly optimises IoU (Jaccard), useful when the metric is per-nail IoU. · Consistent IoU gains over cross-entropy in the paper's benchmarks.
- _Boundary IoU: Improving Object-Centric Image Segmentation Evaluation (Cheng, Girshick, Dollar, Berg, Kirillov, CVPR 2021)_ — IoU computed only within a band around contours; sensitive to boundary quality independent of object size. · Recommended metric for cuticle and sidewall edge quality of nails.
- _What is a good evaluation measure for semantic segmentation? (Csurka, Larlus, Perronnin, BMVC 2013) - BF score_ — Boundary F1 with a distance tolerance; complements region IoU. · Use BF at a 2 px tolerance at crop resolution as an acceptance metric.
- _YOLACT: Real-time Instance Segmentation (Bolya et al., ICCV 2019)_ — Prototype masks plus per-instance coefficients. · Real-time on desktop GPUs; MIT license; too heavy for phone browsers with ResNet backbones.
- _RTMDet: An Empirical Study of Designing Real-Time Object Detectors (Lyu et al., 2022) - RTMDet-Ins_ — Real-time one-stage detector with a dynamic-kernel instance-mask head. · RTMDet-Ins-tiny: 5.6M params, COCO mask AP 35.4 at 640 px (verified in the MMDetection README).
- _SAM 2: Segment Anything in Images and Videos (Ravi et al., 2024)_ — Promptable segmentation with streaming memory for video propagation. · SAM2.1-tiny 38.9M params, 91.2 FPS (A100), SA-V J&F 76.5 (verified in the repo README). Use it for labeling.
- _SAM 3: Segment Anything with Concepts (Meta, Nov 2025; SAM 3.1 March 2026)_ — Detector plus tracker sharing a vision encoder; text or exemplar 'concept' prompts return all matching instances. · 848M parameters (verified in the README); suitable for auto-labeling 'fingernail' on a GPU.
- _SegFormer: Simple and Efficient Design for Semantic Segmentation with Transformers (Xie et al., 2021)_ — Hierarchical Mix Transformer encoder plus an all-MLP decoder. · Strong accuracy and efficiency, BUT the official NVIDIA code and weights are non-commercial (verified LICENSE).
- _1 Euro Filter: A Simple Speed-based Low-pass Filter for Noisy Input in Interactive Systems (Casiez, Roussel, Vogel, CHI 2012)_ — Adaptive low-pass filter: low cutoff when slow (less jitter), higher when fast (less lag). · Standard for stabilising landmarks; already used in src/ai/oneEuro.js.
- _Skin colour typology and suntanning pathways (Chardon, Cretois, Hourseau, Int. J. Cosmetic Science 1991) - Individual Typology Angle (ITA)_ — ITA = atan((L* - 50) / b*) in degrees from CIELAB, classifying skin from very light to dark. · Bins: above 55 very light, 41-55 light, 28-41 intermediate, 10-28 tan, -30 to 10 brown, below -30 dark (standard literature values; quoted from memory).
- _Monk Skin Tone (MST) Scale (Ellis Monk; Google, 2022)_ — 10-point skin-tone scale designed to be more inclusive than Fitzpatrick for ML fairness evaluation. · Use as the stratification and reporting axis for dataset and fairness metrics (license details UNVERIFIED this session).
- _GHUM & GHUML: Generative 3D Human Shape and Articulated Pose Models (Xu et al., CVPR 2020)_ — Articulated 3D human body and hand model; MediaPipe world landmarks and screen z values are derived from GHUM fits on synthetic data. · Explains why the world z value is approximate; model available via request form.
- _11K Hands: Gender recognition and biometric identification using a large dataset of hand images (Afifi, MTAP 2019)_ — Two-stream CNN on dorsal and palmar hand images with metadata (age, skin color, nail polish, accessories). · Dataset with skin-color, age and nail-polish metadata; useful for internal evaluation, unclear for commercial training.

## Rendering realism

PHOTO-REALISTIC NAIL POLISH RENDERING IN THE BROWSER: research findings (2026-10-24).

RESEARCH CONDITIONS: The shared web-search budget for this workflow turn was already used up (the WebSearch tool refused every query). WebFetch could not resolve any host. Through the session proxy only GitHub (raw.githubusercontent.com) and the npm registry were reachable; MDN, W3C.org, arXiv, DOI resolvers, vendor sites and Wikipedia were all blocked. So the facts here were checked against PRIMARY SOURCE CODE AND DATA rather than web pages:
- WebKit IDL and AVFoundation capture source
- Chromium Android Camera2 capture source
- The W3C Image Capture spec source (gh-pages branch)
- MDN browser-compat-data v8.1.7 (raw data.json)
- The Khronos WebGL spec source
- Google Filament PBR docs
- MediaPipe source and type definitions
- three.js source
- npm registry license metadata
- Bundle sizes I measured locally (esbuild 0.28.2 + gzip -9)
- A test I ran of culori's CIEDE2000 against Sharma reference pairs
Paper citations, ΔE threshold literature and the commercial context come from domain knowledge. Each is marked "(not re-verified this session)". If you want the paper links checked, send a follow-up message to continue the web search, or raise CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_WEB_SEARCHES_PER_SESSION.

CORE ARCHITECTURE RECOMMENDATION:
- Treat realism as a RELIGHTING problem in camera space, not as painting a color.
- For each nail, decompose the real bare nail into four parts: low-frequency shading, high-frequency detail (ridges), specular highlights, and nail-plate albedo.
- Replace the albedo with the polish's camera-referred reflectance, using a Kubelka-Munk coverage model (sheer → opaque by coat count).
- Re-apply the real shading, plus a reduced share of the detail (polish levels ridges).
- Reuse the real highlight positions; they are physically correct for free. Then add finish-specific specular layers (GGX dielectric, clearcoat, anisotropic metal, thin-film, diffraction, flakes) on an analytic cylinder/ellipsoid normal map in a per-nail coordinate frame. That frame is built from MediaPipe Hand Landmarker (Apache-2.0) DIP→TIP axes plus PCA of a custom nail-segmentation mask.
- Do all math in linear light on half-float buffers. Encode to sRGB, or to Display-P3 where supported.

THE BIGGEST ACCURACY LEVER IS CAMERA COLOR CALIBRATION, not the shader:
- A printed card with ArUco markers and spectro-measured patches (or a Calibrite ColorChecker) lets you fit a root-polynomial camera→XYZ matrix. You then render the polish as THIS camera would record it under THIS light.
- On Android Chrome you can lock white balance (AWB lock), exposure (AE lock), exposure compensation, ISO and preset color temperatures. Verified in Chromium VideoCaptureCamera2.java; colorTemperature maps to 7 AWB presets only (2850–7000 K).
- On iOS/iPadOS Safari, WebKit exposes ONLY whiteBalanceMode (manual = AVCaptureWhiteBalanceModeLocked, single-shot, continuous), plus zoom, torch, backgroundBlur and powerEfficient. exposureMode, colorTemperature, exposureCompensation, ISO and focusMode are explicit FIXMEs in WebKit's MediaTrackConstraints.idl. So iOS needs per-frame software correction from the card or a tracked reference.

RENDERER CHOICE:
- Use raw WebGL2 (Safari iOS 15+, Chrome Android 58+) with a tiny helper: twgl.js 24.7 KB gz (MIT) or OGL 13.9 KB gz (Unlicense).
- Port the needed GLSL from three.js (MIT): GGX, Kelemen clearcoat, Charlie sheen, Belcour thin-film iridescence, LTC area lights, SH irradiance.
- A minimal three.js import is about 132 KB gz and three/webgpu about 297 KB gz (measured).
- WebGPU now ships in Safari 26 and Chrome Android 121+, but it is not needed and is less battle-tested on older iPhones.

TEMPORAL STABILITY:
- requestVideoFrameCallback timestamps (Safari 15.4+, Chrome 83+).
- One-Euro filtering of scale-normalized landmarks (Casiez 2012). MediaPipe's own pose filter uses min_cutoff 0.05, beta 80, d_cutoff 1.0.
- Landmark-driven warping of the previous mask, with hysteresis.
- Glitter, cat-eye and holo effects anchored in nail-local UV and driven by smoothed orientation.
- Composite onto the exact analyzed frame, never the live <video>.
- HD mode uses ImageCapture.takePhoto (Chrome Android 60+, Safari iOS 18.4+; WebKit encodes JPEG at quality 1.0 at the format's max photo dimensions). Re-segment and re-calibrate on the still, because ISP processing differs from the video stream. Share via the Web Share API with files (Safari iOS 14+, Chrome Android 76+).

VALIDATION:
- Prove accuracy in the IMAGE domain: same device, same light, same hand position; app render on bare nails vs a photo of the real manicure, with the card in frame.
- Compute per-nail ΔE00 on highlight-excluded cores. Targets: median ≤2.0, p95 ≤3.0, stretch ≤1.0–1.5. ΔE00 ≈1 is roughly a just-noticeable difference side by side; dental-colorimetry 50:50 thresholds are 0.8 perceptibility and 1.8 acceptability (not re-verified this session).
- Run a 2AFC real-vs-render perception test with salon staff and customers across Monk skin tones; pass if detection is not significantly above chance.
- Verified pitfall: culori's differenceCiede2000 silently converts its inputs to D65 Lab. Passing D50 Lab gave 1.8566 instead of the reference 2.0425 on Sharma pair 1. With lab65 inputs it matched all 5 reference pairs I tested exactly.

- **1. Camera acquisition** — getUserMedia rear camera, 1280x720 or 1920x1080 @30fps; capability probing via track.getCapabilities()/getSettings(); per-platform control plan: Request {video:{facingMode:'environment', width:{ideal:1920}, height:{ideal:1080}, frameRate:{ideal:30}}}. On Android Chrome, after a 1-2 s convergence (ideally with the calibration card/gray mat in view), apply {advanced:[{whiteBalanceMode:'manual'},{exposureMode:'manual'}]}, optionally exposureCompensation slightly negative (e.g. -0.3 to -0.7 EV) so the specular highlights on nails do not clip. Chromium implements whiteBalanceMode 'manual' as CONTROL_AWB_LOCK=true and exposureMode 'manual' as AE lock (verified in VideoCaptureCamera2.java / video_capture_device_android.cc). On iOS Safari only whiteBalanceMode (manual=locked/single-shot/continuous), zoom, torch, backgroundBlur and powerEfficient exist (WebKit IDL), so exposure drifts; compensate in software (step 10). Avoid backgroundBlur (it alters the frame). Never rely on getSupportedConstraints() alone; read getCapabilities() because support depends on the physical camera. Front camera (selfie) is supported but mirrored and lower quality; prefer rear camera for salon tablets.
- **2. Frame timing & synchronization** — HTMLVideoElement.requestVideoFrameCallback (Safari 15.4+, Chrome 83+) or MediaStreamTrackProcessor (Chrome 94+ window-exposed, Safari 18+) to get each decoded frame exactly once with mediaTime/presentationTime: Upload that exact frame into a WebGL2 texture, run inference on it, and composite the polish onto THAT texture, i.e. draw the analyzed frame, not the live <video> element underneath. This removes the 1-3 frame 'polish lags behind the nail' artifact. Pass the frame timestamps to the One-Euro filters as dt. Read VideoFrame.colorSpace (Safari/Chrome support verified in BCD) at startup to log primaries/transfer/matrix per device. Uncertain whether any iPhone delivers P3 video via getUserMedia; assume BT.709/sRGB until logged.
- **3. Hand landmarks** — MediaPipe Tasks Vision HandLandmarker (Apache-2.0, @mediapipe/tasks-vision 1.1.1), runningMode VIDEO, GPU delegate: Gives 21 normalized landmarks (x,y in [0,1], z relative depth with roughly the same scale as x) and worldLandmarks in meters (from vision.d.ts). Per finger use TIP (4,8,12,16,20), DIP (3,7,11,15,19), PIP (2/6/10/14/18). Palm normal from wrist(0), index MCP(5), pinky MCP(17) gives the hand's 3D orientation for lighting/flake response. Handedness tells which side is the dorsal (nail) side. Run landmarks every frame (cheap) and segmentation every frame or at a reduced rate (step 9).
- **4. Nail segmentation** — Custom small segmentation network (e.g. MobileNet/EfficientNet-lite U-Net or DeepLab-lite style), trained on nail datasets incl. long/extended nails and deep skin tones; run through MediaPipe ImageSegmenter with a custom .tflite (GPU delegate) or ONNX Runtime Web (MIT) / TF.js (Apache-2.0): Feed per-hand or per-finger crops (rotated so the finger axis is vertical, e.g. 256x256 per hand or 128x128 per fingertip). That gives higher effective resolution on small nails, which matters because the ModiFace nail try-on paper framed nails as a 'small-object segmentation' problem (not re-verified this session). Output a soft probability mask, not a hard category. Ideal outputs: (a) nail-plate probability, (b) optional free-edge class, (c) optional base-to-tip direction field. MediaPipe ImageSegmenter returns confidenceMasks as MPMask, and MPMask.getAsWebGLTexture() keeps the mask on the GPU if you pass the same canvas in the task options (verified in vision.d.ts). That gives zero CPU readback.
- **5. Instance assignment & per-nail local frame** — Connected components / mask peaks assigned to the nearest fingertip (TIP-DIP segment); per-nail frame from landmarks refined by PCA of mask pixels: Axis v (along finger, cuticle→tip) = normalize(TIP − DIP) in 2D, blended (e.g. 50/50) with the mask's principal axis to correct landmark error. u = perpendicular (across nail). Origin = mask centroid. Compute nail length L and width W along the axes, and normalized coords (u∈[-1,1], v∈[0,1]) for every pixel. Fit a smooth outline: a superellipse/spline for the sidewalls and free edge, a parabola for the cuticle/proximal fold. Store per-nail 3D orientation: finger direction from worldLandmarks DIP→TIP plus the dorsal normal from palm normal and finger roll. This frame anchors everything (normal map, French smile line, gradients, flakes).
- **6. Mask refinement (edge accuracy)** — Guided-filter / joint-bilateral upsampling of the low-res probability using the full-res frame luminance as guide (He et al. guided filter; fast guided filter), then small morphological cleanup: Upsample the probability to frame resolution with a guided filter (radius ≈ 4-8 px at 1080p, eps ≈ 1e-3 to 1e-4 on [0,1] luminance) so the edge snaps to the real nail boundary (nail plate vs cuticle skin has strong luminance/chroma edges). Then: fill holes, remove specks below 2% of the nail area, and smooth the outline. Edge feather = 1-2 px at 1080p (≈0.5-1% of nail width). Wider feathers create a visible halo. Compute alpha in linear space and composite linear colors.
- **7. Manicure-realistic shaping: cuticle gap, sidewalls, free edge** — Shape operators in nail-local coordinates: Cuticle gap: shift the proximal boundary distally by ~2-4% of nail length (≈0.3-0.6 mm, user/technician adjustable; real manicures leave a hairline gap). Give it a slightly sharper feather than the sidewalls. Sidewall inset: 0-1.5% of width. Free edge: polish 'caps' the tip, so include the free-edge region (the white distal part) in the mask. Optionally add a 1-px slightly darker/brighter rim at the distal edge (wrapped-tip thickness). Near the proximal fold, apply a subtle ambient-occlusion darkening (≈5-10%) where skin overhangs the plate.
- **8. Occlusion between fingers / thumb** — Segmentation-native occlusion plus landmark depth ordering: If masks from two fingers overlap, keep the nail whose TIP has smaller z (closer to camera) in MediaPipe landmarks. Mask out skin pixels of an occluding finger: the segmentation already excludes them if trained with overlaps; add a skin-probability gate (YCbCr/HSV skin heuristic within the bounding box) as a safety net. Hide a nail entirely when landmark visibility/presence is low or mask area collapses (<30% of its tracked area) to avoid ghost polish.
- **9. Temporal mask propagation** — Warp previous mask by a per-nail similarity/affine transform estimated from smoothed TIP/DIP/PIP landmarks; blend with new prediction; hysteresis thresholds: p_t = α·warp(p_{t-1}) + (1−α)·p_new, with α≈0.5-0.7 when motion is small and α→0.2 when the IoU between warped and new masks drops (fast motion / re-detection). Binarize with hysteresis (turn on at 0.55, off at 0.45) before feathering. If segmentation runs at 15 fps and landmarks at 30 fps, propagate the last mask with the landmark transform on in-between frames.
- **10. Linearization and camera color correction** — sRGB EOTF decode to linear (use SRGB8_ALPHA8 textures or a shader decode), then a camera→XYZ (or camera→reference-linear-sRGB) matrix fitted from a calibration card; root-polynomial regression (Finlayson 2015) for exposure invariance: Card detection: ArUco markers (js-aruco2, MIT) at the card corners → homography → sample patch centers (inner 50% of each patch, median). Fit a 3x3 linear or 3x6/3x13 root-polynomial matrix minimizing ΔE00 (or least squares in linear RGB first, then refine). Keep the INVERSE mapping: polish reflectance (spectro-measured Lab/XYZ) → camera-linear RGB as this camera would see it under this illuminant (white patch = illuminant estimate). That is the polish albedo used in rendering. On iOS, where exposure cannot be locked, re-estimate a scalar exposure gain each frame from a tracked reference: the card if still visible, otherwise skin luminance around the nails relative to its calibrated value (smoothed, τ≈0.5 s). Without a card, fall back to gray-world/white-patch constrained to the daylight/LED locus and warn in the UI that color accuracy is reduced.
- **11. Intrinsic decomposition of the bare nail** — Per-nail luminance-ratio shading + detail + highlight separation (dichromatic model: specular has the illuminant color, body reflection has the nail color): Within the nail mask (linear RGB): (a) Highlight mask: pixels with luminance > mean + k·σ (k≈1.5-2) AND low chroma relative to the illuminant color, or near clipping (≥0.95). Store highlight intensity/shape H(x). (b) Inpaint highlight pixels (push-pull or a few iterations of masked Gaussian diffusion) to get diffuse-only luminance Ld. (c) Low-frequency shading S = blur(Ld)/median(Ld) (guided blur, radius ≈ 10-15% of nail width). (d) Detail D = Ld / blur(Ld), clamped to [0.85, 1.15]; ridges, lunula and free-edge contrast live here. (e) Bare-nail albedo Rg = Ld/S for the Kubelka-Munk sheer model (step 15). For opaque finishes use only S, with D raised to a finish-dependent power k (0.05-0.3). For sheer finishes the real nail (lunula, free edge, pigment streaks) correctly shows through via Rg.
- **12. Lighting estimation from the frame** — (1) Key-light direction from existing nail highlights; (2) 2nd-order spherical harmonics (9 coefficients, Ramamoorthi-Hanrahan) fitted to skin shading with approximate cylinder normals of the fingers; (3) light color from highlights + card white; (4) exponential smoothing: (1) With the analytic nail normal map (step 14) and view vector V≈(0,0,1), each detected highlight centroid gives a normal N_h, so L = reflect(−V, N_h). Average over nails, weighted by highlight confidence. The highlight's elongation along the nail suggests an area light (window/ceiling panel), so estimate its angular size from highlight width. (2) Fit SH to skin luminance around the fingers (normals from finger-cylinder model: axis from landmarks, radius from mask width), dividing out median skin albedo. Solve 9×N least squares in linear space; regularize the L2 band. (3) Illuminant chroma = chroma of highlight pixels (specular takes the light's color) cross-checked with the card's white/gray patches. (4) Smooth light dir/SH/color with an EMA (τ≈0.5-1 s), and reset on large scene changes. Optional Android-only: WebXR Lighting Estimation (XRLightEstimate.sphericalHarmonicsCoefficients/primaryLightDirection; Chrome Android 90+, experimental, not in Safari) requires an immersive-ar session and is not usable in the normal getUserMedia flow.
- **13. Environment map for reflections (chrome/metallic/gel)** — Camera feed as a pseudo-environment: downsampled, mirrored, blurred mip pyramid + synthetic HDR light strips aligned to the estimated key light: Downsample the current frame to ~128x128, build a blur pyramid (e.g. 5 levels of Gaussian/Kawase) for roughness-based lookup. Map reflection vector R to UV with a simple hemispherical projection (uv = 0.5 + 0.5·R.xy·s). Mirror horizontally, because the rear camera sees what lies behind the hand while the nail reflects what is in front of it (toward the viewer/ceiling), so the feed is only a plausible proxy. Blend in a synthetic HDR 'softbox' (a rectangle of radiance 3-10× the scene's mean, at the estimated key-light direction) plus a dim ceiling gradient. Chrome nails in real photos mostly show one or two bright light-strip reflections over darker surroundings. Keep env values HDR in RGBA16F until the final tone map.
- **14. Nail normal map (curvature)** — Analytic normals in nail-local frame: transverse C-curve (cylinder) + mild longitudinal curvature (ellipsoid), rotated by the finger's 3D orientation: n_local = normalize(sin(θu)·û + sin(φv)·v̂ + cos(θu)·cos(φv)·ẑ), with θu = u·θmax (θmax ≈ 35-55°; larger for pinched/sculpted nails, smaller for flat nails), φv = (v − v_apex)·φmax (φmax ≈ 10-20°, v_apex ≈ 0.4). Rotate into camera space using the finger direction and dorsal normal from worldLandmarks (smoothed). Add the D-derived ridge normals at small strength for sheer/natural looks (bump from the detail map), and almost none for gel. The cylinder normal makes highlights elongate along the nail and adds Fresnel brightening at the sidewalls, both of which read as 'real'.
- **15. Base color & coverage (sheer ↔ opaque)** — Kubelka-Munk two-flux model over the nail plate (finite layer over background), evaluated per channel in linear camera RGB (or spectrally for the reference catalogue): For each polish store K/S (or R∞ for opaque) and a 'hiding' parameter per coat (S·X). Reflectance over background Rg: with a = 1 + K/S and b = √(a² − 1), R = (1 − Rg·(a − b·coth(bSX))) / (a − Rg + b·coth(bSX)) (Kubelka 1948 finite-layer solution). Coats = 1/2/3 multiply X. Opaque crème: S·X large, so R→R∞, independent of the nail. Jelly/sheer: small S, low hiding, so the nail detail shows through. Derive K/S, S·X from two spectro/drawdown measurements of the real polish: one coat over white and over black cards, or a swatch stick photographed over a black/white checker. spectral.js (MIT) implements single-constant KM mixing from RGB if spectral data are unavailable. Mixbox is CC-BY-NC-4.0: do not ship it commercially. Final diffuse = R · S_low · D^k · (1 − F_spec) for energy conservation.
- **16. Specular layers** — Cook-Torrance GGX (Filament/three.js formulation) for the polish surface; separate clearcoat lobe (GGX + Kelemen visibility + Schlick Fresnel, F0=0.04, IOR≈1.5) for gel/top coat; LTC area-light (rectangular) evaluation for the key light; SH for ambient: Dielectric polish F0 = 0.04. Use perceptualRoughness² as α. Clamp perceptual roughness ≥ 0.089 when computing in mediump/half precision (Filament guidance). Attenuate the base by (1 − Fc) under clearcoat. LTC tables (selfshadow/ltc_code, used by three.js RectAreaLight) give correctly elongated window-shaped highlights on the cylinder. Combine: synthesized highlight + 'reused real highlight' H(x) (sharpened via pow 1.5-3 and scaled by the finish's gloss ratio) for glossy finishes; suppress H(x) for matte. Highlight color = estimated illuminant color, never white by default.
- **17. Finish-specific shading** — Per-finish shader branches/uniform sets (see finish_recipes), all parameterized in nail-local UV so effects stick to the nail: Flake fields (shimmer, glitter, holo, cat-eye) use deterministic per-nail seeds and cellular noise in (u,v) scaled by real nail size in mm (nail width W_px / assumed ~10-14 mm adult index/middle nail; uncertain, better to calibrate with the card's known size). Pre-integrate each effect's average contribution into the diffuse albedo so the nail's AVERAGE color still matches the measured bottle color (key for the ΔE test).
- **18. Compositing & anti-aliasing** — Premultiplied-alpha linear compositing; analytic edge AA via the feathered mask; optional 2x supersampling in HD mode: out = lerp(frame_lin, polish_lin, α). Do not blend in sRGB (darkens edges). Clamp/soft-knee highlights, and tone map only the HDR polish highlights (e.g. Reinhard on values > 0.8) so the untouched camera pixels stay bit-identical outside the nail. Add a micro contact-shadow under extended free edges in 'extension' mode.
- **19. Output encoding & wide gamut** — Render to RGBA16F (EXT_color_buffer_half_float / EXT_color_buffer_float: Safari iOS 14/15+, Chrome Android 63/56+); final pass encodes sRGB or Display-P3 (drawingBufferColorSpace='display-p3': Chrome 104+, Safari 16.4+): If matchMedia('(color-gamut: p3)') is true and the polish is outside sRGB (neons, saturated reds/magentas/cyans), set gl.drawingBufferColorSpace='display-p3' and convert linear sRGB→linear P3 with [[0.8225,0.1775,0],[0.0332,0.9668,0],[0.0171,0.0724,0.9105]] (computed from primaries) before applying the sRGB transfer curve (P3 uses the same curve). Safari lacks gl.unpackColorSpace (BCD), so video/textures arrive converted to sRGB. Note iPhone OES_texture_float_linear is iPadOS-only, so use half-float (filterable) not float32 for anything sampled with LINEAR filtering.
- **20. HD still mode (freeze, high-quality render, share)** — ImageCapture.takePhoto() (Chrome Android 60+, Safari iOS 18.4+) or grabFrame() (Safari 26+, Chrome 59+); fallback: the current video frame at native resolution: Freeze: keep the last good frame + masks on screen instantly, then replace with the full-res photo result. On the still: re-run landmarks and segmentation at higher res (per-nail 384-512 px crops), guided filter at full res, 2x supersampled edges, glitter with more samples, full-quality blur pyramid. Re-detect the card in the still and refit the color matrix, because the photo pipeline (JPEG, quality 1.0 in WebKit; ISP tone mapping) differs from the video stream. Export with canvas.toBlob('image/jpeg', 0.92) (WebP toBlob unsupported on Safari). Share via navigator.share({files}) (Safari iOS 14+, Chrome Android 76+), with a download fallback. Add before/after slider, polish name and brand/shade code watermark. Store nothing server-side by default (privacy).
- **21. Performance budget & quality tiers** — Adaptive tiers by measured frame time: Target 30 fps live on mid-range Android (e.g. Samsung A-series) and iPhone 11+ (estimates, verify by benchmarking): landmarks ~5-10 ms, segmentation ~8-20 ms GPU, render ≤ 4 ms. Tier down: segmentation every 2nd frame with propagation, half-res env pyramid, fewer flake layers, no LTC (use a GGX point light). Tier up in HD mode. Use OffscreenCanvas + worker for inference where available (OffscreenCanvas webgl2: Safari 17+, Chrome 69+).
- **22. Graceful fallbacks** — Feature detection + honest UI: No WebGL2: static photo mode with Canvas2D. No camera permission: upload a photo. No card: 'approximate color' badge. Low light / clipped highlights / motion blur: on-screen coaching ('move closer to light', 'hold still'). Too small nails (<40 px wide): ask user to move closer.

### Accuracy validation protocol

- Define the claim precisely: 'On the same device, in the same light, the app's virtual polish on the customer's bare nails matches a photo of the real manicure in that shade within ΔE00 X (mean color), and observers cannot reliably tell render from real.' On-screen vs physical-bottle comparisons are confounded by display calibration, so the PRIMARY test is image-vs-image in camera space.
- Ground truth catalogue: for every shade, measure dried swatches (manufacturer-recommended coats + top coat) with a spectrophotometer (D65/10° and D50/2° recorded; at least 3 readings averaged), plus one-coat-over-black/white for KM opacity. Record batch/lot. Multi-angle measurement (e.g. ASTM E2539-style geometries, not re-verified this session) for metallic/duochrome/pearl.
- Controlled capture rig: tripod or phone stand, hand rest on the 18% gray mat with printed card, fixed high-CRI light (≥90-95 CRI; test at ~5000-6500 K AND at the salon's real warm LED ~3000-4000 K). Lock WB (and AE on Android). Same device for both shots. Repeat on at least 3 devices (recent iPhone, older iPhone, mid-range Samsung Android, iPad).
- Protocol per subject/shade: (1) photograph bare nails (live render + HD render saved); (2) a technician applies the real polish exactly per brand instructions without moving the camera; (3) re-position the hand with the jig/markers and photograph the real manicure; (4) register the two images (landmarks + ArUco homography) so the same nail regions are compared.
- Metrics per nail: mean color of the 'core' region (central ~60% of nail area, excluding the top 5-10% luminance pixels = highlights and the 1-px edge band) in calibrated CIELAB (lab65) → ΔE00 render vs real. Also highlight statistics (peak luminance, highlight area fraction, position offset in mm), texture similarity on nail crops (SSIM and LPIPS), and edge accuracy (IoU and boundary F-score of painted region vs real polish region, cuticle-gap width difference in mm).
- Targets (proposed): crème/gel/jelly mean-color ΔE00 median ≤ 2.0 and 95th percentile ≤ 3.0 across devices and skin tones; stretch goal ≤ 1.0-1.5 under the reference light. Edge IoU ≥ 0.92 and boundary error ≤ 0.3 mm. Highlight position error ≤ 1 mm. Report separately per Monk Skin Tone band and per device so no group is worse than the median by more than 0.5 ΔE00.
- How to interpret ΔE00 (from literature; not re-verified this session): ≈1.0 is commonly treated as a just-noticeable difference for side-by-side viewing of uniform patches. Dental colorimetry consensus (Paravina et al. 2015) gives 50:50 perceptibility ≈0.8 and acceptability ≈1.8 ΔE00. CIELAB ΔE*ab JND ≈2.3 (Mahy et al. 1994). Values 2-3 are typically 'noticeable on close side-by-side inspection but commercially acceptable'. Above ~5 means clearly different. Effects on curved, glossy, textured nails mask small differences, so perceptual testing is the final arbiter.
- Effect finishes (glitter, chrome, holo, cat-eye, duochrome): mean ΔE is insufficient. Compare multi-frame sequences of the real and virtual nail while rotating the hand through ±30°: sparkle count per mm² above a brightness threshold, sparkle contrast, band position vs tilt angle (cat-eye), and hue-vs-angle curves (duochrome) against the measured LUT. Automotive sparkle/graininess metrics (e.g. BYK-mac-style, not verified) are a useful model.
- Perception test 1, real-or-render 2AFC: show randomized pairs or single crops (real manicure photo vs app render, same hand, same light) to ≥30 observers (mix of salon technicians and customers, GCC demographic, Arabic/English UI). Pass if accuracy is not significantly above 50% (binomial test, α=0.05; practical target ≤ 60%). Run per finish family.
- Perception test 2, bottle match: under the reference light booth, give the observer the real swatch stick/bottle and show 3-5 renders on a calibrated tablet (true shade + near neighbors at ΔE00 ≈ 2-4). Measure the % choosing the true shade, and the confusion between neighbors, to learn which shade spacing the app can communicate reliably.
- Perception test 3, realism MOS: 1-5 'how real does this look' ratings for live and HD renders vs real photos. Target MOS within 0.3 of the real photos' MOS.
- Customer-outcome test in salons: after the real manicure, ask 'did the result match what you saw in the app?' (5-point scale) and log mismatches with photos for root-cause analysis (calibration, lighting, model, catalogue data).
- Regression dataset & CI: store all paired captures (with consent) plus metadata. Every shader, model or catalogue change re-computes ΔE00/IoU/LPIPS automatically. Include unit tests of the ΔE implementation against Sharma 2005 reference pairs (verified this session with culori in lab65 mode).
- Calibration QA each session: report the card residual (mean ΔE00 over patches after fitting). If > ~2.5, or the card is not detected, disable the 'accurate color' badge and show 'approximate'.
- Fairness coverage: recruit hands across Monk Skin Tone 1-10 (emphasize MST 3-8, plausibly common in Oman/GCC; uncertain), different nail lengths/shapes, natural vs gel/acrylic extensions, older hands with ridges, and hands with henna (common in the GCC). Henna-stained nails/skin need explicit test cases.

### Pitfalls

- Halo / glow ring around the nail edge → Feather only 1-2 px at 1080p. Guided-filter upsampling so the edge snaps to the real boundary. Composite in linear premultiplied space. Erode the mask by ~0.5 px before feathering. Never blur the mask isotropically after upsampling.
- Color too saturated / 'digital' compared with the bottle → Use measured Lab → camera-referred RGB via the calibration matrix, not website hex. Apply the real shading S (which darkens) and the illuminant tint. Gamut-map in OKLCh. Validate with ΔE00 against real-manicure photos. Most phone cameras already boost saturation, so match the camera's rendering, not the colorimetric truth, for on-screen comparisons.
- Plastic / sticker look (flat uniform color) → Transfer low-frequency shading and a fraction of the ridge detail. Reuse and sharpen the real highlights. Add Fresnel rim at the sidewalls via the cylinder normal map. Add a subtle cuticle AO, a wrapped free-edge rim, and slight roughness noise.
- Jitter / swimming of polish and highlights → One-Euro filter on scale-normalized landmarks with requestVideoFrameCallback timestamps. Landmark-driven mask warping + hysteresis. Smooth lighting estimates. Composite onto the analyzed frame (not the live <video>). Anchor flakes in nail UV.
- Polish lags behind the nail when the hand moves → Draw the exact frame that was analyzed as the background (frame-synchronized compositing), and predict landmarks forward by the measured latency only if necessary. Keep One-Euro beta high enough for fast motion.
- Wrong/dark shading on deep skin tones → Normalize shading by the nail's own median, not skin. Use only blurred S for opaque finishes. Clamp the detail ratio. Fix exposure via gray mat, Android exposureCompensation or software gain. Include Monk Skin Tone 6-10 hands in training and validation sets.
- Natural nail streaks, white spots or lunula visible through 'opaque' crème → KM: opaque polish uses R∞ (no background term). Detail exponent ≤0.2. Inpaint strong local anomalies in the detail map for opaque finishes. Keep them only for sheer/jelly, where they are realistic.
- Long nails / extensions extend beyond the mask (model trained on short natural nails) → Train with long, extended, almond/stiletto/coffin nails and with free-edge as a class. Detect the free edge by a brightness/transparency change beyond the fingertip contour (TIP landmark ± margin). Allow the mask to extend along v beyond TIP when the segmentation supports it.
- Virtual shape change (short real nails, wants long almond) → Separate 'extension mode': parametric nail shapes (square, squoval, round, oval, almond, stiletto, coffin/ballerina, lipstick) in the local frame extending past the fingertip. Composite over background with a contact shadow and edge thickness rim. Make shorter-than-real impossible, or require inpainting the real free edge (later feature). Label it as a preview.
- Polish painted onto cuticle skin or sidewall skin → Cuticle-gap erosion (2-4% of length) at the proximal boundary. Skin-probability gate. Guided-filter edges. Per-salon adjustable gap slider for technicians.
- Occlusion errors (thumb crossing fingers, overlapping nails) → Segmentation trained on overlaps. Landmark z-ordering for overlapping masks. Hide nails with low visibility/presence. Area-collapse check against the tracked area.
- Glitter flashes randomly (temporal noise) instead of twinkling → Deterministic per-nail seeds in nail UV. Twinkle driven only by smoothed orientation (landmarks + DeviceOrientation), not by per-frame noise. Footprint-based LOD to remove aliasing.
- Shimmer/glitter aliasing (moiré, sparkle crawl) at small nail sizes → Filter flake density by pixel footprint (fwidth of UV). Fade cells smaller than a pixel into the pre-integrated average. Use stochastic glint BRDF approaches for sub-pixel flakes.
- Average color shifts when sparkle/flake effects are added → Pre-integrate each effect's mean contribution into the albedo and subtract it, so the nail's mean (highlight-excluded) color still equals the measured target.
- Chrome looks gray/flat → HDR pipeline (RGBA16F), HDR synthetic light strips, metallic F0≈0.91-0.95, very low roughness, and highlight tone mapping. Don't clamp the env map to [0,1] before shading.
- Highlights white when the room light is warm → Tint specular with the estimated illuminant color (highlight chroma + card). Never hard-code white.
- Color shifts between frames on iPhone (auto exposure cannot be locked) → Lock WB with whiteBalanceMode:'manual' after convergence. Per-frame software exposure gain from the card or a skin reference ring, smoothed. Show a 'hold steady / keep card in view' hint in accurate mode.
- Assuming colorTemperature works the same everywhere → Android maps it to 7 coarse AWB presets. iOS doesn't support it at all. Use software white balance from the card instead.
- Float32 textures not linearly filterable on iPhone → Use RGBA16F (half float, filterable in WebGL2) for anything sampled with LINEAR filtering. OES_texture_float_linear on iOS is iPadOS-only per BCD.
- GGX artifacts / NaNs / speckles on mobile GPUs at low roughness → Clamp perceptual roughness ≥0.089 when using half precision (Filament guidance). Use highp in fragment shaders where available. Guard divisions with max(·, 1e-4).
- ΔE results wrong because of white-point mismatch in the library → culori's differenceCiede2000 converts to D65 Lab. Feed lab65 or convert D50 spectro data explicitly (Bradford). Unit-test against Sharma 2005 reference pairs in CI.
- Blending in sRGB space (dark fringes, wrong gradients) → Linear workflow end to end. Gradients (ombré) interpolate in OKLab, then convert to linear for shading.
- Neon/out-of-gamut polishes clipped to wrong hue → Render to Display-P3 when the display supports it. Otherwise gamut-map in OKLCh. Disclose limits.
- HD photo looks different from live preview → Re-run segmentation/calibration on the still (different ISP path). Same shader parameters. Offer both live and HD outputs with consistent calibration.
- Mask flicker at boundaries (pixels toggling on/off) → Hysteresis thresholds (0.55 on / 0.45 off). Temporal EMA in a motion-compensated frame. Morphological cleanup of small islands.
- Wrong finger orientation flips the normal map (highlights on the wrong side) → Use handedness + palm normal to determine dorsal side. Use worldLandmarks for 3D finger direction. Smooth roll angle. Sanity-check that the predicted highlight side matches detected real highlights and flip if consistently contradicted.
- Thumb nail treated like other nails (it faces the camera at a different angle) → The thumb has its own 3D orientation (CMC/MCP/IP/TIP landmarks 1-4). Compute its frame separately. It is often seen more side-on, so increase sidewall Fresnel and adjust curvature.
- DeviceOrientation silently unavailable on iOS → Request permission from a tap with DeviceOrientationEvent.requestPermission() (Safari iOS 14.5+ per BCD), serve over HTTPS. Fall back to hand-rotation-driven effects when denied or on a fixed stand.
- Front-camera selfie mode mirrors and breaks handedness/orientation → Detect facingMode from getSettings(). Un-mirror coordinates consistently for landmarks, normals and DeviceOrientation mapping.
- Card printed on glossy or optically brightened paper → Matte, OBA-free stock. Measure each batch. Reject the card if its patch residual ΔE00 is high or glare is detected (patch variance check).
- Small nails in frame (hand too far) give poor masks and visible pixelation → Minimum nail width threshold (~40 px) with coaching UI. Per-finger crops for inference. HD mode for final images.
- Performance collapse on mid-range Android (thermal throttling) → Adaptive quality tiers, segmentation at reduced rate with propagation, avoid CPU readbacks (MPMask.getAsWebGLTexture with a shared canvas), cap at 30 fps.

## Skin tone science

RESEARCH CONDITIONS (read first). The shared WebSearch budget for this workflow turn (200 calls, shared by all agents) was already used up when this agent started, so no WebSearch could run. WebFetch could only reach github.com and raw.githubusercontent.com, and Bash could only reach PyPI and GitHub raw. Every other domain failed DNS: skintone.google, Wikipedia, PubMed/NCBI, arXiv, brand sites, Pantone and qanoon.om. So this report has two tiers of evidence. Tier one was checked this session from primary code or data: the MST hex codes (from the skin-tone-classifier package, which cites skintone.google), Sony's L*/hue-angle method and its thresholds, the Fitzpatrick17k ITA-vs-label results, MediaPipe's 21 landmarks and Apache-2.0 license, the licenses of the white-balance and color-constancy models, and W3C Image Capture support. Tier two comes from my prior knowledge (cutoff mid-2026) and is marked 'verify' in sources. That includes the ITA thresholds, Fitzpatrick history, MST's CC BY 4.0 license, Pantone colors, Oman law, Islamic-calendar dates and all brand or product hexes. A follow-up run with search budget should re-check tier two before launch. I also ran my own CIELAB computations: Lab, ITA and hue angle for every MST swatch, a white-balance and exposure sensitivity test, computed nude-ladder targets, and henna-stain Lab prototypes.

KEY CONCLUSIONS. (1) Use the Monk Skin Tone scale (10 levels, hexes #f6ede4 to #292420) as the customer-facing depth vocabulary. Never put Fitzpatrick in the UI: it measures UV burn/tan response, not color, and is health-adjacent. Use ITA° = atan2(L*-50, b*)·180/π internally as the continuous depth metric (Chardon 1991; six categories from Del Bino 2006). Use CIELAB hue angle h_ab = atan2(b*, a*) as the undertone axis (Sony ICCV 2023 shows apparent skin color is at least two-dimensional: L* plus hue). (2) MST hexes are display swatches made for human annotation, not colorimetric skin values. My Lab conversion shows MST 1-4 at L* 88-94 with C* only 6-18, far lighter and less saturated than spectrophotometer readings of real skin. Never compare raw camera Lab to MST hexes by nearest ΔE without calibration. (3) Accuracy close to 100% cannot come from an uncalibrated phone photo. In the Fitzpatrick17k notebook, ITA from uncalibrated photos agreed with expert Fitzpatrick labels within ±1 type only 45.8% of the time (52.97% with a YCbCr skin mask). In my simulation, a -0.5 EV exposure error moves ITA by 10-18° (about one whole ITA category), and an 8-12% residual color cast moves ITA by about 5° and chroma by about 20%. To be dependable you need: a neutral reference in the frame (a gray card at minimum, a 24-patch chart for best results), a fixed 5000-6500 K high-CRI 'match station' lamp, temporal median sampling from a landmark-defined patch on the back of the hand that leaves out henna, veins, knuckles, nails and specular highlights, measurement before soaking or massage, and a final confirmation by the user or staff on rendered swatches. (4) Undertone from photos is only moderately reliable even after calibration, and olive is the least reliable. The vein test has a weak physical basis (vein color comes from scattering and depth; Kienle 1996). The jewelry test is a preference question, and sun reaction measures melanin or UV sensitivity, not undertone. Best practice is to estimate hue angle, then confirm with side-by-side try-on comparisons (for example a warm red against a blue-red) and with physical swatch sticks in the salon. (5) Every depth × undertone rule for nails is industry consensus or opinion; I found no peer-reviewed validation. The parts that do rest on research are general vision science (simultaneous contrast) and color-harmony findings (Ou & Luo 2006). So recommendations should be a ranked shortlist with a 'why', never a verdict, and the photo-real try-on should decide. The nude rule (industry consensus) should be expressed in CIELAB, not 'shades': a nude about 4-10 L* lighter or 4-12 L* darker than measured skin, hue within about ±10°, chroma equal to skin or a little higher. Avoid nudes more than about 15-20 L* lighter on deep skin, because they read ashy. (6) Do not estimate age from hands. No validated hand-only estimator is known; face estimators tested by NIST still show multi-year errors that vary by demographic; the output is hurtful and ageist; and Oman's PDPL treats biometric data as sensitive. Use style persona, occasion and opt-in hand-concern toggles (for example 'soften the look of veins or sun spots') instead. (7) For GCC and Oman, the app needs henna-aware skin masking and rendering of sheer polish over henna-stained nails. It also needs 'wudu-friendly' filters worded strictly as manufacturer claims, never religious rulings (Oman is majority Ibadi; refer users to their own scholars). Plan for Ramadan and Eid peaks (Ramadan about 8 Feb 2027, Eid al-Fitr about 9-10 Mar 2027, Eid al-Adha about 16-17 May 2027; all subject to moon sighting), bridal gold and the henna night, workplace and clinical-hygiene limits, the extreme summer UV and heat, and on-device processing for privacy. (8) Licensing traps: Mahmoud Afifi's sRGB white-balance models (CVPR 2019, WACV 2022) are research-only, so not commercial. The Fitzpatrick17k dataset is CC BY-NC-SA 3.0, so not commercial. The skin-tone-classifier (stone) code is GPL-3.0, so do not bundle it in closed source; the hex values themselves are facts and can be reused. MediaPipe (Apache-2.0), Sony's apparent_skincolor code (Apache-2.0), FC4 (MIT) and FFCC (Apache-2.0, but it expects linear RAW input) are usable.

### Recommendation matrix

| Depth | Undertone | Best | Avoid | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| all | all | nudes #F8CFB9 #EFBBA0 #D99E7E #B97E5D #8B5A3E #5B3B28 | A nude so close to the skin (/ΔL*/ < 3 and ΔE00 < 5) that it reads as 'nothing on', unless that is the explicit goal. A nude more than about 15-20 L* lighter than skin on tan, deep or very deep hands, which reads chalky or ashy. A nude whose hue is more than 15° from the skin's (a pink nude on olive skin, or a yellow-beige nude on pink-cool skin), which reads sallow or 'band-aid'. | industry-consensus |
| all | all | blacks, whites, nudes #111111 #FFFFFF #F3EFE8 | Judging a polish only from a swatch on a white UI background. | research |
| all | all | reds, metallics #A6192E #B5121B #E0352B #C9A54C #C0C3C7 #C98F7A | Treating undertone rules as hard blocks. User preference, the occasion and the try-on result always win. | industry-consensus |
| all | uncertain (low-confidence measurement) | nudes, reds, berries, browns, whites, metallics #D99E7E #B5121B #7B1E2C #A47864 #F3EFE8 #E3CBA3 | Showing extreme-undertone shades (icy lilac, neon yellow, lime) as 'top picks' when the measurement is unsure. | opinion |
