Vinyl adds a slight edge ledge; water decals are invisible under a top coat. Metallic foil stickers reflect like foil. Sim: decal as an albedo plus metallic and roughness map in nail UV, warped to curvature, under the top coat. |
| Henna nail stain | حناء الأظافر | weeks, until grown out (fades gradually) | No coat: natural nail sheen, ridges and texture remain visible. Orange to red-brown, uneven, darker at margins, changing over 24–48 h (general knowledge). Sim: multiply-blend stain with no clearcoat. |

## Shapes & hand analysis

RESEARCH STATUS, PLEASE READ FIRST. When this agent started, this turn's shared web-search budget (200 calls across all agents) was already used up, so WebSearch returned nothing. WebFetch also failed: DNS for ai.google.dev, nhs.uk, aad.org and mayoclinic.org did not resolve. Through the session proxy, almost every general host was refused (403): Wikipedia, Allure, Vogue, OPI, Essie, CND, NAILS Magazine, DermNet, Cleveland Clinic, MedlinePlus, arXiv, NCBI/PubMed, MDPI, jsDelivr, unpkg, HuggingFace, Kaggle. Only raw.githubusercontent.com, registry.npmjs.org and storage.googleapis.com could be reached. What that means for each part of this answer:
(1) VERIFIED from primary sources this session: all MediaPipe facts. Sources were the HandLandmark enum in the source code, the official hands doc, the official Hand Tracking model card PDF (fairness and error tables, out-of-scope list), the @mediapipe/tasks-vision npm package typings and README, and the model files on Google storage.
(2) NOT re-verified this session: nail shape geometry, length bands, which shapes suit which hands, 2026 trend notes and the nail-condition wording. These come from model knowledge (cutoff mid-2026), meaning industry and educator consensus plus editorial coverage. Every field says how reliable it is. A follow-up run with web access should check the trend claims and the medical-referral wording against the NHS, AAD and Mayo pages listed in sources. No published standard defines nail-shape geometry numerically, so the parameter values below are engineering defaults built from salon teaching and should be tuned with Muscat technicians on real hands.

KEY FINDINGS
A) MediaPipe Hand Landmarker (verified). 21 landmarks: 0 WRIST; thumb 1 CMC, 2 MCP, 3 IP, 4 TIP; index 5 MCP, 6 PIP, 7 DIP, 8 TIP; middle 9, 10, 11, 12; ring 13, 14, 15, 16; pinky 17, 18, 19, 20. So the tips are 4, 8, 12, 16, 20, the DIPs are 7, 11, 15, 19, and the thumb uses its IP joint (3) in the DIP role.
- x and y are normalised to [0,1] by image width and height respectively. z is depth with the wrist as origin; smaller z means closer to the camera, and z is on roughly the same scale as x.
- worldLandmarks are in metres with the origin at the hand's approximate centre. The z values and world coordinates are estimated from synthetic GHUM-model data (model card), so treat them as coarse.
- Handedness assumes a mirrored, selfie-style image. Swap Left and Right for rear-camera photos.
- Accuracy (model card): Full model mean normalised absolute error is 10.09% of palm size (wrist to middle-finger MCP) on in-the-wild data from 14 regions, and 5.08% on the Fitzpatrick 1–6 set (range 4.53–6.21%, with no skin-tone error pattern found). The Lite model is 12.02% and 5.67%. Error is smallest at the finger bases and largest at the fingertips, and is worse with blur or occlusion.
- The model card lists jewelry, tattoos and HENNA as out-of-scope occlusions. Henna and rings are very common in Oman and the GCC, so the app needs low-confidence handling and should ask customers to remove rings.
- The landmarks give joint centres only. There is no finger width and no nail outline, so all nail metrics must come from the per-nail mask, and finger and palm widths need a hand mask. Free on-device options: (a) the MediaPipe selfie_multiclass_256x256 segmenter, whose labels I confirmed from the model file as background, hair, body-skin, face-skin, clothes, others; it is low resolution and not validated on hands. (b) MediaPipe InteractiveSegmenter, prompted with keypoints at landmark positions. (c) Seeded skin-colour segmentation.
- Current npm stable is @mediapipe/tasks-vision 1.1.1 (released 2026-10-14, Apache-2.0). 1.1.0 (2026-10-06) and 1.0.1 (2026-07-31) are older if a pinned version at least two weeks old is required.
- Decision: use landmarks for orientation, finger identity, pose and quality gating, and coarse proportions. Use the nail masks for nail width, length, cuticle and free edge. Take a median over 10–30 frames.

B) A single drawing model for every shape, used by every geometry field below.
Nail-local frame:
- Axis u points along the finger toward the tip: normalize(P_TIP − P_DIP), i.e. 8−7, 12−11, 16−15, 20−19, and 4−3 for the thumb. Refine it with the nail-mask principal axis when the two agree within about 10°. Axis v is perpendicular to u.
- The stress points SPL and SPR are where the lateral edges of the nail plate leave the skin (the distal end of the lateral nail folds, where the free edge begins). Origin O is their midpoint.
- W0 = |SPR − SPL|, the nail width at the stress points.
- Lb is the nail-bed length, measured along the centreline from the proximal mask edge at the cuticle to O.
- E is the extension (free-edge) length beyond O, set as E = k·Lb from the length table.
- The extension outline occupies u ∈ [0, E] and is unioned with the natural nail-bed mask.
Units: widths are fractions of W0. Taper angles θ are measured per side against the u-axis; positive means narrowing, negative means flaring.
The outline has two parts:
- (a) Sidewalls, from the stress points to the cap start u_c. Half-width h(u) = W0/2 for u < u_s, and W0/2 − (u − u_s)·tanθ after the taper start u_s.
- (b) A tip cap modelled as a superellipse (Lamé curve): (|v|/a)^p + ((u − u_c)/b)^p = 1, with a = h(u_c) and b = E − u_c. Guide values: p ≥ 12 gives a flat square tip, p = 4–5 squoval, p = 2 round or oval, p = 1.5–1.8 almond, and p = 1.0–1.15 gives the straight lines of a stiletto.
- Fillet corners with radius r (as % of W0) and blunt pointed tips with r_tip. Add a short fillet or Chaikin smoothing where (a) meets (b) so the outline is C1-continuous. For p > 1 the cap tangent at v = ±a is parallel to u, so it joins parallel sidewalls smoothly.
- Extend sidewalls along the FINGER axis rather than following the natural fan-out of the nail; technicians file to the finger axis.
- 'Apex' means the structural apex: the highest point of the arch, on the centreline, used to place highlights and the normal map. Default is about 40–45% of the visible nail length from the cuticle; for long and XL nails, move it toward the stress area.
- C-curve is the cross-section as a % of a circle: natural about 20–35%, typical enhancement about 40–50%, pipe 50% or more. It drives how wide the specular highlight band is.
- Keep the same relative parameters (Wt/W0, p, r%) on all ten fingers so the shape reads as the same shape on the thumb and the pinky.

C) Which hand features suit which shapes. This is industry consensus; no peer-reviewed validation exists, so the evidence level is C.
- Short or wide nail beds, short fingers and broad palms: tapering, elongating shapes (oval, short almond, almond, coffin or ballerina, tapered square) at short-medium to medium length. Avoid square, flare and wide round at bare length.
- Long, narrow nail beds and slender fingers can wear anything; square, squoval, coffin and lipstick add balance.
- 'Mirror the cuticle' is a classic salon rule: a flat cuticle suits square or squoval, a round cuticle suits round, oval or almond.
- Pointed and long shapes (stiletto, edge, pipe, flare, XL and XXL) need enhancements. Avoid them for hands-on jobs.
- All numeric thresholds are provisional, estimated from adult anthropometric averages and the offsets between joint centres and skin creases. Recalibrate them as tertiles on at least 200 local hands.

D) Age, which the owner asked about. Do not infer age from hands: it is unreliable, sensitive and can offend. Offer an optional self-selected style profile or age bracket instead, and handle visible age-related changes (ridges, dryness, thin nails) as care suggestions. Do not use 2D:4D digit ratio, palmistry hand types or gender inference.

E) Nail conditions. Never diagnose. Use 'we noticed / our technician will check' wording in Arabic and English, apply high confidence thresholds, keep health inferences out of shared images and storage, and give the technician an override. Never suggest enhancements over possible lifting, a possible fungal change, broken skin or inflammation. Always suggest seeing a doctor for a new or changing dark streak.
GCC-specific: henna staining is cosmetic and common. Sheer and jelly finishes laid over henna will look warmer than a neutral preview, so the try-on renderer should composite those finishes over the actual nail colour.

F) Arabic UI naming. GCC salons mostly use transliterations (سكوير، أوفال، ألموند، ستيليتو، كوفن). For coffin, avoid the literal 'تابوت' because of its death connotation; label it 'باليرينا (كوفن)'. Naming advice is cultural judgement and was not verified with local salons.

| Shape | AR | Geometry | Suits | Avoid |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Natural (follow own nail) | طبيعي (حسب شكل الظفر) | No new outline. Use the detected nail mask as-is and only lengthen or shorten it along u to the chosen E, scaling the existing free-edge contour (distal boundary) with it. Default for a colour-only try-on. If E is greater than the existing free edge Ef, extrapolate the current tip contour, which is usually round to squoval: fit a superellipse with p 2.5–4 to the existing distal boundary. Apex is centred at about 40% from the cuticle; use the C-curve of a natural nail, 20–35%. | Everyone, and every colour-only try-on. Good for bare or short nails, nails growing back after biting, and customers who are not changing shape. | Any session where a shape change is the point. Also nails that are visibly uneven or broken: suggest 'match lengths' first (see conditions). |
| Square | مربع (سكوير) | Sidewalls are parallel, θ = 0° (tolerance ±1°). Extend them straight along the finger axis u, ignoring any natural fan-out in the mask. Tip is a straight line perpendicular to u at u = E, at full width (Wt = 1.0·W0). Corner radius r is 0–5% of W0 (sharp); a 'soft square' variant uses 6–12%. In the unified model, use a flat cap (p ≥ 12) with u_c = E − r. Apex is centred at about 40–45% of visible length from the cuticle. C-curve is typically 30–40%. Usual E is 0.1–0.6·Lb (short to medium). At long lengths it looks blocky and the corners snag or break. | Long or narrow nail beds (NAR > 1.25). Long, slender fingers (FS high, FLR high). Narrow palm with long fingers. Flat or squarish cuticles (cuticle sagitta CS < 0.12), following the mirror-the-cuticle rule. Natural nails that need strength: the wide free edge is considered sturdy. Short, neat professional looks. Evidence: industry consensus. | Wide or short nail beds (NAR < 0.95), short or wide fingers, broad palms (PWR high) and prominent knuckles at bare or short length, because square stresses width. Very round cuticles. Long or XL lengths for daily wear, because the corners catch. |
| Squoval (soft square) | مربع بحواف دائرية (سكوفال) | Sidewalls are parallel, θ = 0°. The tip is flat to slightly convex: use a superellipse cap with p = 4–5 and b = 0.15–0.25·W0, giving a tip-curve sagitta of about 5–10% of W0. Equivalent alternative: a flat edge with corner radius r = 18–30% of W0. Full tip width, Wt = 1.0·W0. Apex is centred at about 40–45% from the cuticle; C-curve 30–40%. Usual E is 0.05–0.6·Lb. It is the most forgiving shape and keeps the same width as the nail bed. | Almost everyone, especially balanced to long nail beds (NAR 0.95–1.4) and average hands. Flat to oval cuticles (CS < 0.22). Natural nails, short lengths and active or hands-on customers, including salon staff. Men's grooming looks. Evidence: industry consensus. | Very wide, short nail beds combined with short fingers, where it can still add width; oval or short almond is better there. Customers asking for a dramatic or elongating effect. |
| Round | دائري (راوند) | Sidewalls are parallel, θ = 0°. The tip is a circular cap centred on the axis. When E ≥ 0.5·W0, use a true semicircle (superellipse with p = 2 and b = a = W0/2) on straight parallel sides. When E < 0.5·W0, start the cap at u_c = 0 and draw a shallow circular arc with sagitta = E. No corners (r effectively 50% of W0). It follows a rounded cuticle. Usual E is 0–0.3·Lb; longer than that, it turns into oval. Apex is centred at about 40%; C-curve 25–35%. | Short nails and bare-to-short lengths. Nails growing out after biting. Round, high-arched cuticles (CS > 0.22). Active lifestyles, men, children and seniors. Short nail beds when kept very short, because it looks tidy. Evidence: consensus. | Wide nail beds plus short fingers at bare length, where it can look stubby; oval adds a little length. Customers who want elongation or drama. |
| Oval | بيضاوي (أوفال) | Sidewalls taper gently, θ = 3–8° per side, starting at u_s ≈ 0.1–0.3·E; they can be straight or slightly convex. The tip is an elongated half-ellipse: superellipse with p = 2, a = h(u_c), and b ≈ 0.8–1.2·a, so it is longer than round. Width at the cap start Wt ≈ 0.80–0.92·W0. No corners; symmetric about the axis. The tip's radius of curvature is about 30–45% of W0, blunter than almond. Usual E is 0.25–0.8·Lb. Apex at about 40–45% from the cuticle; C-curve 30–45%. | Wide or short nail beds (NAR < 0.95), a small nail on a fuller fingertip (NFR < 0.60), short fingers (FLR low), broad palms and prominent knuckles at medium length. Oval and round cuticles. Mature or classic style profiles when chosen by the customer. Generally slimming and universally flattering. Evidence: consensus. | Very long, thin natural nails at long length, because the narrowed tip is weaker without an overlay. Customers who want a crisp, modern square look. |
| Almond | لوزي (ألموند) | Sidewalls are convex and taper from the stress points. The tangent at each stress point is parallel to u (C1 with the nail bed), and the taper steepens to about 25–35° per side near the tip. Model: u_c = 0–0.15·E, superellipse cap with p = 1.5–1.7, a = W0/2, b = E − u_c. Then round the point with r_tip = 8–15% of W0 for a blunt point centred on the axis. Just before the tip rounding, the tangents meet at about 60–80°. Width at 50% of E is about 0.75–0.85·W0. Usual E is 0.5–1.0·Lb (medium to long); below 0.4·Lb switch to short_almond. Apex at about 40–45% from the cuticle, moving toward the stress area at long lengths. C-curve 35–45%. | Wide or short nail beds (NAR < 0.95). Short fingers (FLR low) and broad palms (PWR high). Tapered fingers (FT > 1.3), whose taper the shape echoes. Prominent knuckles at medium length. Small nails on fuller fingertips. Oval or round cuticles. Evidence: consensus; almond is the textbook 'elongating' shape. | Very short or weak natural nails without an overlay, because the narrow tip breaks. Heavy manual work. Very narrow pinky nails, where the absolute taper can look needle-like: keep r_tip in % of each nail's own W0. Customers with very columnar fingers who want a strong geometric look, for whom coffin may suit better. |
| Short / soft almond ('baby almond') | لوزي قصير (ألموند ناعم) | Uses the same convex taper as almond but shorter and blunter. u_c = 0, superellipse with p = 1.75–1.9, b = E, r_tip = 15–25% of W0. Width at 50% of E is about 0.85–0.90·W0. Usual E is 0.25–0.5·Lb. It sits between oval and almond and still reads as almond on natural nails without extensions. Apex at about 40%; C-curve 30–40%. | Wide or short nail beds and short fingers when the customer wants a practical length. Natural nails, and nails strengthened with builder gel or BIAB. Office and hands-on routines. Customers new to almond. Evidence: consensus. | Very narrow, long nail beds where a shorter square or squoval balances better (optional). Customers who want dramatic length. |
| Russian almond | لوزي روسي (رشن ألموند) | The term is UNCERTAIN; educators use it differently. The most common description is an elongated, slim almond. The sidewalls stay straight and nearly parallel (θ = 0–3°) for the first 40–60% of the extension (u_c ≈ 0.4–0.6·E), then taper late and fairly steeply into a narrow, softly rounded tip: superellipse cap with p = 1.6–1.8, b = E − u_c, r_tip = 6–10% of W0. It is usually built with a high C-curve (40–50%) and paired with 'Russian' e-file cuticle work, with colour applied very close to the cuticle. Usual E is 0.7–1.2·Lb. Apex at about 45%, shifted slightly toward the stress area. Confirm the definition with local technicians before using the name in the UI. | Balanced to long nail beds and slender or average fingers. Customers who want a refined, long, glossy look on enhancements, and clients of salons offering Russian or e-file manicures. Evidence: low, because the term is emerging. | Short natural nails, very wide nail beds (the late taper leaves a blunt wide shoulder) and hands-on work. |
| Mountain peak | قمة الجبل (ماونتن بيك) | A hybrid of almond and stiletto with a sharp 'peak' tip. The sidewalls start convex (tangent parallel at the stress points) and converge into near-straight lines that meet at a sharp, centred point. Model: u_c = 0–0.1·E, superellipse with p = 1.2–1.4, r_tip = 0–3% of W0. Included tip angle is about 50–70°, wider than a stiletto's 30–50°. Width at 50% of E is about 0.65–0.75·W0. Usual E is 0.6–1.0·Lb (medium to long). Apex at about 45%; C-curve 40–45%. Variant definitions exist; some sources call it a 'shorter, sharper almond' (uncertain). | Average to narrow nail beds, slender to average fingers. Customers who want an edgier almond without full stiletto length. Gel or acrylic overlays or extensions. Evidence: low (editorial). | Very wide nail beds, where the taper angle becomes too steep and looks blunt. Natural short or weak nails. Childcare or hands-on jobs, because the point is sharp. |
| Stiletto | ستيليتو (مدبّب) | Sidewalls are straight and strongly tapered from the stress points (u_s = 0) to a sharp, centred point. The per-side taper is θ = atan((W0/2)/E), about 15–25° per side at long or XL lengths, giving an included tip angle of about 30–50°. In the unified model: superellipse with p = 1.0–1.15 (p = 1 gives straight lines) and r_tip = 0–3% of W0. It needs E ≥ 1.0·Lb (long or longer); at shorter E the angle widens and it reads as mountain peak. Apex shifts toward the stress area, at about 50–60% of Lb from the cuticle, because of the longer lever. C-curve 40–50%, often pinched. Most customers need enhancements (gel, acrylic or tips). | Long, slender fingers and narrow to average nail beds (NAR ≥ 1.0) that can carry length. Also elongates short fingers when the customer accepts long length. Tapered fingers. Statement, event and nail-art looks. Evidence: consensus. | Very wide or short nail beds, where the taper is steep and squat. Natural nails without enhancement. Manual or caregiving work and contact-lens wearers, because of the sharp tip. Petite hands at XL or XXL, where it can look claw-like. |
| Coffin / Ballerina | باليرينا (كوفن) | Sidewalls are straight, tapered θ = 7–12° per side, starting at u_s = 0–0.2·E. The tip is flat and perpendicular to u at u = E, with tip width Wt = 0.45–0.65·W0. Wt follows from Wt = W0 − 2(E − u_s)·tanθ; adjust θ to keep Wt in that range. Corners have a small radius, r = 3–8% of W0. It looks like a stiletto with the point cut off. Usual E is 0.7–1.5·Lb (medium-long to XL); below 0.5·Lb it collapses into tapered_square. Apex at about 45–50%; C-curve 40–50%. In Arabic UI, prefer 'باليرينا (كوفن)' and avoid the literal 'تابوت'. | Wide nail beds that need slimming (the taper narrows them) at medium length or longer. Long fingers. Columnar, straight fingers (FT < 1.15), whose straight lines it echoes. Enhancement customers, and occasion and wedding sets. Evidence: consensus. | Very short nails, where it looks like a tapered square. Very narrow nail beds, where the tip becomes too narrow. Thin natural nails, whose corners break. Hands-on work at long lengths. |
| Ballerina (narrow-tip coffin variant) | باليرينا | Many salons treat it as identical to coffin. Where educators distinguish them, ballerina's sidewalls taper a little more and with a gentle convex curve (effective θ 9–14° per side), the tip is narrower (Wt = 0.35–0.5·W0) and the corners are softer (r = 6–12% of W0). Other parameters match coffin, with E of 0.8–1.5·Lb. The distinction is UNCERTAIN. Recommendation: implement a single coffin key labelled 'Ballerina (Coffin)' with an optional 'narrow tip' slider. | Same as coffin. A slightly more feminine, softer look on long, slender fingers. | Same as coffin, plus very narrow nail beds where the tip becomes almost pointed. |
| Tapered square | مربع مستدق (تيبرد سكوير) | Sidewalls are straight with a mild taper, θ = 3–7° per side, starting at u_s ≈ 0. The tip is flat and perpendicular to u, with Wt = 0.75–0.90·W0. Corners range from sharp to soft, r = 2–8% of W0. It is a square look with a slimmer silhouette, essentially a short coffin. Usual E is 0.3–1.0·Lb. Apex at about 45%; C-curve 35–45%. | Wide nail beds (NAR < 0.95, NFR > 0.75) on customers who like a square look; the taper offsets the width. Medium-length acrylic or gel. Evidence: consensus (acrylic tradition). | Very narrow nail beds, which get even narrower. Bare length, where the taper does not show. |
| Lipstick (slanted) | ليبستيك (مائل) | Sidewalls are parallel, θ = 0°. The tip is one diagonal edge, straight or very slightly convex (sagitta ≤ 5% of W0), cut at α = 25–40° from the v-axis, so one side is longer. The long corner sits at u = E and the short corner at u = E − W0·tanα; at α = 30° the short side is 0.58·W0 shorter. The long corner is rounded like a lipstick bullet (radius 10–20% of W0); the short corner has radius 3–8%. Constraint: the short side must keep at least 0.15·Lb of free edge, so E ≥ W0·tanα + 0.15·Lb (medium or longer). I found no fixed industry convention for which side is longer: add a mirror toggle and default to mirror-symmetric slants on left and right hands. Apex at about 45%; C-curve 35–45%. | Balanced to long nail beds and average to long fingers. Customers who want a distinctive statement at medium to long length. Evidence: low (editorial). | Short nails, where there is no room for the slant. Nails that grow at a strong angle (axis deviation > 7°), because the slant exaggerates the asymmetry. Customers who prefer classic looks. |
| Flare / Duck | فلير / ذيل البطة (داك) | Sidewalls are straight and flare OUTWARD, θ = −10° to −25° per side, starting at u_s ≈ 0–0.2·E. The tip is flat and perpendicular to u, and wider than the nail bed: Wt = W0 + 2(E − u_s)·tan/θ/, about 1.2–1.7·W0. Corners are sharp, r = 0–5% of W0. The extension leaves the finger silhouette, so the renderer must draw the overhang over the background (alpha composite with edge anti-aliasing and a cast shadow). Usual E is 0.8–1.5·Lb. Apex at about 45–55%. Enhancement only. | Long, slender fingers with narrow nail beds (NAR > 1.25, FS high) on customers who want a Y2K or statement look. Photo shoots and events. Evidence: low. | Wide nail beds, short fingers and broad palms, because it amplifies width. Natural nails. Daily practicality: it catches and breaks easily. Most professional settings. |
| Edge | إيدج (بحافة مركزية) | The outline is usually pointed, almond- or stiletto-like: superellipse with p = 1.1–1.4, r_tip = 0–3% of W0, E = 0.9–1.5·Lb. Its defining feature is 3D: a sharp ridge runs down the centreline from the structural apex (about 40% from the cuticle) to the tip, with two flat planes sloping down to the sidewalls, giving a triangular, prism-like cross-section. Render it as two facets with a crisp specular line along the ridge instead of the smooth highlight band of a C-curve. Coffin-outline 'edge' variants also exist. Enhancement only. | Long fingers and average to narrow nail beds on customers who want sculpted, editorial nail art. Acrylic or hard-gel customers. Evidence: low. | Natural nails, short lengths, wide nail beds and hands-on work. |
| Arrowhead | رأس السهم (أروهيد) | The definition is UNCERTAIN; it is a newer editorial term. The common depiction has parallel sidewalls (θ = 0°) up to u_c ≈ 0.3–0.5·E. There an angular 'shoulder' forms (corner radius ≤ 3% of W0), and two straight edges converge to a sharp, centred point at u = E. The half-angle at the point is atan((W0/2)/(E − u_c)), about 35–50° (70–100° included). r_tip = 0–3% of W0. It is shorter and wider-angled than a stiletto and has straighter, more geometric sides than mountain peak. Usual E is 0.6–1.0·Lb. Apex at about 45%. | Slender to average fingers and average to narrow nail beds on customers who want a geometric statement at medium length. Evidence: low. | Wide, short nail beds, where it reads as blunt and triangular. Natural, weak nails. Sharp-tip safety concerns. |
| Pipe | بايب (أنبوبي) | This is a structure rather than an outline. The top-view outline is usually stiletto or coffin, but the sidewalls are pinched so they fall almost vertically from the centre, giving a tube-like cross-section (C-curve about 50% or more, approaching a half-circle). To render it: in the extension zone (u > 0.3·E), multiply the projected half-width by about 0.80–0.90 because the sides drop away from the camera. Use a narrow, bright central highlight with darker sidewalls. E ≥ 1.0·Lb. Enhancement only, using an advanced sculpting technique. | Wide, flat nail plates that the customer wants to look slimmer at long length. Competition and advanced sculpted sets. Evidence: low. | Natural nails, short lengths, very curved natural nails, and budget or quick services. |

### Hand analysis rules

- **Pixel-space conversion (a precondition for every ratio)** — P_i = (landmarks[i].x × imageWidth, landmarks[i].y × imageHeight). Never measure distances in raw normalised units: x and y are normalised by different dimensions, which distorts the aspect ratio. If z is needed, scale it like x: z_px = z × imageWidth (the docs say z uses roughly the same scale as x). Use the processed frame size and undo any CSS mirroring of the selfie preview before mapping masks. → Build one helper, toPx(lm, w, h), and use it everywhere. Store raw landmarks per frame for median filtering. _(A, documented (MediaPipe hands doc: x and y normalised to [0,1] by image width and height; z relative to the wrist).)_
- **Dorsal (back of hand) facing the camera, plus handedness correction** — Compute the normal n = (P5 − P0) × (P17 − P0) with pixel x, y and scaled z. The sign of n_z differs between palm-facing and back-facing views and flips between left and right hands. MediaPipe's handedness assumes a mirrored, selfie-style image, so swap Left and Right for rear-camera or un-mirrored photos. Build the sign table empirically from about 20 test photos per case rather than hard-coding it from theory. Simpler fallback: the view is dorsal if the nail segmenter finds at least 3 nails. → If the palm is facing the camera, prompt in both languages: 'Turn your hand over, nails facing the camera' / 'اقلبي يدك بحيث تظهر الأظافر للكاميرا'. _(A for handedness and mirroring (MediaPipe doc). B for the sign method, which must be validated on test images.)_
- **Finger extension and spread (pose gating)** — Joint bend at the PIP is the angle between (P_PIP − P_MCP) and (P_DIP − P_PIP); bend at the DIP is the angle between (P_DIP − P_PIP) and (P_TIP − P_DIP). Index 5-6-7-8, middle 9-10-11-12, ring 13-14-15-16, pinky 17-18-19-20, thumb 2-3-4 (IP = 3). Accept the frame if both bends are under about 20–25° in 2D. Spread check: tip-to-tip distances d(8,12), d(12,16) and d(16,20) must each exceed about 0.8 × the mean nail width, so neighbouring nail masks do not touch. → Capture guidance: hand flat on a table, fingers slightly apart, camera 20–30 cm directly above, even light, no rings. Auto-capture when the gates pass for about 10 consecutive frames. _(B, a geometric method; thresholds are provisional.)_
- **Nail and finger tilt (foreshortening correction)** — Scale factor s = d2D(P0, P9) / dWorld(W0, W9), treating the palm as roughly parallel to the image plane; check it by comparing d2D(5,17) with s·dWorld(5,17). For each distal segment, r = d2D(DIP, TIP) / (s × dWorld(DIP, TIP)), which approximates cos(tilt). Pairs: 7-8, 11-12, 15-16, 19-20, and 3-4 for the thumb. → Reject frames with r < 0.8, or correct Lb_corr = Lb_img / r when 0.8 ≤ r < 0.95. Use the median of r over 10–30 frames. Never correct by more than 1.25×. _(B. The method is sound; the noise in world landmarks is not quantified by Google (model card: z and world coordinates come from synthetic data).)_
- **Landmark reliability and smoothing** — Use the Full model bundle (hand_landmarker.task, float16, about 7.8 MB on storage.googleapis.com). Per frame, discard detections with a handedness score below about 0.8 or low hand presence, and blurry frames (variance of the Laplacian below a calibrated threshold). Take the per-landmark median over a 10–30 frame window, or a One-Euro filter for live AR. → Never use the TIP landmarks (4, 8, 12, 16, 20) to place the nail free edge or measure nail length; use the nail mask. Use landmarks for orientation, finger identity, pose gating and coarse proportions only. Ratios built from MCP and PIP landmarks are more reliable than ones involving tips. _(A (official model card).)_
- **Occlusions: rings, henna, tattoos (important in the GCC)** — Signs of occlusion: (a) high frame-to-frame landmark jitter; (b) dark brown, orange or red patterns inside the hand mask that differ from sampled skin colour (henna); (c) a local width bulge or colour break on a proximal phalanx (a ring). → Ask the customer to remove rings for analysis. With henna present, still run the try-on but show a low-confidence note ('Results may be less precise with henna' / 'قد تكون النتائج أقل دقة مع وجود الحناء'). Never refuse the service. Pass the henna flag to the colour renderer. _(A (model card out-of-scope list); B for the detection heuristics.)_
- **Matching each nail mask to its finger** — For each nail mask, take its centroid c and its distance to each distal segment: 7-8 index, 11-12 middle, 15-16 ring, 19-20 pinky, 3-4 thumb. Assign the mask to the nearest segment. Require c to project onto the distal 40–110% of that segment measured from the DIP, and the mask's major axis to lie within 25° of the segment. Resolve conflicts with Hungarian assignment. → If fewer than 4 nails are assigned, ask for a recapture. Handle the thumb separately (see the thumb rule). _(B.)_
- **Nail-local frame and base dimensions (W0, Lb, Wmax, stress points)** — Axis u = normalize(P_TIP − P_DIP), blended with the nail-mask major axis from principal component analysis: weight the mask axis 0.7 if the two differ by less than 10°; otherwise keep the landmark axis and flag a possible deviation. Cuticle point: the most proximal mask boundary point on the centreline. Stress points: the distal ends of the lateral mask boundaries where the nail leaves the skin, found from the curvature change and the colour transition from the pink bed to a white or translucent free edge (in CIELAB, lightness rises and redness falls past the onychodermal band). W0 = distance between the stress points. Wmax = maximum mask width perpendicular to u. Lb = centreline distance from the cuticle to O. Existing free edge Ef = distance from O to the distal mask tip. → Quality check: W0 / finger width should be about 0.5–0.85 and the mask should be roughly convex and symmetric; otherwise reject the mask. Smooth W0 and Lb over frames. _(B. The stress-point detection needs validation on real salon photos.)_
- **Nail-bed aspect ratio NAR = Lb / W0 (or Lb / Wmax)** — From the nail mask in the nail-local frame, with the tilt correction applied. Compute per finger and use the mean of index, middle and ring for the hand-level class. Thumbs are naturally lower and pinkies can be higher. → Wide or short: oval, short almond, almond, coffin or ballerina, tapered square, at least short-to-medium length (E 0.3–0.6·Lb); avoid square, flare and wide round at bare length. Balanced: anything. Long or narrow: square, squoval, round, coffin and lipstick flatter; avoid very sharp XL stilettos unless requested, because they look needle-like. _(B for the measurement. C for the advice (industry consensus; no peer-reviewed validation).)_
- **Nail width vs finger width NFR = W0 / finger width at the DIP** — Finger width is the hand-mask silhouette width perpendicular to u through P_DIP (7, 11, 15, 19; IP joint 3 for the thumb). This needs a hand or finger mask: seeded skin segmentation, the selfie_multiclass body-skin class, or InteractiveSegmenter. → Low NFR: oval or almond at medium length to lengthen; tell the colour module that colours close to skin tone visually extend the nail; avoid tiny square tips at bare length. High NFR: tapered shapes (almond, coffin, tapered square) slim the nail; avoid flare. _(C (consensus); thresholds provisional.)_
- **Short nail bed: Lb relative to the distal phalanx** — Lb divided by the distance from the cuticle to the end of the fingertip skin along u, taken from the hand mask. Fallback: Lb / d(DIP, TIP), noting that the TIP landmark is noisy. → Oval or short almond at short-to-medium length. Builder gel, BIAB or tips to move the visual free edge. A deep-smile-line French visually lengthens. Avoid square at bare length. _(C; thresholds provisional.)_
- **Existing free edge and whether a natural length is achievable** — Ef / Lb from the nail mask, using the white free-edge region beyond O. → If the selected E is greater than Ef, label the preview 'with extensions/tips' (AR 'مع الإطالة/التركيب') and optionally estimate the natural grow-out time at about 3 mm a month (literature about 3.5 mm a month for fingernails, not verified this session). In natural-only mode, cap E at Ef. _(B for the measurement. The growth-rate figure is from memory (Yaemsiri et al. 2010) and not re-verified.)_
- **Cuticle shape (the mirror-the-cuticle rule)** — Fit a circle or parabola to the proximal 25% of the nail-mask boundary. Normalised cuticle sagitta CS = sagitta / W0. → Flat: square or squoval. Oval: oval, squoval or almond. Round: round, oval or almond. Use this as a tie-breaker weight (about 0.15). _(C (a classic salon rule); thresholds provisional.)_
- **Smile-line depth (onychodermal band), for French-tip rendering** — Find the distal boundary of the pink nail bed from the colour transition, fit a curve, and take depth = sagitta / W0. → Use it to draw the French white tip. Offer a 'deep smile' French for short nail beds because it elongates. _(C.)_
- **Nail growth-angle deviation** — The angle between the nail-mask major axis and the finger axis (DIP to TIP). → Draw the extension along the FINGER axis, as technicians do to correct the growth angle. Prefer symmetric shapes (oval, squoval). Avoid lipstick on that nail. Tell the technician. _(B for the measurement; C for the practice.)_
- **Finger length vs palm length FLR (middle finger)** — FLR = (d(9,10) + d(10,11) + d(11,12)) / d(0,9). d(0,9) is also MediaPipe's own palm-size normaliser. Repeat for the other fingers with their own MCP-to-TIP path if wanted. → Short fingers: elongating outlines (oval, almond, short almond; stiletto or mountain peak if the customer wants long nails), medium-to-long length, vertical designs; avoid square or flare at bare or short length and very wide tips. Long fingers: square, squoval, coffin and short lengths look elegant; XL pointed shapes can look exaggerated. _(C (consensus); numbers provisional.)_
- **Palm breadth vs finger length PWR** — Breadth = hand-mask width along the line through P5 and P17, extended to the silhouette on both sides. Fallback: 1.25 × d(5,17), because MCP joint centres sit inside the silhouette (the factor is provisional). PWR = breadth / middle-finger length (path 9-10-11-12). → Broad with short fingers: oval, almond or coffin at medium length or longer; avoid short square and flare. Narrow with long fingers: square, squoval and round all work, and short lengths are elegant. _(C; numbers provisional.)_
- **Finger slenderness FS** — FS = finger length (MCP to TIP path) / finger width at the middle-phalanx midpoint. The width is the hand-mask width perpendicular to the segment at the midpoint between PIP and DIP: index (P6 + P7)/2, middle (P10 + P11)/2, ring (P14 + P15)/2, pinky (P18 + P19)/2. Needs a hand mask. → Slender: square, coffin, flare, stiletto and lipstick all carry. Short or wide: oval, almond, short almond or mountain peak at medium length; avoid square and flare. _(C; thresholds provisional.)_
- **Finger taper FT** — FT = mask width at the proximal-phalanx midpoint (index (P5 + P6)/2, middle (P9 + P10)/2, ring (P13 + P14)/2, pinky (P17 + P18)/2) divided by mask width at the DIP (7, 11, 15, 19). → Tapered fingers: almond, oval and stiletto echo the taper. Columnar fingers: square, coffin and squoval echo the straight lines, or soften them with oval. Use a low weight (about 0.1). _(C (low).)_
- **Knuckle prominence KP** — KP = mask width at the PIP (6, 10, 14, 18) divided by the mean of the widths at the proximal-phalanx and middle-phalanx midpoints. Note: a top-down photo shows only the sideways bulge. Dorsal knuckle height cannot be measured, and the landmarks are joint centres, not the skin surface. → Medium length with elongating shapes (oval, almond, coffin) to draw the eye to the tips. Avoid very short square. Never mention knuckles to the customer; only change the recommendation. _(C (low).)_
- **Absolute hand size (for length caps only)** — Hand length ≈ sum of world-landmark distances along 0→9→10→11→12, in metres. → Petite: cap the default suggestion at 'long' and prefer slimmer shapes. Large: XL works well. Use internally only; never show the customer a measured size. _(B (documented metric output, unvalidated accuracy).)_
- **Consistency across fingers and uneven lengths** — Ef and W0 per finger. A nail is flagged as broken or short if its Ef < median(Ef) − 0.25·Lb. → Offer 'match lengths': a tip or repair on the short nail, or file the others down. Apply the same relative shape parameters on every finger. Use the same absolute free-edge length in mm on the thumb through ring finger, and about 85–90% of it on the pinky (salon consensus). _(C.)_
- **Thumb handling** — Thumb landmarks are 1 CMC, 2 MCP, 3 IP and 4 TIP; there is no DIP, so the IP joint (3) takes the DIP role and the axis is 3→4. In a flat-hand photo the thumb nail is usually rotated sideways: check the foreshortening ratio r for segment 3-4 (see the tilt rule). → If r(3-4) < 0.8, ask for a separate thumb capture with the thumb resting flat and the nail facing the camera. Give pointed shapes on the thumb slightly less taper so they stay centred. _(A for the indices (MediaPipe enum); C for the practice.)_
- **Age (requested by the owner): do NOT infer** — Not computed. Optional: a self-selected style profile (classic, trendy or bold) or an optional age bracket entered by the customer. → Handle age-related nail changes (ridges, dryness, thinner nails) through the condition suggestions (ridge filler, strengthening, cuticle care). Never generate stereotyped advice such as 'mature hands should wear nude'. _(Ethical and regulatory judgement; legal detail not verified.)_
- **Signals NOT to use** — 2D:4D digit ratio (index length 5→8 vs ring length 13→16); palmistry hand types (earth, air, fire, water); gender inference. → Exclude them from the engine and the marketing copy. _(D (excluded).)_
- **Source of the finger and palm mask (needed for width-based rules)** — All free and on-device options: (1) MediaPipe ImageSegmenter selfie_multiclass_256x256, whose labels I verified from the model file: background, hair, body-skin, face-skin, clothes, others. It is built for selfies, runs at 256×256, and is unvalidated on hands. (2) MediaPipe InteractiveSegmenter (magic_touch), prompted with keypoints at the mid-phalanx landmark positions. (3) Classical segmentation: sample skin colour at landmarks 5–17 and the mid-phalanges, threshold in YCrCb or CIELAB inside a corridor around each finger's landmark chain, then refine with a GrabCut-style step. → Prototype option 3 as the main method and option 2 as a fallback. Validate the measured width against calliper measurements on about 20 hands before trusting FS, FT, KP and NFR. _(A for the model labels and API; B for suitability.)_
- **Combining everything into recommendations** — score(shape) = Σ w_f × fit(shape, feature_class). Starting weights: NAR 0.30, FLR or FS 0.25, NFR 0.15, cuticle 0.15, taper and knuckles 0.10, trend prior 0.05. Hard constraints: natural-only mode caps E at Ef; a nail with possible lifting, a possible fungal change, broken skin or inflammation gets no enhancement suggestion; 'hands-on work' (customer answer) caps length at medium and removes stiletto, flare, edge, pipe and arrowhead. → Show the top 3 shapes and 2 lengths, each with a one-line reason tied to a measured feature, in Arabic and English (for example 'Almond adds length to wider nail beds' / 'الشكل اللوزي يضيف طولاً للأظافر العريضة'). Always allow a free choice. Log the chosen shapes, with consent, to re-tune the weights. _(Design proposal; the individual feature advice is level C.)_

### Natural nail conditions (never diagnose)

- **GLOBAL POLICY (applies to every condition below)** — Show a note only above a high confidence threshold, and default to silence when unsure. Show notes privately, never in shared or exported try-on images or the public gallery. Do not store health inferences by default; if stored, require explicit consent, because health data is sensitive (Oman PDPL detail not verified). The technician can always override or dismiss. Notes adjust recommendations (for example, no enhancements on an affected nail) but the colour try-on stays available. Add a disclaimer: 'This is a beauty preview, not a medical assessment' / 'هذه معاينة تجميلية وليست تقييماً طبياً'.
- **Vertical (lengthwise) ridges** — Suggest a ridge-filling base or builder gel (BIAB) add-on. In the renderer, high-gloss and chrome finishes on ridged nails will show texture: optionally use the detected ridge texture to modulate the specular term for realism.
- **Horizontal grooves or lines across the nail** — Never attribute a cause. Suggest a ridge filler. Do not block the service. Mention only when several nails show it and confidence is high.
- **Short or bitten nails (shortened nail bed, irregular free edge)** — Default to round, squoval, natural or short-almond previews. Offer a BIAB or builder-gel overlay, plus a 'with tips' preview labelled as such. If broken skin is detected near a nail, mark that finger 'technician to check' and suggest no enhancement there today.
- **Peeling, splitting or brittle nails** — Recommend shorter lengths and sturdy shapes (squoval, round, short almond), and add a strengthening or builder-gel option. Suggest soak-off rather than peel-off removal. Avoid recommending long natural pointed shapes.
- **White spots or marks** — No warning needed unless the spots are widespread. In the renderer, an opaque polish hides the spots, but a sheer or jelly polish should let them show through (composite over the real nail colour).
- **Yellowing or staining** — Recommend a base coat. If thickening or crumbling also appears, switch to the 'possible fungal signs' handling. In the try-on, sheer shades must be composited over the yellow base so the result is accurate.
- **Henna staining (GCC-specific, cosmetic)** — Treat it as cosmetic and never as a condition. Set a henna flag. The colour renderer must composite sheer, jelly and French finishes over the measured nail colour. Lower confidence on hand proportions, because the MediaPipe model card lists henna as out of scope. Never refuse the service.
- **Lifting or separation from the nail bed** — No enhancement or extension recommendation for that nail. Suggest a short length. Mark it 'technician to check'. Do not compute Lb from the visible pink area on that nail, because lifting shortens it; exclude the nail from the NAR averages.
- **Possible fungal-type changes (do NOT name)** — Never show the word 'fungus'. No enhancement suggestions for affected nails. Show only to the technician by default, or privately to the customer. Do not store the result. The colour try-on stays allowed.
- **Green or dark discoloration under an existing enhancement** — Flag 'remove and check' for the technician. Do not preview a new enhancement on that nail. Never suggest covering it with opaque polish.
- **A dark streak along the nail (must not be hidden)** — Always surface this to the customer privately, even at moderate confidence, because the cost of missing it is high. Recommend seeing a doctor, not a product. Do not suggest dark opaque cover-ups as a 'fix'. Allow the service only after the customer acknowledges the note.
- **Dry, overgrown or damaged cuticles and hangnails** — Suggest a cuticle care add-on (Russian or e-file manicure, oil). In the renderer, keep the polish edge a fixed, small margin (about 0.3–0.5 mm, roughly 3–5% of Lb) away from the cuticle and sidewalls, as a real manicure does; this is essential for realism.
- **Red, swollen or painful skin around the nail** — Mark the finger 'no service today'. Colour preview only. Recommend a medical check if painful. Never recommend enhancements on it.
- **Thin, weak or damaged nails after gel or acrylic removal** — Recommend short lengths and strengthening options. Avoid long natural or pointed shapes. Add a removal-method tip.
- **Small dents (pitting)** — Suggest a ridge filler or builder gel. Mention only when confident. Never name associated conditions.
- **A dark mark under the nail after a knock (bruise-like)** — Do not apply pressure-heavy services to that nail (no tip application or aggressive filing there). Show the note privately. Handle it like a dark streak if the mark is band-shaped.
- **Unusual curvature (strongly curved, spoon-shaped, very flat, or ingrown edges)** — Do not attempt detection from a single photo (out of scope). Let the technician flag it manually, which then adjusts the C-curve rendering and the tip-size suggestion.
- **Broken or uneven nail lengths** — Offer two previews: 'match with repair' and 'shorten all'. Use the target length consistently across all fingers.
- **Warts, cuts or open skin around the nails** — Exclude the affected finger from enhancement suggestions. Colour preview only. No diagnosis text.
- **A history of itching, redness or swelling after gel or acrylic (possible product sensitivity)** — Add a pre-service yes/no question. If the answer is yes, recommend regular polish only, add a medical-check note, and hide gel or acrylic upsells. Store the answer only with consent.

## Color catalog research

This is the master nail-colour catalog for the Muscat/GCC virtual try-on app, researched on 2026-10-24. It has 20 colour families, each with a Modern Standard Arabic name as used in Gulf salons and retail. Together they hold 241 named catalog shades spanning light/medium/deep and warm/cool/neutral, each with a representative sRGB hex, the finish it is usually sold in, and reference products from OPI, Essie, CND Shellac/Vinylux, DND, Gelish, Kiara Sky, Zoya, Chanel, Dior, Inglot O2M (breathable) and a few others. There are 53 iconic shades, 20 trend palettes for 2025-2026 (going into 2027), 16 brand/channel entries with what could be confirmed about GCC availability, and 20 GCC/Oman preference findings.

Four limits to read before using the data:
1. HEX VALUES ARE ESTIMATES. Direct page fetching was blocked in this environment (WebFetch DNS failure, proxy 403), so I could not pixel-sample swatch photos. Every real-product hex is a visual estimate of how the polish usually looks in swatch photos. It is not a measurement and no brand publishes official hexes. Before launch, re-derive every catalog colour from physical bottles or swatch sticks photographed next to an X-Rite/Calibrite ColorChecker under D65 light. Store CIELAB, opacity/translucency (sheer, jelly, creme), and finish parameters (shimmer, pearl, chrome, holo, glitter, matte) as well as the hex. Polish is not a flat colour, and a sheer polish over the natural nail is a blend, not a hex.
2. NEONS ARE OUT OF GAMUT. True fluorescent shades fall outside sRGB, and true neon pigments are restricted in US cosmetics, so many 'neons' are just very bright shades. Their hexes are clipped approximations; consider Display-P3 values for capable phones.
3. CODES AND NAMES MARKED '(verify)' are from memory or a single retailer. Examples are OPI codes such as N25, S86 and W42 (confirmed), versus shades named in collections I could not open. Chanel renumbered Le Vernis: Rouge Noir is 18 in the old numbering and 155 in current listings, Ballerina 167 to 111, Particulière 505 to 105, Pirate 08 to 151.
4. GCC DISTRIBUTION IS THE WEAKEST AREA. Search is US-centric. Confirmed: Nazih Group lists OPI and has showrooms in the UAE, KSA, Qatar, Kuwait, Bahrain, Oman and Lebanon (directory profile). Madi International signed an exclusive Essie GCC deal in 2009 (current status unverified). Inglot is run by Apparel Group, with Muscat stores at City Centre Muscat, Muscat Grand Mall and Mall of Oman. Gelish/Morgan Taylor appeared in KSA via Sawaya's Nail Arena at Beautyworld Saudi Arabia 2025. OPI and Dior are sold at UAE retail (Faces/ex-Wojooh, Sephora, Namshi). I found no confirmed GCC distributor for CND, DND, Kiara Sky, Bio Sculpture, The GelBottle, Semilac or Zoya. Call these distributors directly before promising brand-exact catalogs.

The main GCC preferences: skin-tone-correct nudes (Western pale nudes look ashy on medium, olive and deep Gulf skin), milky/sheer looks that cope with heat, French (often with gold), red, maroon/burgundy (عنابي), baby pink and off-white for Eid, nude bases plus red and gold around henna for weddings, chrome/gold/pearl and 3D for occasions, and demand for 'breathable/halal' polish for wudu. That last claim is contested by scholars, so label it as a manufacturer claim only.

Current trends to ship as palettes: Pantone 2025 Mocha Mousse (#A47864, widely published), Pantone 2026 Cloud Dancer (#F0EEE9, widely published), WGSN 2026 Transformative Teal and 2027 Luminous Blue (no official hex; approximations), Pinterest 2026 (Cool Blue, Jade, Plum Noir, Wasabi, Persimmon), butter yellow, milky nails, chrome/glazed, cherry mocha, and the OPI 45th-anniversary 2026 remix and vault collections.

For the recommender, OPI's own skin-tone guidance is a usable base: fair skin, pink or soft-peach nudes; olive skin, caramel, toffee or beige; medium to tan, mocha, warm peach or pinky-brown; deep skin, cocoa to deep caramel. Avoid grey or cool nudes on olive skin.

Typical professional gel catalog sizes: OPI GelColor Intelli-Gel relaunched in 2024 with about 100 shades (71 crèmes and 29 effects), plus 12 per season. Kiara Sky Gel PRO has about 150-176. Bio Sculpture says 200+. Semilac has 300+. DND numbering suggests about 300-400+. The GelBottle says 500+. CND Shellac plus Vinylux together come to about 223. OPI Nail Lacquer has 200+, Essie has made over 1,000 shades since 1981, Zoya has about 400, and Inglot O2M has 40+. So a salon realistically stocks 60-200 shades, and the app should let each salon tick off the subset it owns.

### GCC preferences

- Skin-tone-correct nudes are the core need. Bahrain Confidential (14 Apr 2026) says standard 'nude' advice centres on pale pinks and peachy beiges made for lighter skin, which look ashy, chalky or 'off' on the medium, olive and deeper skin tones common in the GCC. Ship nudes from light to deep and recommend by the user's measured skin colour.
- Heat and humidity favour low-maintenance, 'heat-proof' sheer pinks, creamy beiges, sandy nudes and milky tones ('your nails but better'), the leading GCC summer 2026 neutral direction (Bahrain Confidential).
- Eid is peak season. Oman salons are among their busiest before Eid, and nails are a major part of Eid beauty. French nails and classic shades are the go-to requests, with cat eye, ombré, chrome and 3D art gaining. Bright red, maroon, baby pink and off-white are expected to stand out (Oman Observer, quoting the founder of Waw Beauty Lounge, Al Khuwair, Muscat).
- Summer-Eid picks from Sayidaty (2025): pale pastel blue for white or light-blue outfits, pink leaning to mauve ('الوردي المائل إلى الموف') for romantic looks, and apricot orange ('البرتقالي المشمشي'), noted as especially flattering on tan or brown skin. Sayidaty also ran a 2026 Eid nails feature on 8 March 2026.
- Ramadan leans restrained: neutral shades, classic French and clear gloss, with minimal geometric art for some personality (Kuwait Times). Gulf News reports nail parlours are packed in the final days before Eid, and henna services draw the biggest crowds.
- Burgundy/maroon ('عنابي') and classic reds remain 2026 staples in Arabic coverage (Mobtada lists ombré, gradients, classic reds and burgundy tones among 2026 trends). Burgundy and red are safe defaults for GCC evening and occasion looks.
- Henna is central to Omani weddings (an 'indispensable part of a bride's adornment') and to the night before Eid, when women gather and artists work on hands and feet (Oman Observer). Gulf brides like fingertip henna in red, black or white (Arabia Weddings), and the Abu Dhabi heritage register records the al-muhannya henna tradition across the UAE and Gulf.
- Black henna is banned in Oman but still popular (PPD allergy risk), and safer alternatives are offered in Muscat (Oman Observer). The app should never suggest 'black henna' looks as safe.
- Pairings that work with henna: deep red, often with gold-foil tips, is the traditional match for bridal mehndi, while nude or sheer bases (sometimes with white henna-style art) let the henna stand out. Gold-shimmer nudes, gold cat-eye and soft French with gold or off-white tips are bridal favourites (Aza Fashions, Refinery29).
- Natural henna stain starts orange (lawsone) and darkens to deep reddish-brown over about 1-3 days, holding a few days before fading over 1-3 weeks (Wikipedia: Henna). The skin-analysis AI must spot henna-stained skin or nails and not read it as skin undertone; offer polish suggestions that harmonise with the stain colour.
- Gold accents and luxury finishes matter. Buro 247's 'Hottest types of nails in the GCC' lists gel (durability), acrylic length (almond, square, stiletto), chrome in silver, gold or holographic, glass nails, 3D pearls, rhinestones and floral appliqués (popular for weddings and parties), classic French and dip. Henna-inspired designs and Swarovski crystals are cited as Dubai favourites.
- Rose gold is described in Arabic beauty media as suiting all skin tones (Sayidaty). Bronze metallic was a summer 2025 trend (Sayidaty). Metallic and chrome are popular for New Year and celebration looks (Okaz).
- National Days. Oman's National Day is now 20 November: Sultan Haitham proclaimed the change on 11 January 2025, marking the 1744 founding of the Al Busaidi state, and 21 November 2025 was added as an extra holiday (Times of Oman, Euronews). Flag colours are red, white and green. UAE (2 Dec): red, green, white and black (OPI Red, Jade and Alpine Snow mani, Emirates Woman). Saudi (23 Sept): green swirls with thin gold and green French tips (Grazia ME).
- Breathable/'halal' polish demand. Polish blocks water from the nail during wudu, so Inglot O2M became a surprise hit with Muslim women in 2013 (Arab News, NBC). Nailstation Paris (Dubai) sold a permeable 'Breathe' line mainly into Bahrain, Kuwait, Jordan and France. BCI Group launched a halal-certified polish in the UAE for Ramadan. But permeability is contested: Jamiatul Ulama KZN reported O2M is not water-permeable, and Darul Iftaa advised against relying on it. Label these 'breathable (manufacturer claim)' and never as 'valid for wudu'.
- Muslim and conservative clients value women-only salons and self-expression without showing their faces (e.g. Dubai's 'nelfie' face-portrait nail art for clients who don't post their faces; Refinery29). Keep camera try-on privacy-first: on-device processing, hand-only crops, no face capture.
- GCC summer 2026 direction (Bahrain Confidential, 5 May 2026): maximalist and wearable. Colours are butter yellow, coral, jade green and a moodier ocean blue. Texture means 3D fruit, pearls, micro-chrome and tiny gems. The French tip is reworked as a thin coloured line in sage, peach or pastel blue.
- Dubai salon language (JDEED, May 2026): clients ask for 'espresso martini brown' or 'rich girl nude', and debate 'five different versions of translucent pink'. Arabic and English mood and nickname search terms should map to catalog shades.
- Gulf retail Arabic labels seen in practice: 'وردي نود', 'نود فاتح', 'لاتيه' and 'بني داكن' (Al-Dawaa KSA listings), plus ميتاليك, كروم, ذهبي وردي, برونزي, نحاسي, كحلي, موف, بيج, فرنسي (Sayidaty, Annahar, Dostor). Brand shade names stay in English/Latin script even on Arabic sites (e.g. wet n wild Arabic pages).
- Price context for Muscat: gel nails cost about OMR 4-18 across 14 Fresha-listed venues (Sib OMR 2.5-15). The market is gel-dominant and price-sensitive, so mid-tier gel lines and large numbered libraries (DND-style) compete with OPI and CND.
- Recommendation baseline from OPI's own skin-tone guide: fair skin, nudes with a hint of pink or soft peach (avoid too-yellow or brown). Olive skin (very common in the GCC), caramel, toffee or beige that mirror golden undertones; avoid grey or cool nudes that look ashy. Medium to tan, mocha, warm peach and pinky-brown, sheer for glow. Deep, bold creamy nudes from cocoa to deep caramel. For deep skin, editors favour rosy, taupe or warm-brown nudes applied sheer to avoid chalkiness (Makeup.com, SheerLuxe).

### Brands for salons

- **OPI (Wella Company; 'No.1 salon nail brand')** — Nail Lacquer, GelColor Intelli-Gel System (3-step, relaunched 2024), Infinite Shine, Powder Perfection (dip), xPRESS/ON press-ons, Chrome Effects powders (Tin Man Can, Pearl Pressure, Chrome to Mama, Mermaid for Me) · GCC: CONFIRMED in the UAE: retail via Faces (ex-Wojooh), Sephora, Lifestyle and major salons (Emirates Woman), and online on Namshi UAE. Distributor lead: Nazih Group's company profile lists OPI among its brands, with showrooms and offices in the UAE, KSA, Qatar, Kuwait, Bahrain, Oman and Lebanon, supplying salons with training (directory source; verify it is current). OPI ran the 'Nail It!' competition at Beautyworld Middle East Dubai in 2017-19. Oman-specific stockist not confirmed by search; ask Nazih's Oman office.
- **Essie (L'Oréal)** — Essie Original nail polish, Gel Couture (no-lamp long-wear), Expressie quick-dry, essie gel (professional; availability varies by region) · GCC: HISTORICAL: in July 2009 Madi International signed an exclusive deal to distribute Essie in the UAE, Kuwait, KSA, Oman, Qatar, Bahrain and Lebanon, with salon training (Khaleej Times). Madi still operates in the UAE, KSA, Qatar, Oman and Bahrain as a multi-brand nail, hair and skin distributor. Whether the deal is still in force is unverified. Widely sold at retail (e.g. Cloud10 Beauty KSA).
- **CND (Shellac, Vinylux)** — CND Shellac (14+ day UV/LED gel polish), CND Vinylux (weekly long-wear polish), CND enhancement / builder systems · GCC: UNCONFIRMED. An undated SalonGeek post suggests a Dubai distributor called 'Momentum'. In 2025 CND set up 'Team Eurasia' educators to support distributor partners across the region, without naming them. Check the cnd.com distributor locator or contact CND EMEA.
- **Gelish + Morgan Taylor (Hand & Nail Harmony / Nail Alliance)** — Gelish Soak-Off Gel Polish, Gelish Xpress Dip, Gelish PolyGel, Morgan Taylor professional lacquer (colour-matched) · GCC: PARTIAL. Gelish and Morgan Taylor React were featured at Beautyworld Saudi Arabia 2025's Nail Arena, 'powered by Sawaya' (a likely KSA partner, not confirmed as distributor). A Dubai vendor guide claims Gelish is sold through professional distributors and salons across the UAE (marketing source). Sold in 80+ countries. No Oman distributor found.
- **DND (DND Daisy)** — DND Duo (gel + matching lacquer), DND DC line, DND HEMA-free Duo, DND dip powder · GCC: NOT FOUND in GCC distribution searches. Bought online or via grey import. Called the most widely used gel brand in US salons (retailer claim).
- **Kiara Sky** — Gel PRO (HEMA-free), Gel Colors (classic), Dip powder (matched), Nail lacquer, Gel FX / builder · GCC: UNCONFIRMED. One Dubai vendor guide calls it 'well-distributed in the Dubai market' (marketing). No official GCC distributor found.
- **Bio Sculpture Gel** — Bio Sculpture Gel colour, Evo, Colour Builder Gel · GCC: UNCONFIRMED. Distributors in 30+ countries, found through a distributor contact form. No GCC listing found.
- **The GelBottle Inc (BIAB)** — Gel Colour, BIAB (Original, HEMA-free, File-Off, All-in-One) · GCC: NOT LISTED. The brand's (undated) distributor page names no GCC country. Likely grey import or salon direct-order only.
- **Inglot (O2M Breathable)** — O2M Breathable Nail Enamel, O2M Soft Matte · GCC: CONFIRMED. Apparel Group is Inglot's GCC partner, with Muscat stores at City Centre Muscat, Muscat Grand Mall and Mall of Oman (one older listing adds Mall of Muscat), and Kuwait relaunched in 2025. Retail-led, not a pro gel brand, but very relevant to Muslim clients.
- **Semilac (Poland)** — UV/LED Gel Polish (hybrid), Builder / base / top · GCC: NOT FOUND in GCC searches.
- **Zoya** — Zoya Nail Polish (creme, holo, PixieDust) · GCC: NOT FOUND (online import).
- **Chanel Le Vernis (luxury retail)** — Le Vernis (longwear) · GCC: Chanel beauty is sold through GCC luxury retail. Specific Oman stockists not verified.
- **Dior Vernis (luxury retail)** — Dior Vernis (gel-effect couture colour) · GCC: CONFIRMED UAE: sold on faces.ae (e.g. 100 Nude Look).
- **Nailstation Paris (Dubai-based)** — Breathe (halal / permeable), Organic and vegan lacquers · GCC: Dubai brand since 2000, shop opened 2001. Its permeable 'Breathe' halal line sold mainly in France, Jordan, Bahrain and Kuwait, about 15% of the business (2019 report; current status unverified).
- **Halal / breathable niche brands (786 Cosmetics, Orly Breathable, BCI Group UAE halal polish)** — Breathable / water-permeable lacquers · GCC: Mostly online. BCI Group launched a halal-certified polish in the UAE for Ramadan (Arabian Business). Orly ran a #HalalPaint collaboration with MuslimGirl.
- **Regional multi-brand distributors (not polish brands)** — Distribution, salon training, retail placement · GCC: Nazih Group (OPI and other salon brands; UAE, KSA, Qatar, Kuwait, Bahrain, Oman, Lebanon). Madi International (multi-category beauty distributor; historic Essie and Wella Professionals deals). Apparel Group (Inglot). Sawaya (KSA, Gelish/Morgan Taylor event partner). These are the first calls for confirming Oman salon catalogs.

| OPI | Russian Navy (R54) | `#1B2340` | blues | Benchmark near-black navy with micro-shimmer. Other brands' navies are reviewed against it. |

| OPI | You Don't Know Jacques! (F15) | `#8A7569` | greys_taupe | Cult mauve-toned taupe-brown, often suggested as a Mocha Mousse approximation. |
| OPI | Samoan Sand (P61) | `#E3C7AE` | nudes | Cult-classic nude with golden undertones. Repeatedly recommended for olive skin, which is directly relevant to GCC clients. |
| OPI | Kyoto Pearl (L03) | `#F1ECE5` | whites | Pearlescent white that Zola Ganzorigt recommends (mixed with top coat) for a lacquer version of glazed-donut nails. Riffed as 'Kyoto Oyster' in Spring 2026. |
| OPI | Strawberry Margarita (M23) | `#E6476A` | pinks | In The Zoe Report's top 10 best-selling OPI shades. |
| OPI | Tickle My France-y (F16) | `#DDBBAD` | nudes | In The Zoe Report's top 10. Classic mauve-leaning neutral, matched across OPI GelColor and lacquer. |
| OPI | Mod About You (B56) | `#F4C7D5` | pastels | Classic pastel pink, remixed for OPI's 45th anniversary as 'Mod About Me' (Spring 2026) and 'Mod About You Pastel' (Fall 2026). |
| Essie | Ballet Slippers | `#F5D9D9` | milky_sheer | Per Essie, the Queen's hairdresser wrote in 1989 that it was 'the only colour Her Majesty would wear'. About 818 bottles sold per day in the US. #1 on Who What Wear's Essie list. |
| Essie | Mademoiselle | `#EFCBC8` | milky_sheer | Makeup.com calls it the top salon shade in the world. #3 on Who What Wear's all-time Essie list. |
| Essie | Blanc | `#FBFBFA` | whites | #2 on Who What Wear's all-time Essie best-seller list. The opaque counterpart to Marshmallow. |
| Essie | Bikini So Teeny | `#A6B2E6` | blues | Reported as Essie's all-time top seller in one Who What Wear piece (other rankings put Ballet Slippers first; conflicting). |
| Essie | Wicked | `#4B0F1A` | berries_wines | Cult deep red-black. #6 in The Zoe Report's 2015 Essie best-sellers and on Who What Wear's all-time list. |
| Essie | Bordeaux | `#6D1423` | berries_wines | The only one of Essie's 12 original 1981 shades still sold (Makeup.com). Still a best-seller (women.com, 2026). |
| Essie | Licorice | `#0B0B0C` | blacks | #18 on Who What Wear's all-time Essie list. Staple black. |
| Essie | Fiji | `#F6CCD3` | pastels | Opaque full-coverage baby pink. #19 on Who What Wear's all-time Essie list. |
| Essie | Mint Candy Apple | `#B5E4CF` | greens | Essie names it one of its best-sellers and its top pastel green. |
| Essie | Chinchilly | `#A69D95` | greys_taupe | Cult cool greige that Essie says flatters practically every skin tone. |
| Essie | Marshmallow | `#F5F2EE` | milky_sheer | Classic sheer white. Among the best milky polishes (Marie Claire UK). |
| Essie | Geranium | `#E1412D` | reds | Named 'best orange-red' in a Who What Wear red guide. Bright fiery red for warm and golden skin. |
| Essie | Berry Naughty | `#6E1837` | berries_wines | Long-time best-seller, paired with Bordeaux in fall 2026 recommendations (women.com). |
| Essie | Lilacism | `#DDD0E9` | purples | Essie's classic soft lilac, a staple pastel. |
| Chanel | Le Vernis Rouge Noir (18 legacy / 155 current; 'Vamp' in US) | `#4A0E18` | berries_wines | Created on the 1994 runway and associated with Uma Thurman in Pulp Fiction (disputed). Chanel calls it its most popular and iconic nail shade. Brought back in the Couleurs Culte reissue. |
| Chanel | Le Vernis Ballerina (167 legacy / 111 current) | `#EFD2C9` | milky_sheer | Sheer nude-pink top seller. Session manicurist Jenni Draper says she replaces it and Rouge Noir more than any other shades in her kit. |
| Chanel | Le Vernis Particuliere (505 legacy / 105 current) | `#9C8B80` | greys_taupe | Debuted at SS10 shows and hit stores around January 2010. Lines, waiting lists and sell-outs. Started the taupe/greige nail craze. |
| Chanel | Le Vernis Pirate (08 legacy / 151 current) | `#C1192D` | reds | Ranked Chanel's best-selling shade of all time on one Who What Wear list. A classic red. |
| Chanel | Le Vernis Jade (2009, limited) | `#A9D6BD` | pastels | Released 11 Sept 2009 and sold out everywhere, with eBay resale up to about $200 versus about $25 retail. The original 'it' pastel green. |
| Dior | Dior Vernis 999 Rouge | `#C8172C` | reds | Dior's signature couture red, described by retailers as the line's iconic shade. Sold in the GCC on faces.ae. |
| Dior | Dior Vernis 100 Nude Look | `#E2C2B4` | nudes | Dior's rosy-beige nude icon, paired in marketing with 999. |
| CND | Shellac Romantique | `#F2D7D2` | milky_sheer | Long-standing Shellac sheer pink for natural and French looks. |
| CND | Shellac Negligee | `#F0D3CB` | milky_sheer | Salon favourite for French. 'In the CND stable forever' (SalonGeek pros). |
| CND | Shellac Cream Puff | `#F7F2EA` | whites | Salon pros' top-used Shellac white for French tips. |
| CND | Shellac Wildfire | `#CC1A2B` | reds | Salon pros' safe-bet red, on most-used Shellac lists. |
| CND | Shellac Black Pool | `#0D0D0F` | blacks | Core Shellac black, included in CND's salon-proven fall prepack. |
| CND | Shellac / Vinylux Oxblood | `#591A1E` | berries_wines | Compliment-magnet burgundy, a SheerLuxe favourite, in CND's salon-proven prepack. |
| DND | Ballet Pink #601 | `#F1D0D0` | milky_sheer | Ranked #1 nude in Beyond Polish's 2026 DND list. 'One of DND's most reordered shades by salon pros.' |
| DND | Cherry Mocha #751 | `#5B1E26` | berries_wines | Credited by NewBeauty and Her Campus with starting the 'cherry mocha nails' trend (burgundy meets coffee). |
| DND | Perfect Nude #867 | `#D1AD93` | nudes | #2 nude in Beyond Polish's 2026 DND list. A true skin-tone nude with no pink or peach lean. |
| Gelish | Good Gossip | `#AE1028` | reds | Salon forums repeatedly name it the most popular Gelish shade (very sparkly red). |
| Gelish | Sheek White | `#FAFAFA` | whites | Gelish salons' go-to French white. |
| Zoya | Carmen | `#C41E3A` | reds | Described as Zoya's best-selling true red. |
| Zoya | Alicia | `#C3C5CB` | metallics | Zoya's best-selling holographic: silver with gold hints. |
| Revlon | Cherries in the Snow | `#BE1E46` | reds | 1950s blue-red classic (launch dated 1953 by most sources, conflicting). Revlon says it remains in its top 20 nail shades. |
| OPI | Tin Man Can (Chrome Effects powder) | `#ECE7E0` | metallics | The powder behind the glazed-donut manicure. Hard to find for years and relaunched in the Tin Man Can Glaze / Pearl Pressure chrome collection. |
| Inglot | O2M Breathable Nail Enamel (e.g. 653 crimson) | `#A51B30` | reds | Launched the 'breathable/halal' polish category. A surprise hit with Muslim women in 2013 (Arab News, NBC). Sold in Muscat through Apparel Group stores. Its wudu suitability is contested. |

### Trend palettes

- **Pantone Color of the Year 2025: Mocha Mousse (17-1230)** (Announced 5 Dec 2024; 2025, with browns still leading fall 2026) — `#A47864` `#C9A688` `#8D7366` `#7A4A34` `#DDBBAD` #A47864 is the widely published sRGB for 17-1230 TCX. Pantone's own page did not show a hex in my retrieval, and one blog lists #9E7A68. The other hexes are nail interpretations (latte, cool mocha, milk chocolate, rosy-beige), not Pantone. CND said it had no exact match and suggested Shellac Satin Pajamas #265 and We Want Mauve #425, plus Vinylux Flowerbed Folly #346 and Bellini #290.
- **Pantone Color of the Year 2026: Cloud Dancer (11-4201)** (Announced 4 Dec 2025; 2026) — `#F0EEE9` `#F7F8F9` `#F3EFE9` `#ECE4D6` `#F7F2EA` The first (near-)white Color of the Year. #F0EEE9 is widely published, from secondary sources. Editorial nail matches: Essie Allure (warm broken white), OPI Alpine Snow (cool white). Manicurists cite it for airy, milky whites. Essie published a Cloud Dancer white selection.
- **WGSN x Coloro Colour of the Year 2026: Transformative Teal + A/W 26/27 Key Colours** (Announced Sept 2024; 2026 to A/W 26/27) — `#2B7377` `#EDE6D6` `#8F6CC2` `#6E4B3B` `#A5CF4C` Transformative Teal is Coloro 092-37-14. No official hex was found, so all five hexes are approximations. Key colours: Transformative Teal, Wax Paper, Fresh Purple, Cocoa Powder, Green Glow.
- **WGSN x Coloro Colour of the Year 2027: Luminous Blue + S/S 27 Key Colours** (Announced 29 Apr 2025; for 2027 (early adoption in late-2026 collections)) — `#2C5FD8` `#F07A1E` `#F06CA8` `#6D9A4B` `#B5715A` Luminous Blue is Coloro 125-28-38. Keys: Luminous Blue, Energy Orange, Pop Pink, Meadowland Green, Clay. Hexes approximate.
- **Pinterest Palette 2026** (2026) — `#A9C9E8` `#4E9D84` `#3E1D2D` `#B4C432` `#DD5A3A` Cool Blue (icy blue chrome nail searches +235%), Jade ('between mint and moss'; jade marble nails +450%), Plum Noir (deep purple, burnt burgundy and velvet brown; dark plum and deep burgundy nails +220%), Wasabi (chartreuse +175%), Persimmon. Search percentages come from secondary coverage. Hexes approximate.
- **Butter Yellow (2025 peak, summer 2026 revival)** (Spring/Summer 2025 and Summer 2026) — `#F7EBA9` `#F5E096` `#F4D872` `#F3E6C3` Called a defining 2025 nail trend. Key shades: Essie Buttercup (sold out at NYC salons), Essie Atelier at the Bay, OPI Blinded by the Ring Light, OPI Buttafly. Fresha data via Scratch: butter-yellow searches +1,025% over three months (summer 2026). Named in Bahrain Confidential's GCC summer 2026 trends.
- **Milky / 'Your Nails But Better' Neutrals** (2025 to 2026, year-round) — `#F3EFE9` `#F5D9D9` `#F1D5CA` `#F1D0D0` `#E0B9A8` `#F4C8CD` `#DCE4EF` Sheer pinks, creamy beiges, sandy nudes and milky tones, plus strawberry-milk and blueberry-milk variants. Bahrain Confidential (Apr 2026) calls it the leading GCC summer neutral look because it suits the heat.
- **Chrome & Glazed Evolution** (2023 glazed donut, running through fall 2026) — `#ECE7E0` `#BED3E5` `#D8A091` `#8D8C8F` `#6E4C3B` `#C3C5CB` Glazed pearl (Funny Bunny + Tin Man Can), icy-blue chrome (Pinterest 2026), rose gold, soft pewter and brown chrome (fall 2026 per Stephanie Stone via Who What Wear), holo silver. OPI Fall 2025 included the 'Met-tle Detector' mercury metallic. Chrome is one of Buro 247's top GCC nail types.
- **Cherry Mocha & Vamp Romantic** (Fall/Winter 2024 to 2026) — `#5B1E26` `#4A0E18` `#6D1423` `#34192E` `#32221C` DND #751 Cherry Mocha started the trend. Pinterest 2026 'Vamp Romantic' beauty trend. Burgundy forecast to return as a 2026 classic. OPI Lincoln Park at Midnight in Fall 2026.
- **Fall 2026 'Earthy, Elevated, Effortless' (Marie Claire / Andrea Forero)** (Fall 2026) — `#8B1B25` `#682D22` `#56512A` `#1F4C78` `#6E4A63` `#8A4A2C` `#2A2A2D` Deep red jelly, mahogany, burnt olive, deep ocean blue, dusty plum, cinnamon brown, smoky coal jelly (DND Coal #027). Plus Kiara Sky: chocolate brown and deep moody greens. Plus Who What Wear: understated metallics.
- **October 2026 Colour Trends (Who What Wear)** (Autumn 2026) — `#5A2244` `#141A32` `#0B0B0C` `#BF5F3F` `#583325` `#34192E` `#DCC3C6` Deep plum (an Essie shade), midnight blue (a Bio Sculpture shade), jet black, terracotta, chocolate/espresso, aubergine, milky mauve. Only part of the six-item list was visible.
- **2026 Quiet-Luxury Muted Forecast (manicurists)** (2026) — `#A8B8C7` `#5A7CA5` `#CC999B` `#9C7489` `#D0CBC4` `#050506` `#6D1423` Fog blue, denim, dusty rose, cool mauve, pearlescent oyster grey, extra-glossy black, burgundy. Fine shimmer replacing chunky glitter. Velvet and aura finishes rising. Short squoval and square shapes.
- **2026 Earth Tones Replacing Yellow** (2026) — `#5D6A3A` `#B5715A` `#BF5F3F` `#583325` `#E1412D` Manicurists name mossy green, clay and terracotta as yellow's successors, plus chocolate brown year-round and a 'tomato-y' orange-undertone red.
- **Summer 2026 Brights (OPI 'Trip to the Brite Side' + GCC summer)** (Summer 2026) — `#6FB4E6` `#1FA6A3` `#F6E04C` `#FFD03E` `#F06A57` `#B5E4CF` `#1F4C78` `#4E9D84` OPI summer 2026: 24 neon/bright shades, including Sky Blue's the Limit, Catch a Wave of Happiness, Sea-ze the Day, Beach Party Teal Dawn, No Summer Blues, Paintin' on Sunshine!, Sunkissed and Tell, Limon-yello!. Bahrain Confidential GCC summer 2026: butter yellow, coral, jade, moody ocean blue, with micro-French tips in sage, peach or pastel blue. Mint +233% and coral +260% (Fresha). Hexes approximate.
- **OPI 45th Anniversary: Spring 2026 'The New OPIcons' (remixes)** (Spring 2026) — `#F2E4E0` `#D01F2C` `#F06A57` `#8E5A6F` `#F2C0D3` `#F7EBC4` `#E8456B` 12 GC/NL/Powder Perfection shades: OPI'm a Bubble Bunny, Put It in Airplane Mode, You Don't Know Suzi, Mod About Me, Bathtime Bubbly, Queens Rule, Pompeii Pink, Shrimp Cocktail, Sunny Bunny, Strawberry Cosmo, Lincoln Park After Brunch, Big Apple Energy. 12 Infinite Shine shades: Kyoto Oyster, Dulce De Latte, Passion-ista, Lavender Bath, What's Not to Lilac?, Good Nighty Aphrodite, I'm Not Really an Influencer, Shock Em Orange, Less Is More, Oh Baby!, Big Apple Green, Sea Me Now. Hexes are guesses from the names and parent shades; verify against bottles.
- **OPI Fall 2026 'Most Requested' (vault revivals + remixes)** (Fall 2026) — `#6A1C24` `#C4561F` `#3C1219` `#2A1A33` `#5A2D1F` `#A29287` `#F5D3DF` 24 shades. Revivals include Every Month Is Oktoberfest (2012), Mrs. O'Leary's BBQ, Vampire State Building (2000), Route Beer Float (1997), It's All Greek to Me (2004), Vodka & Caviar (2007), Louvre Me Louvre Me Not, Koala Bear-y, Deer Valley Spice, Nein! Nein! Nein! OK Fine!. Remixes: Mod About You Pastel, Suzi Take a Vow, Lincoln Park at Midnight, Berlin There, Doin' That. Hexes are guesses; verify.
- **OPI Fall 2025 'What's Your Mani-tude?' (90s)** (Fall 2025) — `#7A1E28` `#8A4A2C` `#C8102E` `#5A2D1F` `#A08481` `#8D8C8F` Includes Slip Dressed Up, Penny Loafer Lane, Totally Red-ical, Band Tease, A Total Suzi, Lip Hot Liner, Grunge Queen, Goth 'n' Glam, Mood Ring Finger (mauve-taupe), Met-tle Detector (mercury metallic), plus the Velvet Underground matte top coat. Hexes approximate.
- **GCC Eid Palette (Muscat salons)** (Eid al-Fitr / Eid al-Adha 2025 to 2026) — `#D2232A` `#6A1C24` `#F6CCD3` `#F3EBD9` `#D3AE3A` `#C98E9E` `#F3A57A` `#B9D3EC` Oman Observer (Waw Beauty Lounge, Al Khuwair): French and classic shades are most requested, and bright red, maroon, baby pink and off-white stand out. Sayidaty summer-Eid picks: pale pastel blue, mauve-pink, apricot. Gold is the usual accent.
- **GCC Bridal & Henna-Night Palette** (Year-round (wedding season)) — `#F1D5CA` `#F5D9D9` `#FBFBFA` `#D3AE3A` `#ECE7E0` `#B3121F` `#6D1423` Nude or milky bases so henna stays the focus. Red with gold foil as the traditional pairing with mehndi. White or gold French. Pearl, 3D pearls and rhinestones for weddings (Buro 247). Henna stains run from orange to deep reddish-brown.

UAE: red, green, white, black (Emirates Woman used OPI Red, Jade and Alpine Snow). Saudi: green with gold swirls (Grazia ME). Flag hexes are approximations.
