## UX research

LIMITS ON THE RESEARCH (read first): Other agents in this workflow turn had already used the whole shared WebSearch budget (200 calls) before this agent's first query. Most hosts also could not be reached from this sandbox: w3.org, developer.mozilla.org, material.io, pantone.com, perfectcorp.com, nngroup.com and decree.om all failed with DNS errors or a proxy 403. Primary sources were therefore read straight from their official GitHub repositories and registries. VERIFIED this session: WCAG 2.2 success-criterion text (w3c/wcag repo); browser support data (mdn/browser-compat-data); Google Fonts licence, axes, weights and subsets (google/fonts METADATA.pb); real WOFF2 file sizes (Fontsource 5.3.0 packages from npm); W3C Arabic and Persian Layout Requirements (w3c/alreq); W3C i18n guidance on the dir attribute; CLDR locale behaviour, run locally in Node 22.22 (ICU 77.1, CLDR 47). All contrast ratios below were computed from the hex values. UNVERIFIED (prior knowledge, re-check when search budget is available): the AR-app patterns of YouCam/Perfect Corp, Sephora Virtual Artist/ModiFace, Snapchat, Instagram and the ARKit coaching overlay; Pantone 2025 'Mocha Mousse' and 2026 'Cloud Dancer'; 2025-26 nail trends; Oman PDPL details; WhatsApp usage in Oman. The user can send a follow-up message to continue web research. I also looked at the 5 reference images in /home/user/nail-mirror/dev-test/1.png to 5.png and at the existing build ('Nail Mirror', with 'Porcelain & Glaze' tokens in tailwind.config.js and src/index.css).

RECOMMENDATION: one design system called 'Glaze Atelier'. It grows out of the existing Porcelain & Glaze tokens instead of replacing them, so the code can migrate step by step. It takes from each reference: porcelain and linen neutrals (REF2/REF5); cocoa-mocha brand colours and an editorial serif (REF4); icy 'glaze' blue used only as the live/AR signal and as a seasonal accent (REF1); a floating dock with a dark central action button (REF3); and a filterable shade grid whose main action is 'Try on my hand', not add-to-cart (REF5). The rule that overrides everything else: nails and swatches always sit on a neutral background. No tinted glass, gradients or glows next to the paint, because surrounding colour shifts how a colour is perceived (simultaneous contrast). That would directly undermine the owner's 'as close to 100% accurate' demand.

KEY UX CHANGES: (1) The camera studio is built for ONE free hand. The customer holds the phone in one hand and points the rear camera at the other, so every control sits in a thumb dock that can be switched to the left or right side. (2) The app automatically freezes the best frame so the arm can rest; live mode is kept for 'wiggle to see the shine' on chrome, cat-eye and velvet finishes. (3) A tablet 'Table Mode' uses a free printable calibration mat (grey/white patches plus a mm ruler). This gives measurable colour and length accuracy and shows a 'Calibrated' badge. (4) Decision path: favourites tray, then a 2-up/4-up grid on the frozen photo, then a split slider, then 'Help me decide'. (5) A QR session links the customer's phone to the technician's tablet, and WhatsApp handles booking. (6) Only in-stock bottles are recommended; sold-out shades get the nearest in-stock alternative by colour distance (ΔE). (7) Arabic details verified in CLDR 47: ar-OM defaults to Arabic-Indic digits, while plain 'ar' and 'ar-AE' now default to Western digits. The app must therefore set the digit system explicitly. Recommendation: one global ١٢٣/123 setting with Western digits as the default for prices and codes, to be checked with Omani users. Other locale facts: the week starts on Saturday, the weekend is Friday-Saturday, the clock is 12-hour, and OMR uses 3 decimals. (8) The owner asked about age: do not infer age from hand photos. It is sensitive, offensive if wrong, a PDPL risk, and low-accuracy; offer an optional occasion/style choice instead.

BUILD NOW vs LATER (the user said 'make it ready now; anything for later can be enabled later'):
- NOW: live and frozen studio, colour/finish/shape/art/per-finger pickers, favourites tray, grid and split compare, AI result and recommendations, lookbook, WhatsApp booking handoff, admin inventory/prices/promos/QR, the RTL and accessibility rules, and the token system.
- LATER (behind feature flags): remote WhatsApp vote, tournament, lighting preview, outfit/henna match, calibration-mat badge, group/bridal board, offline home-service sync, story share cards.

REPO ISSUE FOUND IN PASSING: src/App.jsx imports ./pages/Landing, CatalogPage, Login and NotFound, plus the admin pages Requests, Looks, Inventory, Services, Reports, Staff, Settings and Audit. src/pages is empty and those admin files do not exist, so the Vite build will fail until they are created. Also, public/models/hand_landmarker.task is 7.8 MB. Preload it during the camera-permission screen and cache it with the service worker, or the first try-on will be slow on 4G.

### Core flows

- **F1 Walk-in QR quick try (reception to own phone)** (KPI: Proposed targets, not industry benchmarks. Time to first painted nail (QR scan to first paint): median ≤10 s on 4G, ≤5 s on salon Wi-Fi or a return visit with the model cached. Camera permission grant ≥85%. Try-ons per session ≥10. Shortlist created in ≥50% of sessions. Handoff to technician in ≥40% of sessions. Hand-tracking loss under 5% of session time.) — Scan the bilingual QR table card. The deep link carries salon id and station id; no install, no login. → Page opens in the device language with an EN/عربي toggle visible. Start loading the 7.8 MB hand model and the shaders immediately. → Mode chooser: Live camera (recommended), Use a photo, or Model hand. Then pick Left/Right hand and which hand holds the phone, which sets the dock side. → Camera primer explains on-device processing, then triggers getUserMedia with facingMode 'environment'. → Hand coach overlay ('ضعي يدك داخل الإطار' / 'Place your hand inside the frame') until 5 nails are tracked. A glaze-blue ring confirms the lock. → The first 'Today's pick' swatch is applied automatically, so the client sees value at once. → Tap swatches in the shutter-side tray; each tap repaints in about 150 ms. → Auto-freeze the best frame and say 'Captured — you can relax your hand'. Keep trying looks on the still photo. → Heart up to 8 looks, which go to the favourites tray. → 'Show my shortlist to the technician': a 4-digit session code syncs the tray to the technician's tablet.
- **F2 Technician-led tablet consultation (Table Mode)** (KPI: Median time to decision ≤4 min from opening the consultation to 'Start service'. Consultations logged for ≥90% of services. Upsell attach rate (gel/BIAB/art) tracked against baseline. Redo/complaint rate trending down. Percentage of consultations run in calibrated Table Mode.) — Technician opens 'Today's Clients', picks the booked client or walk-in, or types the client's 4-digit session code to pull her favourites. → Tablet goes on a stand pointing down at the table; the client rests her hand on the printed calibration mat. → App detects the mat, white-balances from the grey patch and reads the mm scale. A 'Calibrated' badge appears. → Client reads the AI consent and taps it herself; the analysis runs on the device. → Technician reviews in-stock AI picks with bilingual talking points ('warm undertone, so gold-based nudes'). → Shortlist plus AI picks are shown in a 4-up grid on the frozen photo. Adjust shape, length, art and per-finger accents. → Price and time estimate updates live (service plus add-ons, OMR to 3 decimals, minutes). → Client picks a look, using 'Help me decide' if torn. → 'Start service' saves the consultation to client history, with an optional after-photo (consented). The WhatsApp/PDF summary is offered.
- **F3 AI Match (skin depth, undertone, nail shape, proportions to recommendations)** (KPI: AI flow completion ≥70% of starters. ≥60% of recommended shades tried. Attach rate ≥35% (final look chosen from the AI set). Manual correction rate under 20%, used as a quality signal for the model.) — AI consent screen: what is analysed and what is not (no face, no age estimation, no upload). Analyse or Skip. → Capture guide: back of the hand, fingers apart, neutral light. Ask 'Do you have henna right now?' Optional mat detection. → Auto-capture when the light, steadiness and all-nails checks pass. → Progressive reveal: depth on the 10-orb Monk scale, then undertone (warm/cool/neutral/olive) with confidence, then nail shape, bed length and finger proportions. → 'Not right? Adjust' lets the user pick an orb or undertone manually, or take a quick gold-vs-silver jewellery test. Recommendations re-rank instantly. → In-stock recommendations in groups: everyday nudes, statement, trending, for your occasion, shape suggestion. → 'Try top 4' opens the grid compare on her own frozen hand.
- **F4 Compare and decide** (KPI: Compare used in ≥40% of sessions with 2 or more favourites. Median time from first favourite to decision ≤90 s. Decisions per compare session ≥0.7.) — Favourites tray (up to 8) then select 2 or 4. → Grid compare on the same frozen frame, cropped to the nails, with synchronized zoom. → Remove a tile (×) to narrow 4 to 3 to 2. → Tap a tile for full screen; split slider against the original or Look A against Look B; hold to peek at bare nails. → Optional per-finger mix on the winner (accent ring finger, French on two). → 'Help me decide' pairwise tournament if still undecided. → Winner goes to Save or Book.
- **F5 Save lookbook, share and ask friends (remote vote)** (KPI: Share rate ≥15% of sessions. Remote vote response rate ≥50% of links. New visitors and bookings attributed to share links (UTM/look code).) — Save the look with an automatic name, e.g. 'Mocha Glaze • Almond • Short'. → Lookbook keeps it on the device by default; optionally save it to the salon profile with a phone number. → Share card generator in 1:1 or 9:16, cropped to hands only, with salon branding and bottle codes. → Web Share with files where supported (Chrome Android 76+, Safari 14+), otherwise download plus a wa.me link. → 'Ask my circle': a vote link (no login, expires in 48 h); friends tap their pick and the tally updates live (Firestore). → Client closes the vote, then books.
- **F6 Online visitor to try to book** (KPI: Visit to try-start ≥20%. Try to booking handoff ≥8%. Handoff to confirmed booking ≥60%. Booking form completion time ≤60 s. Website LCP ≤2.5 s on 4G.) — Lands on Home from Instagram or Maps. Hero has 'Try on my hand' and 'Book'. → Shade library with filters (family, finish, type, price, in-stock, occasion, trend). → Shade detail shows the render, real bottle, swatch stick and renders on 3 skin tones. → 'Try' deep-links into the studio with that shade preselected (the product-page-to-try-on pattern). On desktop, photo upload or webcam. → Save the look, then 'Book this look'. → Services are derived from the look (e.g. Gel + Almond extensions + French) with duration and OMR price. → Pick branch, a date (week starts Saturday) and a time (12-hour, ص/م), plus name, phone (+968 default, LTR field) and notes. → Send through WhatsApp with a prefilled message containing look code and link, or submit a Firebase booking request. → Confirmation shows the look code and offers .ics calendar export.
- **F7 Home-service technician visit (offline)** (KPI: Offline session success ≥95%. Kit hit rate (chosen look available in the kit) ≥90%. Time to decision ≤5 min.) — Before leaving: 'Pack kit', selecting the bottles she is carrying. → App caches the kit catalogue, shaders and hand model; an offline readiness checklist turns green. → At the client's home: lighting check plus optional calibration mat. → Studio shows only kit colours. Freeze, compare, choose. → Record the service and price offline. → Sync when back online and send a WhatsApp summary or receipt.
- **F8 Admin: add or update a bottle (inventory)** (KPI: Time to add a shade ≤2 min. ≥90% of catalogue has a real photo plus calibrated colour. Staff-reported screen-to-bottle mismatches per month trending to 0.) — Inventory, then '+ Add shade'. → Photograph the bottle and its painted swatch stick on the calibration card; the app samples colour (hex/Lab) and suggests a colour family. → Show ΔE between the render and the real swatch photo; tweak finish parameters until they match. → Enter name EN/AR, brand, code, finish, product type, price (OMR), stock and low-stock threshold, tags (trend, occasion, breathable/wudu-friendly shown as the brand's claim) and collections. → Preview on 3 model hands (light, medium, deep). → Publish. The shade appears in the catalogue, the studio and the recommender. Out of stock means it hides automatically and suggests an alternative.
- **F9 Admin: promos and seasonal collections** (KPI: Promo share of try-ons. Promo try-to-booking conversion against non-promo. Time to launch a promo ≤10 min.) — Create a collection (e.g. 'Winter Glaze' using the REF1 ice accent slot). → Pick and order shades (drag, with move-up/down buttons as an alternative). → Schedule it with Gregorian dates plus an optional Hijri display for Ramadan/Eid. → Set a promo price or bundle and a banner image. → Assign it to the website hero and to the studio's 'Today's picks'. → Track tries and bookings from the collection.
- **F10 Returning client: repeat or evolve** (KPI: Rebook rate within 6 weeks. Time to rebook ≤30 s. Share of bookings carrying a look code.) — Client opens her previous WhatsApp summary link, or reception searches her phone number. → History timeline shows past looks on her own hand and after-service photos. → 'Repeat' or 'Seasonal twist' (same undertone logic, current collection). → One-tap booking with the look attached.
- **F11 No-camera path (privacy or unsupported device)** (KPI: ≥60% of camera-denied sessions continue to at least one try-on. Booking conversion parity within 70% of camera sessions.) — Camera declined or WebGL2/camera unsupported. → Offer 'Use a photo' or 'Model hand'. → Shade quiz: Monk orb, gold/silver jewellery, vein colour, occasion/persona. → Recommendations shown on the model hand closest to her tone and nail length. → Keep the 'Try on my real hand' CTA visible; book.
- **F12 Bridal / group board (later)** (KPI: Group bookings per month; average ticket per group booking.) — Bride creates a board and shares a link. → Each member tries looks and pins her favourite. → App suggests coordinated palettes (same family, varied finishes). → Group booking request with all looks attached.

### Reference UI critique

- REF1 'Winter nail salon landing'. STRENGTHS: product-first full-bleed macro of a hand; a coherent ice-blue/silver story; elegant serif headline; friendly pill CTAs; a simple 3-card next-step row. WEAKNESSES: the AI-generated copy is gibberish and even the headline is misspelled ('Winter Hlare'). Body text sits on a busy photo with no scrim. White text on the ice pill computes to about 1.7:1 (fails WCAG 1.4.3). The hands and nails are AI-rendered, which is fatal for a brand promising accuracy: real salon photos are mandatory. The bottom cards have tiny captions (about 10 px) and no visible primary action. The hero CTA is not 'Try on'. The heart icon's meaning is unclear. A seasonal theme is used as the permanent identity. There is no Arabic/RTL consideration. TAKE: real macro photography as hero, ice blue as a SEASONAL accent and as the 'live' signal, pill shapes, a tiny sparkle glyph as a brand mark. LEAVE: text on photos without a scrim, low-contrast pills, AI hands, gibberish.
- REF2 '10 Graphic Effects'. This is an effects catalogue, not a UI, so it should be read as a menu of options. The warm off-white (#EEEAE4) is a good neutral base. Glass, soft drop shadow, warm glow, inner shadow, satin and texture are usable if rationed. Neumorphism and the subtle inner-shadow tiles have about 1.1-1.2:1 boundary contrast (fails 3:1). Bevel & emboss is dated. The purple→orange gradient is off-brand and would distort colour perception next to swatches. The blue outer glow can be reused only as the live-tracking signal.
- REF3 'My storehouse' 3D dashboard. STRENGTHS: the floating bottom toolbar with a dark circular primary action is excellent thumb ergonomics and maps directly to the try-on Freeze button. List rows with thumbnails are efficient for inventory. A bold shape creates focus. WEAKNESSES: electric purple (#7B4DFF) clashes with a luxury nail palette and, as a large field, biases colour judgement. Icons have no labels. Grey labels are tiny and low-contrast. 3D renders are expensive to produce and heavy to load. It is a Dribbble-style concept with unclear affordances and a desktop-panel layout. TAKE: the dock plus central action, list-row inventory, the blob as a static website motif, purple toned down to Orchid for AI only. LEAVE: unlabelled icons, saturated purple surfaces, 3D renders in tools.
- REF4 'Luxury editorial'. STRENGTHS: a convincing premium mood; the chocolate→taupe gradient matches the brown/mocha direction (Pantone 2025 'Mocha Mousse', UNVERIFIED this session); large serif headings; layered cards and satin textures add depth. WEAKNESSES: placeholder gibberish headings ('Herniur', 'Panling', 'Cedling'); stacked glass cards over busy imagery cut legibility; very low-contrast small body text; overlapping cards get cramped on phones; heavy layering is slow on mid-range Android; a mostly brown UI around the nails can make warm nudes look muddy (surround effect). TAKE: cocoa/mocha brand colours, editorial serif voice, satin imagery for website sections, the Cocoa→Taupe gradient on editorial cards. LEAVE: stacked glass over imagery, low-contrast captions, brown surrounds inside the try-on views.
- REF5 'Shop All Nails' e-commerce. STRENGTHS: clear IA with categories, price range, colour dots and shape filter, a 3×3 grid with price, a hero banner. WEAKNESSES: desktop-first left sidebar; colour dots without labels (fails colour-blind users and WCAG 1.4.1, and is ambiguous across languages); tiny product text; product photos look alike (press-on sets) so comparing is hard; 'Add to cart' is the wrong primary action for a salon (try and book are); beige-on-beige is low contrast; no stock indicator; no 'Try on' entry. TAKE: the filter taxonomy (family, finish, shape, length, price, in-stock, occasion), grid density, hero promo banner. LEAVE: sidebar on mobile, unlabelled dots, add-to-cart primacy.
- OVERALL: all five are AI-generated concept shots. They are attractive but unvalidated, and NONE of them shows a camera try-on UI, which is the product's core. Mixing five styles risks incoherence, so the proposal turns them into one token system. Neutrals and type are fixed; one seasonal accent slot changes; effects are rationed.
- CURRENT BUILD (Porcelain & Glaze tokens in tailwind.config.js and src/index.css), honest audit: (a) the focus ring #C8A97E on Porcelain is 2.02:1, failing the 3:1 non-text minimum; use an Ink double ring on light and Champagne/Glaze on dark. (b) .glass-dark at 0.55 alpha gives 4.28:1 for white and 3.87:1 for porcelain text in the worst case over a bright frame; raise it to 0.70 or more. (c) .btn-glass (white/10 with white text) can fall to near 1:1 over bright camera frames; use Glass Dark at 0.70. (d) .chip min-height is 40 px; raise it to 44 px (AAA 2.5.5). (e) Manrope Latin paired with IBM Plex Sans Arabic gives mismatched metrics; switch to IBM Plex Sans. (f) Cormorant Garamond's hairlines are fragile under bright light below 24 px. (g) taxonomy.js hard-codes Arabic-Indic digits ('٢–٣ أسابيع'); format with Intl from a global digit setting. (h) Warning #B7791F gives 3.30:1 as text; use #8A5A12. The REF1-5 mapping documented in the token file is a good start; this audit tightens accessibility and accuracy.

### Accessibility & RTL

- TARGET SIZE: WCAG 2.2 SC 2.5.8 (AA) requires 24×24 CSS px or equivalent spacing; SC 2.5.5 (AAA) requires 44×44. Adopt 44×44 everywhere. Camera primary button 72 px, dock buttons 56 px, swatches 52 px visible with a 56 px hit area, chips 44 px tall (current .chip is 40 px), steppers 44 px, list rows 48 px. Keep 8 px or more between targets.
- CONTRAST: text 4.5:1 or more (SC 1.4.3), large text 3:1. Target 7:1 for camera-overlay text and primary body because of salon glare. Non-text 3:1 or more (SC 1.4.11) for input borders, focus rings and the swatch selection ring. Use the computed palette pairs; never put text on Taupe, Glaze or Champagne except Ink on their fills.
- FOCUS: visible focus with a 2 px Ink ring plus a 2 px white outer ring on light, and a Champagne or Glaze ring on dark. The current Champagne ring on Porcelain is 2.02:1 and fails. Sticky dock and tray must not hide focused elements (SC 2.4.11): set scroll-padding-bottom to the dock height.
- COLOUR IS NEVER THE ONLY CUE (SC 1.4.1), which is critical in a colour app. Every swatch has a name, family and finish icon. Selection uses ring plus check plus a screen-reader label. Stock state uses text plus icon. Filter colour dots always carry labels.
- DRAGGING AND GESTURES: the split slider, per-finger assignment, collection ordering and fine-tune handles all have tap or button alternatives (SC 2.5.7). Pinch zoom has +/- buttons (SC 2.5.1). Any tilt-to-see-shimmer has a slider alternative and can be switched off (SC 2.5.4).
- ORIENTATION AND REFLOW: support portrait and landscape (SC 1.3.4); the tablet on a stand is landscape. Safari has no Screen Orientation lock anyway, so design for both. Reflow at 320 CSS px (SC 1.4.10). Use rem sizing so OS text scaling works.
- SCREEN READERS: polite aria-live announcements such as 'Applied Mocha Mousse, gel gloss, almond, short' and 'Hand found, 5 nails' / 'Hand lost, move closer'. Give the canvas a text alternative summarizing the current look. Label every icon button in both languages. Set the lang attribute on mixed-language fragments.
- PHOTOSENSITIVITY: glitter, holographic and chrome shader animation must not produce more than 3 flashes per second over large areas (WCAG 2.3.1). No white flash on capture.
- USER PREFERENCES: honour prefers-reduced-motion (Chrome 74, Firefox 63, Safari 10.1) and prefers-contrast (Chrome 96, Firefox 101, Safari 14.1, mapped to a high-contrast token set). Add in-app 'Solid panels' and 'Reduce motion' toggles, because prefers-reduced-transparency is Chrome 118+ only (Firefox behind a flag, Safari unsupported).
- BRIGHT SALON LIGHT: body text 16 px or larger, weights 400 or heavier, no mid-grey text lighter than Ink Muted. The studio UI is dark with Glass Dark at 0.70 alpha or more. A 'High-contrast studio' toggle swaps glass for solid Night 800. Kiosk setup checklist: fixed high brightness, auto-brightness off, Night Shift/True Tone off for colour consistency, matte anti-glare screen protector.
- ONE-HAND USE: the subject hand is busy, so every frequent action sits in the bottom 40% thumb zone. A handedness switch moves the dock and tray to the left or right edge, independent of language. Nothing critical goes only in the top corners; the close and back actions are duplicated in the dock. Sheets dismiss with swipe-down and also have a visible close button.
- TECHNICIANS WITH GLOVED OR WET HANDS: no hover-dependent UI, large targets, 500 ms long-press threshold, no precise drags. Consultation controls are 56 px.
- OLDER AND LOW-VISION CLIENTS: 'Large Studio' mode with 1.25× type, 2 swatches per row, high contrast, and optional speechSynthesis readout of shade names (support exists in major browsers, but Arabic voice availability depends on the OS; UNVERIFIED per device).
- AUTHENTICATION: staff sign-in by Google, magic link or passkey; PIN switching allows paste and password managers; no CAPTCHA puzzles (SC 3.3.8).
- HAPTICS AND KIOSK APIs: navigator.vibrate is unsupported in Safari, so never rely on haptics. Screen Wake Lock is in Chrome 84+, Firefox 126+ and Safari 16.4+ (iOS listed as 18.4 in MDN data), for the tablet consultation and kiosk. iOS Fullscreen API is partial (iPad only), so use the PWA standalone display plus iPad Guided Access for the reception kiosk.
- PRIVACY UX: camera-on indicator always visible; frames processed on the device; saved images cropped to the hand; auto-reset the kiosk after 90 s idle; explicit consent before AI analysis; no age inference. Oman PDPL (Royal Decree 6/2022 and its executive regulations) likely treats biometric data as sensitive. This is UNVERIFIED here and needs legal review.
- RTL BASE: set dir='rtl' and lang='ar' on <html>, never with CSS direction (W3C i18n). Use logical properties everywhere (margin-inline-start, inset-inline-end, text-align: start); Tailwind ms-/me-/ps-/pe-/start-/end- utilities. Logical properties are supported since Safari/iOS 12.2.
- MIRROR IN RTL: navigation order, back/forward chevrons, carousels and trays (first item at the right), steppers and progress direction, breadcrumbs, swipe directions, toast slide-in direction, sidebar side, dock button order (the shutter stays centred), arrow glyphs next to text (e.g. [dir=rtl] .icon-dir { transform: scaleX(-1) }).
- DO NOT MIRROR: the camera preview and photos (they show the physical world; front-camera mirroring is a separate setting), illustrations of real hands, brand logos and the WhatsApp icon, media/play icons, symmetric nail-shape icons, numbers and phone numbers (always LTR runs), the camera-switch icon.
- FINGER ORDER IS PHYSICAL: per-finger controls are anchored to the nails detected in the image, and the thumb's position comes from the detected handedness. Never re-order fingers by reading direction.
- SPLIT SLIDER IN RTL: the original (before) sits on the right and the new look (after) on the left. Arrow keys follow the visual direction. Test with VoiceOver and TalkBack.
- DIGITS (verified in Node 22 / ICU 77.1 / CLDR 47): Intl defaults ar-OM, ar-SA, ar-QA, ar-KW, ar-BH and ar-EG to Arabic-Indic digits (١٢٣), while plain 'ar', 'ar-AE' and 'ar-MA' default to Western digits. Always pass an explicit numbering system from one global setting (e.g. 'ar-OM-u-nu-latn' or 'ar-OM-u-nu-arab'). Never hard-code digits in strings. Never mix digit systems on one screen. Phone numbers, OTPs, look codes and SKUs are always Western digits inside an LTR-isolated span. Recommended default: Western digits for commerce, with a ١٢٣ toggle; validate with Omani users.
- CURRENCY, TIME, CALENDAR (verified with Intl): OMR has 3 decimals ('OMR 12.500' in en-OM, '١٢٫٥٠٠ ر.ع.' in ar-OM). ar-OM and en-OM use a 12-hour clock (ص/م in Arabic). Week info: firstDay = Saturday, weekend = Friday and Saturday, so booking calendars start on Saturday. An optional Hijri (islamic-umalqura) date can sit beside the Gregorian date for Ramadan/Eid promos; Intl supports it.
- BIDI ISOLATION: wrap brand and shade names in Latin inside Arabic text (e.g. 'OPI Big Apple Red') in <bdi>. User-entered names and notes get dir='auto'. Phone inputs get dir='ltr' and inputmode='tel'. Let Intl place percent signs (W3C ALReq notes ٪ placement differs with Arabic-Indic digits).
- ARABIC TYPOGRAPHY (W3C ALReq): no letter-spacing (Arabic stretching is not letter-spacing, and tracking breaks joins); no uppercase transforms; no synthetic italics; no justified UI text (justifying Arabic properly needs kashida and careful handling). Line-height 1.6-1.8 because Arabic ascenders and descenders extend much further than Latin. Arabic size 1-2 px larger than Latin in the same role. Leave about 30% slack for label length differences, and do not truncate mid-word.
- CAMERA UI IN RTL: the shutter stays centred. The tray starts at the right. The torch and camera-switch positions mirror but their icons do not. Coach text in Arabic sits above the hand outline, never over the nails. The hand outline shows the hand the user selected, whatever the language.
- GENDERED ARABIC COPY: customer-facing strings can use the feminine imperative usual for women's salons (the existing copy uses 'جرّبي'). Staff and admin UI uses neutral noun forms ('حجز موعد', 'إضافة لون'). Keep one dictionary key per string so wording can change if the salon serves men.
- ADMIN TABLES AND CHARTS IN RTL: numeric columns use tabular numerals and end alignment. Choose one convention for time-axis direction in charts (mirrored right→left or LTR throughout), apply it consistently, and test with Omani staff. Values themselves stay LTR.

## Competitors & market

IMPORTANT CAVEAT: live verification was not possible in this run. The workflow's shared WebSearch budget (200 calls per turn) was already used up when this agent started, and the egress proxy refused direct fetches of every primary source I tried (perfectcorp.com, docs.snap.com, banuba.com, deepar.ai all returned 403 / connect_rejected). Everything below comes from model knowledge (cutoff around mid-2026) and is tagged by confidence. Every price, date and market figure should be re-checked against the URLs in "sources" before it goes into customer-facing material or a pitch. Hex values are not given here: real-product hexes taken from swatch photos are approximations at best.

KEY FINDINGS
(1) No product on the market joins nail try-on with a salon's own shelf, its consultation, its upsells and its booking. The field splits into four groups:
- Consumer apps (YouCam Nails, generic AR apps) use generic colour palettes.
- Brand try-ons (essie via L'Oreal ModiFace, OPI, Nails Inc, the old Sally Hansen ManiMatch) show only one brand's catalogue, are built for e-commerce, and are often geo-limited.
- B2B AR SDKs (Perfect Corp, Banuba, DeepAR, Snap Camera Kit) are paid, or tied to a platform, and none of them handle salon operations.
- Salon software (Fresha, Booksy, Vagaro, Zenoti, Mindbody, GlossGenius, Glamera) has booking, POS and client records but, as far as I know, no nail visualisation at all.
(2) Correction to the brief: as far as I know, Snap did NOT buy Wannaby (maker of Wanna Nails and Wanna Kicks). Wannaby built Snapchat Lenses for brands such as Gucci, which is likely where the link comes from. I recall that Perfect Corp announced a deal to acquire Wannaby around early 2025 (medium-low confidence; verify on ir.perfectcorp.com). Snap also shut down its AR Enterprise / Shopping Suite business (ARES) in 2023, which signals that Snap has stepped back from B2B try-on.
(3) Snap Camera Kit (web SDK published on npm as @snap/camera-kit) has, to my knowledge, carried no licence fee. It does require Snap's approval and acceptance of Snap's terms, Lenses built in Lens Studio, and data flowing to Snap. Use it only as an optional marketing channel (Snapchat is very strong in the Gulf), not as the core engine.
(4) The app's real moat is a CALIBRATED, salon-specific workflow: in-stock bottles scanned by barcode and colour-calibrated against a printed reference card; previews under different lighting (salon, daylight, wedding hall); per-finger comparison; upsell layers with live prices in OMR; a stored "Nail Passport" of each consultation; and WhatsApp click-to-chat sharing, booking and reminders. All of it is Arabic-first and processed on-device for privacy in women-only salons. Nobody can deliver "100% accurate", competitors included, and none of them claims it. The honest, defensible pitch is "calibrated preview with a confidence indicator plus a swatch-verify step".
(5) GCC and Oman specifics that global tools miss:
- Bookings run through Instagram discovery and WhatsApp.
- Ladies' salons are women-only and segregated.
- Home-service nail technicians are a large, mostly informal segment.
- Prices are in OMR with 3 decimals (baisa).
- Demand peaks around Eids, weddings and henna nights, and Oman National Day (18 November 2026, less than 4 weeks after today's date, so it is an immediate campaign opportunity).
- Some customers want breathable / "prayer-friendly" polish.
- Oman's Personal Data Protection Law (Royal Decree 6/2022) applies.
(6) Do NOT estimate age automatically from hands. It is unreliable, culturally sensitive, and risky under the PDPL. Offer an optional age band or "style persona" that the customer chooses herself.

READY NOW vs ENABLE LATER (per the user's instruction to make the app fully ready now)
- Can ship today with free, on-device tech: barcode-to-shelf sync, calibration card, light preview, per-finger compare, upsell price layers, Nail Passport, WhatsApp share / booking / reminders via wa.me links, QR kiosk, CSV/PDF reports, occasion collections.
- Needs an owner decision later, built as switches that are off by default:
  - Firebase Blaze plan for photo storage. Cloud Storage for Firebase moved behind the Blaze plan starting Oct 2024, with Spark access to default buckets ending around Feb 2026 (verify). Until then, keep images on the device or store tiny thumbnails in Firestore.
  - A payment gateway for deposits (Thawani, OmanNet etc. charge fees).
  - A Snapchat Lens.
  - Distributor sponsorship deals.

BUSINESS MODEL: SaaS priced per salon per month in OMR, with a cheap solo / home-technician tier, is the best fit. Start as the owner's own-salon tool to prove the KPIs, then sell to other salons. Add distributor sponsorship once there is scale.
