### Market gaps

- No product links try-on to the salon's own in-stock bottles. Brand try-ons show one brand; consumer apps show generic palettes; salon software has inventory but no colour data. A salon carrying OPI, The GelBottle, Gelish and local Chinese gel brands at once has no single visual catalogue.
- None of the major salon platforms (Fresha, Booksy, Vagaro, Zenoti, Mindbody, GlossGenius, Glamera) offers nail visualisation or visual consultation, to my knowledge.
- No consultation workflow that runs try-on, then a saved record (shade, shape, length, finish, technician, products, allergies), then booking, then a reminder, then rebooking. Today it is a WhatsApp chat and the technician's memory.
- Upsell visualisation is missing: chrome powder, cat-eye/velvet magnetic gel, French/micro-French, ombre/aura, glitter top, 3D charms and extensions are not shown on the customer's own hand with the price difference (OMR) and extra time.
- Colour accuracy cannot be verified anywhere: no calibration reference, no confidence indicator, no 'verify with a real swatch' step, no preview under other lighting (salon LED vs Muscat daylight vs warm wedding-hall light).
- No Arabic-first (Gulf dialect), RTL, OMR 3-decimal pricing (baisa) product exists; global tools treat Arabic as an afterthought.
- No WhatsApp-native flows (share a look, ask family or friends, confirm a booking, send reminders through free wa.me click-to-chat). Global software assumes email, SMS or its own app.
- Women-only salon privacy: no tool guarantees hand-only capture (no face), on-device processing and no cloud upload by default.
- Home-service nail technicians (a large informal Instagram/WhatsApp segment) have no lightweight mobile tool for portfolio, try-on, quote and booking link.
- Occasion planning (bridal with henna, Eid al-Fitr, Eid al-Adha, Oman National Day on 18 November, graduation, wedding season, Khareef travel) is not supported with curated in-stock collections.
- Breathable / water-permeable ('prayer-friendly' / halal-certified) polish is not labelled or filterable in any try-on tool. Salons should declare product claims themselves; the app should not make religious rulings.
- Affordability: B2B AR is priced for multinational brands; nothing is priced for a single salon in OMR, and nothing works offline on a budget Android tablet.
- Skin-tone and undertone recommendations, where they exist, analyse the FACE. Nail colour is judged against the HAND's skin, which differs in tone and in visible veins, yet nothing analyses the hand with calibration.
- Nails are not measured at salon level to size press-on tips and plan extension length (Mani-Me does it DTC only, in the UK).
- No tracking of gel/HEMA allergy and product history inside the consultation record, although gel (meth)acrylate allergy is a documented and growing issue.
- No built-in group decision tools: customers in the GCC often consult mothers, sisters and friends, and bridal parties coordinate looks, but no tool supports multi-person voting or coordination.
- Salons and GCC distributors have no data on which shades are tried vs actually booked vs reordered, so buying decisions are guesswork.
- Brands and distributors in Oman have no point-of-consultation channel to feature new shades. Sponsorship inventory does not exist yet.

### GCC market notes

- Data caveat: none of these notes could be checked live in this run (web search budget used up, proxy denied fetches). Treat numbers as estimates to verify via NCSI, DataReportal, the Muscat Municipality and on-the-ground calls to salons.
- Oman population is roughly 5.2-5.3 million (NCSI, 2025), of whom about 40-43% are expatriates. Muscat Governorate has about 1.4-1.6 million people (UNVERIFIED; check ncsi.gov.om).
- Currency: the Omani rial is pegged at about 0.3845 OMR per US$1 (1 OMR is about US$2.60). Prices use 3 decimals (1 OMR = 1000 baisa), so the UI must format like 12.500 OMR / 12.500 ر.ع. VAT of 5% has applied since 16 April 2021, so show prices VAT-inclusive or exclusive consistently.
- Indicative Muscat ladies' salon prices (ESTIMATES, verify by calling 10-15 salons and checking Instagram price lists):
- Classic manicure: about 5-10 OMR
- Gel polish manicure: about 10-18 OMR
- Gel removal: about 2-5 OMR
- Builder gel/BIAB overlay: about 15-25 OMR
- Acrylic or gel extensions full set: about 20-35 OMR (premium salons 40+ OMR); refills about 15-25 OMR
- Nail art: about 0.5-2 OMR per nail (complex 3D more)
- Chrome or cat-eye add-on: about 2-5 OMR
- Pedicure: about 8-15 OMR (gel pedicure about 12-20 OMR)
- Mani-pedi combo: about 15-28 OMR
- Bridal nail packages: often 30-60+ OMR
- Home-service call-out surcharge: about 3-5 OMR
- For comparison, Dubai gel manicures commonly run about AED 100-180 (about 10.5-19 OMR).
- Structure: many small independent ladies' salons (often Omani-owned, expat-managed and staffed) in Al Khuwair, Qurum, Al Ghubra, Bawshar, Al Mouj, Seeb and Al Amerat, plus mall-based and hotel spa salons and a few regional chains. The number of licensed ladies' salons in Muscat is UNVERIFIED (plausibly several hundred); count with Google Maps / municipality data.
- Women-only salons: ladies' salons in Oman are gender-segregated by custom and municipal rules. Men generally cannot enter, and windows and doors are usually frosted or covered. Implications: (a) on-site demos and sales need a female rep or an owner meeting outside the floor, plus demo videos; (b) customers are sensitive about cameras, so frame hands only, never capture faces, and process on-device; (c) staff photos and portfolios need consent.
- Workforce: salon technicians are mostly expatriate women (commonly from the Philippines, India, Indonesia, Morocco, Lebanon/Syria and others). Staff UI should default to English with simple wording; customer UI should be Arabic-first. Omanisation rules restrict some occupations to Omani nationals, and the 'commercial concealment' (hidden expatriate ownership) issue exists. Check current Ministry of Labour decisions on salon roles (UNVERIFIED).
- Home-service nail technicians: a large, partly informal segment working through Instagram and WhatsApp (some licensed via home-business permits through the Invest Easy portal, many not). They need a mobile-first solo mode: portfolio, try-on on the client's hand at home, quotes in OMR, booking link. This could be a separate cheap pricing tier.
- Social channels: Instagram is the main discovery channel for salons and nail art (Arabic hashtags such as #مناكير #اظافر #نيل_ارت #مسقط #صالونات_مسقط). Snapchat has extremely high reach in the Gulf (Oman and KSA among the highest per-capita markets). TikTok is growing fast. Google Maps reviews drive local search. WhatsApp (mostly the free WhatsApp Business app, with catalogue and quick replies) is the default booking and confirmation channel. Phone calls remain common.
- WhatsApp constraint: the WhatsApp Business Platform (API) is paid per message (Meta moved to per-message pricing in 2025; verify). The app should use only free click-to-chat links (https://wa.me/<number>?text=...) and the share sheet, with pre-filled bilingual templates.
- Fresha in Oman: Muscat salons appear on Fresha (to be verified, along with whether Fresha payments are live in Oman). Most Muscat salons still book through WhatsApp or Instagram DM. Offer a 'Book on Fresha' link field for salons already on it instead of competing.
- Payments and deposits: cash and card POS dominate. Bank transfer and the local debit network (OmanNet) are common. Apple Pay is available in Oman (verify banks), and local gateways (e.g., Thawani, bank gateways) charge fees. Deposits are normal for bridal, home service and big extension sets, less so for regular manicures. Start with manual deposit tracking.
- Seasonality and occasions:
- Eid al-Fitr and Eid al-Adha cause the biggest booking peaks; salons are fully booked 3-7 days before.
- Ramadan shifts demand to evenings and nights.
- Wedding season runs roughly through the cooler months (about Oct-Apr), with henna nights.
- Oman National Day, 18 November, brings red/white/green themed nails (18 Nov 2026 is less than 4 weeks after today).
- Graduation season.
- Summer (Jul-Aug) is slower in Muscat as families travel; Khareef in Salalah runs about Jun-Sep.
- Approximate dates (verify against the official Hijri calendar): Ramadan 2027 begins about 8 Feb 2027; Eid al-Fitr 2027 about 10 Mar 2027; Eid al-Adha 2027 about 16-17 May 2027.
- Culture and religion: some practising Muslim customers avoid regular polish because it blocks water during wudu (ablution). Breathable or water-permeable and 'halal-certified' polishes (marketed by brands such as Inglot O2M, Orly Breathable, Tuesday in Love, 786 Cosmetics; claims vary, so verify) have a real niche. Many customers choose gel or polish timing around their prayer practices. The app should let salons LABEL products ('breathable', 'halal-certified per manufacturer') without making religious claims itself.
- Hands are a key fashion expression in the Gulf: with abayas common, nails, henna, rings and handbags carry much of the visible style. Henna (bridal and Eid) interacts with nail colour choices. Popular local preferences (anecdotal, verify): nudes and milky shades for everyday and work, deep reds and burgundy for evenings and weddings, gold/chrome accents, French tips, almond and coffin/ballerina shapes for extensions.
- Global 2025-2026 nail trends to reflect in curated sets (verify with brand trend reports): chrome and 'glazed' finishes, cat-eye/velvet magnetic gel, aura/airbrush nails, micro-French, jelly/sheer tints, milky/'milk bath', 'mocha' browns (Pantone Colour of the Year 2025 was Mocha Mousse 17-1230), cherry and burgundy reds, butter yellow, olive and sage, tortoiseshell, 3D/blooming gel art, short natural squoval for everyday. I believe Pantone's 2026 Colour of the Year is 'Cloud Dancer' (a soft white, PANTONE 11-4201), announced Dec 2025 (medium confidence, verify).
- Data protection: Oman's Personal Data Protection Law (Royal Decree 6/2022), in force from Feb 2023, with Executive Regulations issued by the ministry for transport, communications and IT (MTCIT) in 2024 (Ministerial Decision 34/2024, verify). It requires explicit consent, purpose limitation, data-subject rights and safeguards; biometric, health and genetic data are sensitive and need extra permissions. Hand photos are personal data; allergy notes are health data. Use explicit bilingual consent, on-device processing by default, retention limits and a deletion option.
- Age estimation request: avoid automated age inference from hands. No reliable free on-device model exists, accuracy is poor, it is culturally sensitive (customers may find it offensive), and under the PDPL it adds risk. Instead let the customer optionally choose an age band or style persona ('classic / trendy / bold / minimal') to steer recommendations.
- Cosmetics regulation: nail products in Oman fall under GCC/GSO cosmetic standards and municipal health licensing of salons (hygiene, sterilisation, ventilation). The app can support compliance with sterilisation logs and product batch records, but this is optional (verify current Muscat Municipality salon requirements).
- Digital adoption: Oman internet penetration is about 95%+ and smartphone use is near-universal (DataReportal Digital 2025 Oman; verify). Mid-range Android phones and iPhones are both common; salon tablets are often budget Android devices. Performance targets must include low-end Android Chrome and iOS Safari (where the BarcodeDetector API is not available, so fall back to ZXing).
- Market size (UNVERIFIED estimates; use cautiously):
- Global nail salon services market: roughly US$10-12 billion in the early 2020s, growing about 5-7% a year (Grand View Research-type reports).
- Global nail polish market: roughly US$13-17 billion.
- Global virtual try-on market: high single-digit US$ billions by 2025, with forecasts of 20%+ yearly growth.
- Oman beauty and personal care market: roughly US$0.5-0.8 billion (Statista-type estimate).
- No reliable public figure exists for Oman nail services. Bottom-up estimate: about 300-600 ladies' salons in Muscat x 10-25 nail services a day x about 10-15 OMR is very roughly tens of millions of OMR a year, which needs validation.
- Competition in GCC nail chains: UAE chains (e.g., Tips & Toes, N.Bar, Nail Station, Nstyle) set premium standards and some may operate in Oman (verify). They are both white-label prospects and benchmarks for service menus (e.g., 'BIAB', 'Russian manicure', 'gel extensions', 'chrome', 'cat eye').
- Language and UX: use Gulf Arabic marketing copy (e.g., 'جربي اللون على يدك' = 'try the colour on your hand'), proper RTL layout, Arabic shade names, and optional Arabic-Indic digits. Mirror direction-dependent icons. Check Arabic typography (e.g., Tajawal / IBM Plex Sans Arabic / Noto Kufi Arabic from Google Fonts).

### Business models

- **Own-salon tool (the owner's salon(s) first)** — pros: No customer-acquisition cost; immediate revenue impact through upsells, fewer redos and rebooking; full control over features and data; produces the case study and KPIs (conversion, ticket uplift) needed to sell to others; can run entirely on free tiers at one salon's volume. · cons: No recurring software revenue; growth limited to own branches; development and maintenance cost lands on one business; risk of building only for one salon's quirks.
- **SaaS per salon per month in OMR (tiered)** — pros: Recurring revenue; affordable entry for small salons. Indicative tiers (to validate):
- Solo / home technician: about 4-7 OMR/month
- Salon (up to 5 staff): about 12-19 OMR/month
- Pro / multi-branch: about 35-60 OMR per branch per month
- Annual prepay with 2 months free
- One-off setup and training: about 20-50 OMR

On-device AI keeps the marginal cost near zero. Comparable to or cheaper than Fresha's reported about US$20/month (about 7.7 OMR). · cons: Needs sales (women-only salons mean female reps or owner meetings), onboarding (calibrating shelf bottles takes time) and Arabic/English support. Billing via bank transfer is manual without a paid gateway. A multi-tenant Firebase setup will exceed Spark free quotas (Firestore about 50K reads / 20K writes per day; Cloud Storage now needs the Blaze plan, verify), so a pay-as-you-go Blaze account is needed at scale. Churn risk if KPIs aren't visible.
- **White-label for chains, brands or distributors** — pros: Large contracts (e.g., setup 500-3,000 OMR plus monthly licence); a chain's own branding and colours; distributors could give it to the salons they supply as a loyalty tool; one sale covers many outlets. · cons: Custom feature requests and longer sales cycles; brand-specific catalogues conflict with multi-brand salon shelves; support SLAs; may need separate deployments or Firebase projects per client.
- **Brand / distributor sponsorship and placement** — pros: Monetises without charging salons (the tool can be free or cheap for salons, speeding adoption). 'Featured shade' or new-collection slots, sampling campaigns and anonymised aggregated trend reports (tried vs booked shades in Muscat) are valuable to GCC distributors of OPI, CND, Gelish, The GelBottle, Kiko and others. · cons: Needs scale (dozens of salons) before brands pay; neutrality conflicts with salon recommendations, so sponsorship must be clearly labelled; PDPL compliance for any data product (aggregation, consent); brand approval processes are slow.
- **Booking commission / marketplace** — pros: Revenue aligned with value delivered (e.g., 5-10% of the first booking, or a flat 0.5-1 OMR per new client from the public site); the public website and try-on become a discovery channel for consumers. · cons: Hard to enforce: Omani bookings happen in WhatsApp DMs and are paid in cash at the salon. Collecting commissions needs a payment gateway (paid) or manual invoicing. Competes with Fresha and Instagram; salons dislike commissions (Fresha's 20% new-client fee is a known pain point).
- **Hardware kiosk bundle (tablet + stand + ring light + printed calibration cards) plus subscription** — pros: One-off margin (bundle about 80-150 OMR); controlled lighting greatly improves accuracy; strong in-salon visibility ('try-on station'); easy upsell to the salon tier. · cons: Inventory, logistics and warranty; hardware support burden; capital tied up.
- **Freemium consumer app / public website leading customers to salons** — pros: Top-of-funnel: consumers try looks at home, then the app routes them to partner salons or home technicians; builds a brand and dataset; free features attract organic traffic around Eid and National Day. · cons: Consumer monetisation is weak; competes with free YouCam and similar apps; without calibration at home, accuracy drops, which hurts the 'accurate' promise; customer-support load.
- **Retail add-on: pre-sized press-on kits and take-home products** — pros: Uses nail measurement to sell custom-sized press-ons or care kits through the salon (margin per kit roughly 3-10 OMR); revenue between visits. · cons: Physical product sourcing and quality control; stock risk; not core software; customers may see it as cannibalising salon services.
- **Training and certification (digital consultation and colour-matching course for nail technicians)** — pros: Additional revenue (e.g., 15-40 OMR per technician); improves adoption and accuracy; builds community and credibility. · cons: Content creation effort; translation into English and Arabic (and possibly Tagalog/Hindi for staff); time from the expert team.
- **Hybrid recommended path** — pros: Phase-free in practice: launch now as the owner's salon tool with every free feature on. Open the SaaS signup on the public site at the same time (Solo / Salon / Pro in OMR, manual billing). Add the sponsorship and kiosk bundle once 20+ salons are live. Each stage reuses the same codebase, with switches for paid integrations (gateway, Blaze storage, Snapchat Lens). · cons: Needs a clean multi-tenant data model from day one (salonId on every document, security rules) and disciplined use of feature switches; slightly more upfront engineering.

## Platform, free tier & privacy law

VERIFICATION STATUS. I could not check anything on the live web in this run. (1) WebSearch refused: the 200-search budget shared by every agent in this turn was already used up. (2) WebFetch failed on every domain with DNS errors (getaddrinfo ENOTFOUND for firebase.google.com, developer.mozilla.org and mtcit.gov.om). (3) curl through the agent proxy was refused by egress policy (CONNECT 403 for firebase.google.com and webkit.org). So every fact below comes from model knowledge (cutoff mid-2026), plus direct checks of the project's installed packages: @mediapipe/tasks-vision 0.10.35, firebase 12.19.0, and the downloaded hand_landmarker.task. Items tagged [VERIFY] are ones I am not sure of. The URLs in "sources" are the primary pages to re-check; none of them was fetched in this run. To finish verification, send a follow-up message after this reply (a new turn gets a fresh search budget), or raise CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_WEB_SEARCHES_PER_SESSION.

KEY CONCLUSIONS.
(a) The Firebase Spark plan works for this product if Firestore, Auth (email/Google/anonymous) and Hosting are the only services used. Cloud Storage for Firebase has needed the Blaze plan for new default buckets since 30 Oct 2024. Since early Feb 2026 it is needed for any access at all (the deadline moved from 30 Oct 2025 to about 2–3 Feb 2026 [VERIFY exact day]). Today (24 Oct 2026) a Spark project has no usable Storage. Cloud Functions also need Blaze, and phone/SMS auth is paid per SMS or not available on Spark.
(b) The hidden limit is Hosting bandwidth: 360 MB/day on Spark. I measured the self-hosted MediaPipe assets: hand_landmarker.task is 7.8 MB (5.8 MB gzipped) and vision_wasm_internal.wasm is 11.2 MB (3.3 MB gzipped). A first visit therefore costs about 10–20 MB, so only about 20–35 new devices a day fit before Hosting is capped. The service worker's CacheFirst rule helps returning visitors only. Move the model and wasm to jsDelivr, Google's mediapipe-models bucket or Cloudflare Pages, or host the whole static site on Cloudflare Pages.
(c) Firestore free quota: 1 GiB stored, 50k reads/day, 20k writes/day, 20k deletes/day, 10 GiB egress/month, 1 MiB per document. Quotas reset at midnight Pacific time, which is about 11:00–12:00 Muscat time. A Spark project that hits a quota stops working for the rest of that day, so App Check and tight security rules are needed.
(d) Photos: store nothing by default (see photo_storage_decision).
(e) Camera: getUserMedia works in iOS Safari (iOS 11+), iOS home-screen web apps (13.4+), iOS WKWebView-based browsers (14.3+), Android Chrome and Samsung Internet. Instagram, Facebook, Snapchat and TikTok in-app browsers are unreliable. Detect them by user agent and capability check, send Android users out with an intent:// link, show iOS users an "Open in Safari" overlay, and always offer the native photo picker (input type=file with capture=environment) as a fallback. Hardware controls (torch, focus, white balance, exposure) are fairly complete on Android Chrome but sparse on iOS. Never depend on them; use an in-frame white/grey reference card for color accuracy instead.
(f) ML runtimes: WebGL2 is everywhere. WebGPU ships in Chrome desktop (113+), Chrome Android (121+, Android 12+) and Safari 26 (iOS/iPadOS/macOS 26, Sept 2025); Firefox is partial and Samsung Internet is unclear. MediaPipe Tasks Vision 0.10.35 is single-threaded: its wasm loader has no SharedArrayBuffer and its GPU path is WebGL2. So COOP/COEP cross-origin isolation is not needed today. Turning it on would break signInWithPopup, YouTube, Instagram and Maps embeds, and possibly the reCAPTCHA-based App Check.
(g) Law: Oman's PDPL (Royal Decree 6/2022, in force 13 Feb 2023; Executive Regulation Ministerial Decision 34/2024 with a one-year grace period to about Feb 2025) bans processing of genetic, biometric, health and ethnic-origin data (among others) without a Ministry (MTCIT) permit. It also requires explicit consent, guardian consent for children, breach notification (72 h per the ER [VERIFY]) and controlled cross-border transfers. A hand photo used only for on-device cosmetic rendering is not, on its face, biometric data (the GDPR and UAE definitions need processing that allows unique identification). However, stored hand images or hand-geometry measurements linked to a named customer are a grey zone. Inferred skin tone can be read as revealing ethnic origin (see CJEU C-184/20). Comments on nail condition would be health data.
Design rules that follow: all analysis on-device and ephemeral; no ethnicity labels (use a cosmetic depth plus undertone scale); no automatic age inference (use self-declared age plus guardian consent); no nail-health remarks; images saved only by opt-in thumbnail with a TTL. The EU AI Act's Recital 16 explicitly treats virtual try-on filters as an "ancillary feature", not a biometric categorisation system. The UAE PDPL (Decree-Law 45/2021) and KSA PDPL (in force 14 Sep 2023, enforced from 14 Sep 2024, fines up to SAR 5M) follow the same pattern. GDPR applies only if the site targets or monitors people in the EU.

| Service | Free limit | Gotcha |
|---|---|---|
| Cloud Firestore: storage | 1 GiB stored data (documents, metadata and index entries all count). The free quota covers only one database per project, the '(default)' database. If Firestore 'editions' apply, the free tier is on Standard edition [VERIFY]. | Index entries count toward storage. Exempt big fields (image Bytes, long text) from single-field indexing. The database location is chosen once and cannot be changed. Closest options to Muscat: me-central1 (Doha); me-central2 (Dammam) [VERIFY Firestore availability]. Data stored there is still a cross-border transfer from Oman. |
| Cloud Firestore: operations | 50,000 document reads/day, 20,000 writes/day, 20,000 deletes/day, 10 GiB outbound network transfer/month. Quotas reset at midnight US Pacific time, about 11:00 (PDT) or 12:00 (PST) Muscat time. | On Spark, an exhausted quota makes that operation type fail (resource-exhausted) for the rest of the Pacific day: a hard outage, not a bill. A query with zero results still costs at least 1 read. A listener's first snapshot costs 1 read per document. A listener offline for more than 30 minutes is re-billed as a brand-new query. count() aggregations cost about 1 read per 1,000 index entries. Reads served from the local cache are free. Real-time listeners on large catalogs burn quota quickly, so paginate, cache and use getDocsFromCache. |
| Cloud Firestore: document and request limits | Maximum document size 1 MiB (1,048,576 bytes, field names and document name included). Maximum field value about 1 MiB minus 89 bytes. Maximum 40,000 index entries per document. About 1 sustained write/second per document. Batches/transactions: historically 500 writes; docs now cite a 10 MiB request-size limit [VERIFY]. | Store any image as a Firestore Bytes field (Bytes.fromUint8Array), not a base64 string; base64 adds 33%. Put each image in its own document in a subcollection so list queries never download image data. Don't write one hot 'counter' document on every try-on; use distributed counters, or skip analytics. |
| Cloud Firestore: web offline persistence | Free on all plans. Modern API: initializeFirestore(app, { localCache: persistentLocalCache({ tabManager: persistentMultipleTabManager() }) }). enableIndexedDbPersistence() is @deprecated; this was confirmed in the installed firebase 12.19.0 typings. | Stored in IndexedDB. Safari deletes script-writable storage for sites the user hasn't interacted with for 7 days (ITP); home-screen web apps are said to be exempt [VERIFY]. Private mode may disable it. On a SHARED salon tablet the cache holds customer data: use memoryLocalCache in kiosk mode, or call terminate() then clearIndexedDbPersistence() on logout. Offline writes queue and sync later, but security rules are evaluated only at sync time. |
| Firestore TTL policies | TTL deletes documents automatically after a timestamp field passes (typically within about 24 h of expiry). Each TTL deletion counts as a delete operation. | Use it for retention, e.g. lookbook thumbnails expiring after 90 days and consent-session documents. Check that TTL can be configured on a Spark project and that its deletes fit inside the 20k/day delete quota [VERIFY]. |
| Firebase Authentication: email/password, Google, anonymous, other social providers | Free with no MAU cap on legacy Firebase Auth. If the project is upgraded to 'Firebase Authentication with Identity Platform': Spark is capped at about 3,000 daily active users; Blaze gives about 50,000 MAU free, then per-MAU pricing [VERIFY current numbers]. | Don't upgrade to Identity Platform unless you need MFA, SAML/OIDC or blocking functions. Spark has daily caps on auth emails (verification, password reset, email-link sign-in) [VERIFY numbers on the Auth limits page]. Auth user records (email, name) are stored by Google, generally in the US, regardless of the Firestore region: a cross-border transfer to disclose. signInWithPopup fails under COOP: same-origin. signInWithRedirect needs the 'redirect best practices' setup: on Firebase Hosting with a custom domain, set authDomain to your own domain, because of third-party storage partitioning in Safari, Chrome and Firefox. |
| Firebase Authentication: phone/SMS | Not free. On Blaze, each SMS is billed at a rate that depends on the destination country (Gulf rates are believed to be among the higher tiers [VERIFY Oman +968 rate]). On Spark, SMS auth is unavailable or limited to a token daily quota (about 10/day has been cited) [VERIFY]. | SMS pumping/toll fraud is a real risk; if you ever enable it, set an SMS region policy allowing only +968 and the GCC codes. Recommendation: no SMS auth. Customers need no login (or use anonymous auth); staff use email/password or Google. Collect WhatsApp numbers only as booking data with consent, not as a login method. |
| Firebase Hosting | Spark: 10 GB storage and 360 MB/day data transfer (about 10.8 GB/month). Free custom domain(s) with automatic SSL. Several sites per project. Global CDN with automatic gzip/Brotli for compressible types. Blaze: same free amounts, then about $0.026/GB storage and $0.15/GB transfer [VERIFY]. | CRITICAL for this app. Measured locally: hand_landmarker.task 7.82 MB (5.82 MB gzipped; probably served uncompressed as octet-stream) and vision_wasm_internal.wasm 11.15 MB (3.28 MB gzipped). A first visit therefore moves about 10–20 MB, so 360 MB/day covers only about 20–35 NEW devices a day before the site is capped until the daily reset (behaviour on overrun: site disabled or quota-error until reset [VERIFY]). The service worker CacheFirst rule only helps returning visitors. Mitigations: load the wasm from cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/@mediapipe/tasks-vision@0.10.35/wasm and the model from storage.googleapis.com/mediapipe-models (both free and CORS-enabled [VERIFY CORP headers if COEP is ever enabled]), or put static assets on Cloudflare Pages (no bandwidth cap). Old Hosting releases count toward the 10 GB, so set release retention to keep about 5 versions. |
| Firebase Hosting: custom headers (COOP/COEP, CSP, cache) | Free; set in firebase.json under hosting.headers[] with glob 'source' (or 'regex') and key/value pairs. | Example: { "source": "/tryon/**", "headers": [ {"key":"Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy","value":"same-origin"}, {"key":"Cross-Origin-Embedder-Policy","value":"require-corp"} ] }. Headers apply per document load. A client-side SPA route change does not re-apply them, so an isolated route must be reached by a full page load or a separate HTML entry. Also add Permissions-Policy: camera=(self), a CSP, Referrer-Policy and long immutable Cache-Control for hashed assets. |
| Cloud Storage for Firebase | Spark: NONE as of today. Since 30 Oct 2024, any new default bucket (named PROJECT_ID.firebasestorage.app) needs Blaze. Older PROJECT_ID.appspot.com buckets kept the legacy no-cost limits (5 GB stored, 1 GB/day download, 20k uploads/day, 50k downloads/day) only until the Blaze deadline: first 30 Oct 2025, then extended to early February 2026 (2 or 3 Feb 2026 [VERIFY exact day]). After that, Spark projects lost read/write access to their buckets; Google said the data is not deleted immediately but stays unreachable until the project upgrades [VERIFY]. On Blaze, new buckets get the Google Cloud 'Always Free' tier only in us-central1, us-east1 or us-west1: 5 GB-month storage, 5,000 Class A ops, 50,000 Class B ops, 100 GB egress/month. Upgraded appspot buckets are said to keep the legacy free limits [VERIFY]. | Don't design around Firebase Storage while on Spark. If you go Blaze later: the Always Free tier needs a US region (a cross-border transfer from Oman); a Gulf region (me-central1) is billed from the first byte, though only cents at salon scale. Blaze needs a credit card. Budget alerts do NOT cap spend; automatic shut-off needs a Pub/Sub budget notification that disables billing. |
| Cloud Functions for Firebase | Blaze only (not available on Spark). Blaze free tier: about 2M invocations/month, 400,000 GB-seconds, 200,000 CPU-seconds and 5 GB outbound for 1st gen; 2nd gen uses the Cloud Run free tier (about 2M requests, 180k vCPU-seconds, 360k GiB-seconds per month) [VERIFY]. | Deploying also uses Cloud Build and Artifact Registry. Container images can cost cents a month; set the cleanup policy the CLI offers. Workarounds without Functions: set staff custom claims from a one-off local Admin SDK script, or keep a staff-allowlist document checked in security rules; generate PDFs and CSVs client-side (jsPDF and papaparse are already in the project). |
| Firebase App Check (web) | App Check itself is free. Web providers: ReCaptchaV3Provider, ReCaptchaEnterpriseProvider, CustomProvider (confirmed in the installed SDK typings). reCAPTCHA has moved to Google Cloud pricing: a free tier of about 10,000 assessments/month, then paid tiers; classic v3 keys were being migrated into Cloud projects through 2025 [VERIFY]. | Tokens are cached (default TTL 1 h, configurable from 30 min to 7 days), so assessments roughly equal token refreshes, which is fine for one salon. Enforce App Check on Firestore to stop scripted quota-draining, the main Spark outage risk. reCAPTCHA runs inside a cross-origin iframe, which may break under COEP require-corp. Use a debug token for localhost. App Check is not a substitute for security rules. |
| Realtime Database (fallback option) | Spark: 1 GB stored, 10 GB/month downloaded, 100 simultaneous connections. | Only worth it for live 'mirror' sync, e.g. the customer's phone showing what the salon tablet renders. The 100-connection cap and 1 GB limit make it unsuitable for images. |
| Firebase Cloud Messaging (web push) | Free on all plans. | iOS supports web push only for sites added to the Home Screen (iOS 16.4+), and only after a user gesture grants permission. Useful for appointment reminders; needs a service worker (vite-plugin-pwa is already in the project). |
| Firebase AI Logic (Gemini) — cloud AI, NOT recommended for this product | Usable from Spark through the Gemini Developer API free tier, which is rate-limited [VERIFY current limits and Oman availability]. | It sends hand images to Google's cloud: a cross-border transfer of possibly sensitive data. On the unpaid tier, the Gemini API terms allow Google to use prompts and images to improve its products. This conflicts with the on-device, privacy-first requirement. If used at all: only for text (e.g. writing trend descriptions), never for customer photos. |
| Google Analytics for Firebase / Performance Monitoring / Remote Config | Free. | Analytics sets identifiers and sends data to Google in the US. Under Oman's PDPL that needs consent and a disclosure, and GDPR/ePrivacy apply if EU users are targeted. Recommendation: turn Analytics off for the try-on surface; keep only anonymous aggregate counters, or nothing. Remote Config is harmless (feature flags, trend palettes). |
| Firebase Data Processing and Security Terms | Free; accepted in the console under Project settings > Privacy (Spark included). | Accept the data processing terms and record Google as a processor in your privacy notice and processing records (Oman ER). Also list Auth (US), Firestore region, Hosting (global CDN) and any CDN that serves the model files. |

**Photo storage decision:** DECISION: store no customer photos by default; process everything on the device; never upload raw camera frames. In detail:
TIER 0 (launch default, all users)
- Camera frames and photos stay in memory only: video element, WebGL textures and canvases.
- Leaving the try-on screen does all of the following: stops the MediaStream tracks, revokes object URLs, clears the canvases and resets kiosk state.
- The customer keeps a result by downloading it or using the Web Share API (navigator.share with files, e.g. to WhatsApp). The image is rendered and re-encoded locally, which also strips EXIF/GPS.
- Firestore stores only a 'look recipe': polish IDs, finish, shape, length, date, optional staff ID and optional booking reference. No image, no skin-tone values, no landmark or hand-geometry data.
TIER 1 (optional opt-in lookbook or before/after; salon mode only)
- Needs a separate, unticked consent checkbox in Arabic and English. Record the consent version and timestamp.
- Crop to the hand/nails (never faces) and downscale to at most 480–640 px on the long side.
- Re-encode as WebP or JPEG at about q0.7, roughly 25–70 KB. Canvas re-encoding strips EXIF.
- Store as a Firestore Bytes field (not base64), one image per document, in a subcollection such as salons/{salonId}/lookbookImages/{id}.
- Exempt the image field from indexing. Add an expiresAt timestamp with a TTL policy (e.g. 90 days). Offer a 'delete my photos' path.
- Capacity on the 1 GiB / 10 GiB-egress free tier: about 15k–30k thumbnails stored and about 150k–300k thumbnail views a month. Security rules allow staff only, and App Check is enforced.
TIER 2 (salon-owned marketing content: product swatches, nail-art gallery, trend boards; contains no customer personal data)
- Bundle as static assets in the Hosting build.
- Or, if the owner wants uploads without redeploying, use Cloudinary's free plan (about 25 monthly credits; 1 credit ≈ 1 GB storage, 1 GB bandwidth or 1k transformations [VERIFY]). Only for non-personal images, because Cloudinary is a US processor.
REJECTED
- Cloud Storage for Firebase: Blaze required since 30 Oct 2024 for new buckets and since about Feb 2026 for any access.
- Supabase free Storage: 1 GB storage, about 5 GB egress, and the project is paused after 7 days without activity. It adds another vendor and region to disclose, and the pausing breaks a low-traffic salon app.
- The salon's Google Drive: needs OAuth and app-verification work, data sits in a personal account mixed with other files, there is no per-customer access control, and a mis-shared folder is a breach risk. Acceptable only as a manual offline backup or export by the owner.
LATER UPGRADE PATH (if the owner accepts Blaze)
- A Cloud Storage bucket with Storage rules and App Check, budget alerts plus a billing kill-switch, and a TTL lifecycle rule.
- Choose a US Always-Free region (free, but a transfer outside the GCC) or me-central1 (costs cents at this volume) after a legal check on cross-border transfer under the Oman ER.
- Even then, keep Tier 0 as the default.

### Camera & browsers

- **All platforms: baseline requirements** — navigator.mediaDevices.getUserMedia needs a secure context: HTTPS, or localhost for development. In an iframe it also needs allow="camera". If either is missing, navigator.mediaDevices is undefined. · - Use one canonical HTTPS origin in every QR code; apex vs www, or web.app vs a custom domain, count as different origins, so users get new permission prompts and separate storage.
- Ask for the camera only after a clear user tap with bilingual pre-permission text. A first denial is hard to undo on iOS.
- Handle NotAllowedError, NotFoundError, NotReadableError (camera busy in another app), OverconstrainedError (relax constraints) and SecurityError separately.
- Read device labels only after permission is granted.
- **iOS / iPadOS Safari (iOS 17, 18, 26)** — getUserMedia supported since iOS 11. Rear camera via facingMode 'environment'. 1280x720 or 1920x1080 at 30 fps is reliable. Higher resolutions are device-dependent. · - The video element needs playsinline, muted and autoplay, or iOS takes it fullscreen.
- Permission: default 'Ask'. Expect re-prompts after reload, relaunch or origin change. Users can set aA > Website Settings > Camera > Allow for the domain to stop prompts.
- Only one active capture per page/app. A new getUserMedia call, or another app or tab using the camera, mutes or ends the existing tracks.
- Tracks end when the page goes to the background or the device locks. Re-acquire on visibilitychange (this may prompt again).
- Phones with several rear lenses expose separate devices ('Back Camera', 'Back Ultra Wide Camera', 'Back Dual/Triple Camera'). The virtual multi-camera device may auto-switch to macro, but the main lens's minimum focus distance (about 12–20 cm on Pro models) blurs close-up nails, so let users pick a lens and hold the hand about 20–25 cm away.
- Low Power Mode may cap frame rate and requestAnimationFrame at 30 fps.
- Memory: the tab can be killed or the WebGL context lost on older iPhones. Handle webglcontextlost.
- iOS 26 requirements: iPhone 11 / SE (2nd generation) or newer [VERIFY].
- **iOS Home-Screen web app (standalone PWA)** — getUserMedia works in standalone mode since iOS 13.4. iOS 26 reportedly opens every 'Add to Home Screen' site as a web app by default [VERIFY]. · - Long-standing complaint: the camera permission prompt comes back on every launch, and sometimes after full-page navigations. Reports through iOS 17–18; iOS 26 behaviour not verified. Mitigation: keep the camera in one SPA view with no full reloads, keep the stream alive while in use, and don't stop/restart tracks between captures.
- The home-screen app has its own storage and cookie jar, separate from Safari. A login in Safari does not carry over, and permissions set in Safari may not apply [VERIFY].
- No address bar or share menu, so give an in-app 'share link' and 'reload' button.
- Web push only works in this mode (iOS 16.4+).
- **Chrome / Edge / Firefox / Brave on iOS** — All use WebKit (WKWebView). getUserMedia is available since iOS 14.3. Capabilities match Safari on the same iOS version, including WebGPU on iOS 26. · - Permission prompts come from the host app, e.g. 'Chrome would like to access the camera' first, then the site prompt.
- Torch, zoom and other constraints are limited exactly as in Safari.
- A user-agent check for 'CriOS', 'FxiOS' or 'EdgiOS' identifies them; no special handling is needed.
- **Android Chrome (and installed PWA / WebAPK)** — Full getUserMedia support. A granted permission is remembered per origin (newer versions also offer 'Allow this time' [VERIFY]). The installed PWA shares Chrome's site permissions. Image Capture constraints are available where the device's camera hardware layer (Camera2) exposes them. · - facingMode 'environment' sometimes opens an ultra-wide, depth or monochrome camera on some Samsung, Xiaomi and Huawei models. After permission, enumerate devices and pick the best rear camera by label and capabilities, then remember the choice per device.
- Some devices cap the stream resolution or deliver odd aspect ratios.
- Thermal throttling during long WebGL sessions. Cap processing to about 15–30 fps and pause when idle.
- Use track.getCapabilities() and getSettings(); never assume support.
- **Samsung Internet (Chromium-based)** — getUserMedia supported. The Chromium base lags Chrome by a few versions. Image Capture constraints (torch, zoom) usually behave like Chrome on the same device [VERIFY on current Galaxy models]. · - Its own permission UI and 'Smart anti-tracking' can affect third-party CDNs.
- WebGPU status unclear: detect navigator.gpu at runtime.
- Test on Galaxy A-series mid-range phones, which are common in the GCC.
- **Firefox for Android** — getUserMedia supported. · No torch, zoom, focus, white-balance or exposure constraints, and no ImageCapture. WebGPU not shipped on Android as of my knowledge [VERIFY]. Expect a small user share; basic flow only.
- **Huawei Browser / Android devices without Google services** — Chromium-based; getUserMedia supported [VERIFY]. · - Huawei has meaningful share in the GCC.
- Google sign-in may fail without Google Play Services if a native flow is triggered, so offer email login for staff.
- Test WebGL2 performance on Kirin GPUs.
- **Instagram & Facebook / Messenger in-app browsers (iOS WKWebView, Android WebView)** — UNRELIABLE. getUserMedia may be missing, fail with NotAllowedError or never prompt, depending on app version and OS. On Android the host app must grant WebView permission requests and hold the CAMERA permission. On iOS the host app must allow media capture. · Detect:
- User-agent tokens /Instagram|FBAN|FBAV|FB_IAB|FBIOS|Messenger/, plus the Android WebView marker '; wv)'.
- Capability check: !navigator.mediaDevices?.getUserMedia.
- A NotAllowedError arriving within about 300 ms with no prompt shown.
Bounce:
- Android: link to intent://HOST/PATH#Intent;scheme=https;package=com.android.chrome;S.browser_fallback_url=ENCODED_URL;end (some app versions block intents).
- iOS: no reliable programmatic exit. Show an overlay with an arrow to the ••• / share menu: 'Open in Safari / فتح في المتصفح'. Add a 'Copy link' button. Optionally try the x-safari-https:// scheme (reported to work on recent iOS [VERIFY]) or googlechrome:// when Chrome is installed.
Always offer 'Take/Upload photo' via <input type="file" accept="image/*" capture="environment">, which usually still works in these webviews and opens the native camera.
Privacy: Meta's in-app browser can inject scripts; avoid entering credentials there.
- **WhatsApp (link taps and in-chat QR scan)** — Usually OK: links generally open in the system browser, SFSafariViewController (iOS) or Chrome Custom Tabs (Android), all of which support getUserMedia [VERIFY on current WhatsApp versions]. · - Custom Tabs and SFSafariViewController may keep cookies and storage separate from the main browser (iOS SFSafariViewController is isolated), so a login may not persist.
- Keep the customer flow login-free.
- The 'WhatsApp' user-agent token is the link-preview crawler, not a browser; serve it good Open Graph tags in Arabic and English.
- **Snapchat in-app browser (incl. Snapcode/QR scan inside Snapchat)** — Treat as UNSUPPORTED for live camera: a WebView/WKWebView where camera access is generally blocked or unreliable [VERIFY]. · Detect /Snapchat/ in the user agent; show the bounce overlay plus the photo-upload fallback. Snapchat is very popular in Oman, KSA and the GCC, so print 'Scan with your phone camera' on QR cards.
- **TikTok in-app browser** — Treat as UNSUPPORTED for live camera [VERIFY]. · Detect /musical_ly|Bytedance|BytedanceWebview|TikTok|trill/ in the user agent. Security researchers (Felix Krause, 2022) showed TikTok's in-app browser injecting JavaScript that can observe input events. Bounce, and never collect data inside it.
- **X/Twitter, Telegram, LinkedIn, Gmail, LINE, WeChat** — X and Telegram usually use SFSafariViewController or Custom Tabs, where the camera generally works. LinkedIn (LinkedInApp), WeChat (MicroMessenger) and LINE (Line/) use their own webviews, where it is unreliable. · LINE supports the ?openExternalBrowser=1 query parameter to force the system browser. Keep a single 'unsupported webview' detector list and log only aggregate counts (no IDs).
- **Native QR scanners (iOS Camera app, Control Center scanner, Android camera, Google Lens)** — Best path: they open the default browser (Safari or Chrome) with full camera support. · - QR cards in Arabic and English should say 'Open with your phone camera, not Instagram or Snapchat'.
- Encode a short HTTPS URL with a salon parameter, e.g. https://domain/t?s=muscat1. No personal data in the URL.
- Test print size and contrast under salon lighting.
- **Constraint: torch (flashlight)** — Android Chrome and Samsung Internet: yes on most phones' rear camera, via track.applyConstraints({advanced:[{torch:true}]}). iOS Safari: newer releases reportedly added torch [VERIFY version; possibly Safari 17.4–18.x]. Desktop: no. · - Feature-detect with 'torch' in track.getCapabilities().
- The torch is a harsh point light that creates hot specular spots on gel/glossy nails and shifts skin color.
- For color accuracy, prefer even ambient salon light (a daylight LED panel, about 5000–6500 K, CRI 90+) over the torch. Use the torch only for dim rooms.
- **Constraint: zoom** — Android Chrome 87+ (asking for {video:{zoom:true}} triggers a pan/tilt/zoom permission) and Samsung Internet. iOS Safari: reportedly supported from Safari 17.x [VERIFY]. Firefox: no. · Zoom lets users stay at the focus distance and frame the nails tightly, which is better than moving closer. Range and step vary; read capabilities.zoom.min, max and step. Digital zoom reduces effective resolution.
- **Constraint: focusMode / focusDistance / pointsOfInterest** — Android Chrome: focusMode 'continuous', 'single-shot' or 'manual'; focusDistance and pointsOfInterest on many devices, depending on the camera hardware layer. iOS Safari: not exposed or very limited as of my knowledge [VERIFY]; iOS runs continuous autofocus by itself. · Tap-to-focus via pointsOfInterest at the hand centroid works on many Android phones. On iOS rely on the user's distance guidance (an on-screen 'move back' cue driven by landmark spread or blur score). Measure sharpness per frame (variance of the Laplacian on the nail regions) and only analyse sharp frames.
- **Constraint: whiteBalanceMode / colorTemperature** — Android Chrome: whiteBalanceMode 'manual', 'single-shot' or 'continuous', and colorTemperature, on many devices. iOS Safari: not exposed as of my knowledge [VERIFY]. · - Automatic white balance shifts skin and polish colors from frame to frame and from phone to phone.
- For the '100% look', normalise colors in software: an in-frame reference such as a white/grey card or a printed salon calibration card with a few known patches. Estimate a 3x3 color-correction matrix on the device. Then lock white balance ('manual' at the current colorTemperature) where supported.
- Without a card, fall back to grey-world or white-patch estimation and warn about lighting.
- **Constraint: exposureMode / exposureCompensation / exposureTime / iso (exposure lock)** — Android Chrome: on many devices (exposureMode 'continuous', 'manual' or 'single-shot'; exposureCompensation; exposureTime; iso). iOS Safari: not exposed as of my knowledge [VERIFY]. · Lock exposure after the meter settles on the hand to stop brightness flicker breaking skin-tone estimates. On iOS, average skin color over several frames and reject frames with clipped highlights. Check for a stable luminance histogram before running recommendations.
- **Still capture: ImageCapture.takePhoto() vs canvas grab vs file input** — ImageCapture: Chromium (Android Chrome, Samsung Internet) only. Safari: not supported as of my knowledge [VERIFY]. Canvas drawImage(video) works everywhere. <input capture> works almost everywhere, in-app webviews included. · - A canvas grab is limited to stream resolution (often 1080p).
- takePhoto can return full sensor resolution on Android.
- A file-input photo is full resolution but processed by the phone's camera pipeline (HDR, Smart HDR, tone mapping, skin smoothing on some phones), which changes colors. Correct it with the reference card.
- Strip EXIF (GPS) by re-encoding on a canvas before any save or share. Respect the EXIF orientation via createImageBitmap(file, {imageOrientation:'from-image'}).
- **Permissions API & camera indicators** — navigator.permissions.query({name:'camera'}): Chrome/Android yes; Safari 16+ reportedly yes [VERIFY]; Firefox partial. · Use it only to choose the UI state: pre-prompt, or 'blocked, here's how to re-enable' with bilingual, per-browser screenshots. The OS camera-in-use indicators (iOS green dot, Android privacy indicator) are expected; give a visible 'Stop camera' button and stop automatically after about 60–120 s idle.

### Privacy law

- **Oman — status and scope** — Personal Data Protection Law, Royal Decree 6/2022: issued 9 Feb 2022, published 13 Feb 2022 (Official Gazette no. 1429 [VERIFY]), in force 13 Feb 2023. Executive Regulation: Ministerial Decision 34/2024 from the Ministry of Transport, Communications and Information Technology (MTCIT), issued about Feb 2024, with roughly a one-year grace period to about Feb 2025 [VERIFY exact dates]. Regulator: MTCIT. It covers processing of personal data of natural persons, including electronic processing. Exclusions include purely personal or family use by an individual and certain government security processing. → Fully in force today for a Muscat salon. The salon is the controller for in-salon use; you, if you run the platform for the salon, are a processor, or a joint controller if you decide purposes yourself. Google/Firebase is a sub-processor. Write a bilingual (Arabic/English) privacy notice and processing agreement.
- **Oman — consent and lawful processing** — Processing needs the data subject's explicit consent, which must be clear, specific, documented and provable by the controller, unless a statutory exception applies (e.g. performing a contract with the data subject, a legal obligation, vital interests, public-interest or official purposes) [VERIFY the full exception list and whether a legitimate-interest ground exists]. The data subject may withdraw consent at any time. Before processing, the controller must tell the data subject in writing who the controller is, the purposes, the data categories, recipients, rights, any transfer abroad and the security measures. → Show a short bilingual notice and an 'Agree and start camera' step before getUserMedia, in both customer self-mode and salon-staff mode. In salon mode, staff confirm the customer agreed (a staff tap logged without the image). Keep consent versions and timestamps. Withdrawal must stop processing and delete stored looks or thumbnails.
- **Oman — sensitive data and Ministry permit** — Art. 5 (as I recall it) prohibits processing genetic data, biometric data, health data, and data on ethnic origin, sexual life, political or religious opinions or beliefs, criminal convictions or security measures, except under a permit from the Ministry following the Executive Regulation's procedure (application, conditions, fee) [VERIFY article number, list and permit procedure]. → Design so you never knowingly process these categories:
- Keep hand analysis on-device and ephemeral.
- Don't store hand images or hand-geometry measurements linked to identity unless the customer opts in, and get Omani legal advice on whether a permit is needed for stored hand photos.
- Never label or store ethnicity or race.
- Never comment on nail or skin health (fungus, psoriasis, discolouration).
- If the business later wants a stored 'hand profile' with skin-tone metrics, ask counsel about a permit before building it.
- **Oman — children** — A child's personal data may be processed only with the guardian's consent and in the child's best interest; the ER sets the conditions [VERIFY]. The Omani Child Law (Royal Decree 22/2014) defines a child as anyone under 18 [VERIFY]. → Self-mode: an age confirmation ('I am 18+, or my parent/guardian agrees'). Salon mode: staff tick 'guardian present and consents' for minors. Do NOT infer age from the hand: it is unreliable, intrusive and adds risk. Ask for a self-declared age bracket only if recommendations need it, and make it optional.
- **Oman — data subject rights** — Rights include: withdraw consent; request correction, update or erasure; know what is processed and why; get a copy; data portability; be notified of breaches; object to direct marketing. The ER sets response procedures and deadlines [VERIFY deadline, possibly 45 days]. → Build a 'My data' page: export as JSON or PDF, delete looks and thumbnails, delete account. Give a salon WhatsApp/email contact for requests, and record the date each request is handled.
- **Oman — controller duties (DPO, records, security, registration)** — Controllers must adopt policies and technical and organisational security measures, keep processing records, use processors under written agreement, and (per the ER) appoint a personal data protection officer and possibly register or notify the Ministry [VERIFY whether registration or a DPO is mandatory for small controllers]. → Prepare a 1–2 page record of processing and a simple risk assessment (DPIA-lite). Name a responsible person at the salon. Accept the Firebase data processing terms. Log only aggregate counts. Keep an Arabic version of every document.
- **Oman — breach notification** — The controller must notify the Ministry, and the data subject where the breach may cause harm. The ER sets the timing: commonly cited as within 72 hours of becoming aware [VERIFY]. → Keep a bilingual breach-response template and a contact list. With 'store nothing' there is little to breach; the main assets are Firestore (bookings, look recipes, opt-in thumbnails) and staff accounts. Enforce security rules, App Check and staff 2-step verification on the Google account.
- **Oman — cross-border transfer** — Personal data may be transferred outside Oman only under the controls set by the Executive Regulation (adequate protection, no prejudice to national security or Oman's interests, contractual safeguards and/or consent) [VERIFY exact conditions]. → Firebase has no Omani region (no hyperscaler region in Oman as of my knowledge [VERIFY]):
- Firestore in me-central1 (Doha) or me-central2 (Dammam) is still a transfer.
- Firebase Auth user records and the Hosting CDN are global/US.
So minimise: no accounts for customers; no images by default. Disclose Google as processor and the locations in the notice, and capture consent for the transfer on any opt-in storage.
- **Oman — direct marketing** — Sending marketing or advertising needs the data subject's prior consent and an easy opt-out [VERIFY article]. The Telecommunications Regulatory Authority also has rules on bulk/spam SMS. → A separate, unticked checkbox for WhatsApp/SMS promotions, never bundled with try-on consent. Put an opt-out in every message.
- **Oman — penalties and related laws** — Administrative fines from about OMR 500 up to about OMR 500,000 depending on the violation, with higher bands for sensitive-data and transfer violations [VERIFY bands]. Related laws: Cyber Crime Law, Royal Decree 12/2011 (using IT to violate privacy, e.g. capturing or publishing images of people without consent, is a crime); Consumer Protection Law, Royal Decree 66/2014 (misleading claims). → Never photograph customers without consent. Lookbook images need explicit, separate permission to publish. Don't advertise '100% accurate': say 'realistic preview; actual result may vary with lighting, screen and application' (Arabic and English).
- **Oman — is a hand photo biometric data?** — The PDPL lists biometric data as sensitive. Most comparable definitions (GDPR Art. 4(14), UAE PDPL, KSA implementing regulation) cover data from specific technical processing of physical characteristics that allows or confirms UNIQUE IDENTIFICATION (face, fingerprints). Hand geometry, dorsal hand-vein patterns and fingerprints are recognised biometric modalities. → A conservative reading in three parts:
(1) A live camera frame processed on-device for cosmetic rendering (21 landmarks, nail masks), with no template stored and no identification, is very unlikely to be 'biometric data processing'.
(2) Hand images or landmark/finger-proportion measurements stored server-side and linked to a named customer are a grey zone; treat them as potentially sensitive. Avoid them, or get legal advice and possibly a permit.
(3) Capture only the back of the hand (nails up), never the palm, and keep resolution modest so fingerprints are not captured.
- **Oman — is inferred skin tone or undertone sensitive?** — Ethnic origin is a sensitive category. Skin tone is not ethnicity, but it can act as a proxy. The EU's top court, in CJEU C-184/20 (OT, 1 Aug 2022), held that data INDIRECTLY revealing special-category information is covered; GCC regulators may take a similarly broad view. → - Keep skin analysis on-device and transient.
- Describe skin only in cosmetic terms: depth (e.g. the 10 Monk Skin Tone shades or light/medium/tan/deep) plus undertone (cool/neutral/warm/olive).
- Never output or store 'ethnicity' or 'race', and never use them in recommendations.
- Avoid the Fitzpatrick scale; it is a dermatological phototype, arguably health data.
- Let the user override the detected shade manually; this is also good for accuracy.
- Don't send skin metrics to analytics.
- **UAE (GCC expansion)** — Federal Decree-Law 45/2021 on Personal Data Protection, in force 2 Jan 2022. Its executive regulations and the UAE Data Office's enforcement start were still pending as of my knowledge [VERIFY]. It doesn't apply in financial free zones with their own laws (DIFC Data Protection Law no. 5 of 2020; ADGM Data Protection Regulations 2021). Sensitive data includes family or ethnic origin, political, philosophical or religious beliefs, criminal records, biometric data (GDPR-style: technical processing allowing unique identification, e.g. facial images and fingerprints), health, genetic and sex life. Consent or other legal bases; a DPIA for high-risk or large-scale sensitive processing; a DPO in some cases; breach notification to the Data Office; transfers to adequate countries or with safeguards. → The same on-device, no-ethnicity design works. Check the emirate or free-zone location of each salon (DIFC/ADGM rules differ). Arabic and English notices.
- **Saudi Arabia (GCC expansion)** — PDPL, Royal Decree M/19 (2021), amended by M/148 (2023). In force 14 Sep 2023; enforcement after a one-year grace period, from 14 Sep 2024. Regulator: SDAIA, with Implementing Regulations and a Regulation on Personal Data Transfer outside the Kingdom (2023, amended 2024). Sensitive data includes racial or ethnic origin, religious, intellectual or political belief, security and criminal data, biometric or genetic data used for identification, health data, credit data, and data showing a parent is unknown. Legal guardian consent for minors or persons lacking capacity. Breach notification to SDAIA within 72 hours, and to data subjects without undue delay where harmful. Controllers may need to register on SDAIA's National Data Governance Platform. DPO required in specified cases. Fines up to SAR 5M (doubled for repeat offences); up to 2 years' prison for disclosing sensitive data with intent to harm. → Before a KSA launch: Arabic-first notices; documented consent; a transfer assessment (Firestore in me-central2 Dammam would keep data in the Kingdom [VERIFY Firestore availability there]); check whether SDAIA registration applies.
- **Other GCC (brief)** — Qatar: Law 13/2016 (PDPPL); data of a 'special nature' (ethnic origin, children, health, religion, and others) needs a permit from the regulator, a permit model similar to Oman's. Bahrain: Law 30/2018 (PDPL), with sensitive data including racial/ethnic origin and health. Kuwait: CITRA's Data Privacy Protection Regulation (2021, updated 2024 [VERIFY]). → Every GCC regime treats ethnicity and health as sensitive, and most treat identification-grade biometrics as sensitive too. The same 'on-device, cosmetic-only, opt-in storage' architecture carries over across the GCC.
- **EU — GDPR (EU visitors)** — Territorial scope (Art. 3(2)): GDPR applies to a non-EU business only if it offers goods or services to people in the EU or monitors their behaviour in the EU. A Muscat salon serving people physically in Oman, EU tourists included, is generally out of scope; a site targeting EU users, or tracking them with analytics, could be in scope. Biometric data (Art. 4(14) and Recital 51): photos count only when processed with specific technical means for unique identification. Special categories (Art. 9) include data 'revealing racial or ethnic origin', so explicit consent would be needed. Children: Art. 8, consent age 13–16 depending on member state. DPIA (Art. 35), breach notification within 72 h (Art. 33), transfers (Arts. 44–49), and an EU representative (Art. 27) if in scope. → Don't target the EU (no EU pricing, ads or EU-language marketing). Use no tracking analytics. The privacy-first design largely meets GDPR anyway.
- **EU — AI Act (Regulation 2024/1689), if ever offered in the EU** — Art. 5(1)(g) (applies from 2 Feb 2025) bans biometric categorisation that infers race, political opinions, sex life and similar from biometric data. Recital 16 says features 'purely ancillary' to a commercial service, explicitly including filters that let consumers preview a product on themselves (virtual try-on), are NOT biometric categorisation systems. Art. 50 transparency duties for AI-manipulated content apply from 2 Aug 2026 (the proposed 'digital omnibus' may change timelines [VERIFY]). → A virtual nail try-on is the textbook ancillary use. Keep it that way: no race or ethnicity inference and no age or sex classification. Label renders as 'AI preview / معاينة افتراضية'.

### Privacy by design

- All hand, nail and skin analysis runs ON-DEVICE in the browser (MediaPipe WASM/WebGL2). No camera frame, photo, landmark array or skin metric ever goes to a server or a cloud AI API.
- Ephemeral by default: frames live only in the video element, WebGL textures and canvases. On exit, timeout or 'New customer', stop all MediaStream tracks, revoke object URLs, zero the canvases and reset React state.
- Kiosk/salon mode: a 'New customer' button plus an automatic reset after about 2 minutes idle. Use memoryLocalCache for Firestore on shared tablets, or clear IndexedDB persistence on staff logout. Never write customer images to localStorage, IndexedDB or the service-worker cache.
- Service worker caches only static app files and model/runtime files; no runtime caching of user content, blob: URLs or data: URLs.
- Store 'look recipes', not photos: polish IDs, finish, shape, length and timestamp. No image, skin-tone value, undertone, hand measurement or landmark data in Firestore.
- Opt-in thumbnail storage only, behind a separate unticked consent. Crop to the hand/nails and never faces; downscale to at most 640 px; re-encode on a canvas to strip EXIF/GPS; store as Firestore Bytes in a separate document, exempt from indexing; set a TTL (e.g. 90 days); delete on request.
- Share and save on the device: Download or the Web Share API to WhatsApp. The image is generated locally, with no server copy and no share link that exposes data.
- Cosmetic language only: skin depth on a cosmetic scale (e.g. Monk Skin Tone 1–10, or light/medium/tan/deep) plus undertone (cool/neutral/warm/olive). Never ethnicity, race or nationality labels; never the Fitzpatrick phototype.
- No health claims: never detect, label or comment on nail diseases, fungus, discolouration, brittleness or skin conditions, which would be health data. If the nail looks damaged, show only a neutral 'consult your technician' message, or nothing.
- No automatic age inference from hands or faces. Ask for an optional self-declared age bracket only if recommendations truly need it. Use an age or guardian confirmation for minors (Oman child = under 18).
- Explainable recommendations: show WHY a shade is suggested (e.g. 'warm undertone → gold-based nudes'). Let the user override the detected tone manually. Avoid any 'best for your race' style logic.
- Accuracy honesty: label every render as an 'AI preview — معاينة افتراضية' with a disclaimer that lighting, screens and application affect the real result. No '100% accurate' marketing claims (consumer protection law).
- Calibration without data capture: an in-frame white/grey or salon calibration card corrects color on the device; the corrected frame is never stored.
- Bilingual layered notice (Arabic RTL and English) BEFORE the camera starts: who the controller is (the salon), what happens on the device, what is stored if anything, retention, recipients (Google Firebase as processor), locations, rights and contact.
- Separate consents: (1) camera try-on, (2) optional thumbnail storage or lookbook use, (3) marketing via WhatsApp/SMS. Each is unticked, individually withdrawable, and recorded with version and timestamp, without any image.
- No accounts for customers by default (no login needed to try on). Staff log in with email/password or Google; no SMS auth (cost and fraud). If customer accounts are added later, use anonymous auth upgraded only with consent.
- Least-privilege Firestore security rules: deny by default; customers read and write only their own documents; staff role via custom claims set from a local Admin SDK script, or a staff-allowlist document; validate document shape and size in rules (e.g. reject image fields over 150 KB).
- Enforce Firebase App Check on Firestore (reCAPTCHA Enterprise or v3) to stop quota-draining abuse, a real outage risk on Spark.
- Data location: choose the Firestore region once and deliberately (me-central1 Doha, or me-central2 Dammam if available). Document that Auth and Hosting are global/US. Accept the Firebase data processing terms in the console.
- No third-party trackers on the try-on surface: no Facebook/TikTok/Snap pixels and no Google Analytics, or only consent-gated, cookieless aggregate counts. Self-host fonts and assets where bandwidth allows.
- Security headers on Firebase Hosting: a strict Content-Security-Policy, Permissions-Policy: camera=(self), microphone=(), geolocation=(); Referrer-Policy: strict-origin-when-cross-origin; X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff; frame-ancestors 'self'. HSTS on the custom domain.
- Camera transparency: a visible camera-on indicator in the UI, a big 'Stop camera' button, automatic stop after about 60–120 s idle, and no background capture.
- In-app browser safety: detect Instagram, Facebook, Snapchat and TikTok webviews, show an 'Open in browser' bounce, and offer the photo-upload fallback. Never ask for credentials inside in-app browsers.
- QR codes carry only https://domain/t?s=SALON (no personal data or tokens) and point to one canonical origin.
- Logging hygiene: no personal data or images in console logs, error reports or analytics events; strip URLs of query data before logging.
- Retention schedule written down: look recipes 12 months (or until deletion); opt-in thumbnails 90 days; consent records for as long as the data is kept plus a short legal buffer; staff accounts while employed.
- Data subject request flow: an in-app 'My data' page (export JSON/PDF, delete) plus a salon WhatsApp/email contact. Track response dates against the Oman ER deadline.
- Breach playbook: a bilingual template, the MTCIT contact, a 72-hour clock [VERIFY], and an evidence log. Rotate staff passwords and require 2-step verification on the salon's Google/Firebase owner account.
- Record of processing and a DPIA-lite (1–2 pages) kept by the salon. Get Omani legal review before ANY stored hand images or skin-tone profiles (possible sensitive-data permit).
- Model and licence hygiene: keep a list of every model and asset with its licence (MediaPipe Apache-2.0, Monk Skin Tone scale CC-BY-4.0 [VERIFY]). Never train on customer photos without separate explicit consent, which should be the default 'no'.
- Device hygiene for salon tablets: screen lock; capture inside the app rather than the native camera so photos don't land in the camera roll or iCloud/Google Photos; Guided Access (iOS) or app pinning (Android) for kiosk use.
- Minimise third-party CDN exposure, or disclose it: if model and wasm files come from jsDelivr or Google storage to save Hosting quota, mention that those providers see the visitor's IP address, or self-host them on Cloudflare Pages.

Re-verify the trend claims in a follow-up run with web access.
